### Leaderboard (/leaderboard)

**Purpose & flow.** Public all-time ranking of testnet traders by trade count, volume, and realized PnL, plus three platform-total stat cards — the product's competitive/social-proof surface. Data is computed off-chain by a per-minute Vercel cron BFS-scanning Horizon events into Turso; the page polls `/api/leaderboard` every 60s. Viewing is one click; finding your own rank, sharing, and recovering from a failed load are all unsupported.

**Strengths**

- Every rendered number is real data from the chain-event pipeline — no hardcoded display values (`web/app/api/leaderboard/route.ts:11-20`).
- The sync pipeline is engineered for correctness-over-time: definitive-vs-transient fetch discrimination so trades are never silently dropped (`sync-leaderboard/route.ts:140-171`) and a persistent known_traders set so closed-out wallets stay ranked (`web/lib/db.ts:43-46`).
- Skeletons for both desktop table and mobile cards match final layout (`LeaderboardContent.tsx:20-30,141-142,186-191`); a dedicated mobile card layout replaces the table; addresses deep-link to stellar.expert (`:159-166`).
- PnL is not color-only (signed prefix + green/red); numerics are font-mono right-aligned; formatting pinned en-US — none of the chart's locale leakage (`:11-17`; `format.ts:6-13`).
- Freshness ambition is right: per-minute cron, 60s client refresh, sane cache headers (`web/vercel.json`; `route.ts:22-26`).

| Severity | Issue | Evidence | Fix direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| Critical | **The "PnL" ranking silently excludes all liquidation losses** — the cron parses `position_liquidated` with pnl=0 (the event carries only keeper_reward) and never parses `cross_liq` (the one liq event that DOES carry total_pnl). A trader who banks small wins and gets liquidated on the big loss ranks as a net-positive "top trader"; and PnL is the DEFAULT sort. Poison for a surface whose job is honest comparison — and for any copy-trading discovery built on it. | `sync-leaderboard/route.ts:114,130-133,223-237`; `contracts/market/src/lib.rs:526-529,981-984`; `LeaderboardContent.tsx:35` | Parse `cross_liq` into trades rows; for isolated liqs record the realized loss (add pnl to the event on the next redeploy, or derive as −collateral until then); backfill via seed rescan; label the column "Realized PnL" with an inclusion tooltip. |
| High | **Backend failure renders as "No traders yet"** — DB errors return `[]` (500), the fetch wrapper converts any non-OK to `[]`, the component has no error state: an outage shows a confident empty board with zeroed stat cards. The stale-indexer episode proves this failure mode is real. No retry affordance; the empty-state CTA isn't even a link. | `route.ts:27-30`; `web/lib/stellar/leaderboard.ts:10`; `LeaderboardContent.tsx:42-46,143-148` | Throw on !res.ok; add an error state with Retry; keep last-good data with a stale banner when a background poll fails; make the CTA a real `<Link href="/trade">`. |
| High | **No self-row: the connected wallet's rank is invisible** — the component never reads walletStore; users must visually diff 8-char truncations. "Where am I?" is the #1 leaderboard question and the retention mechanic every reference product leads with. | `LeaderboardContent.tsx:1-58`; `leaderboard/page.tsx:22-27`; `format.ts:81-84` | Highlight the matching row in brand gold with "(you)" and pin a sticky "Your rank: #N" summary (with a "not ranked yet" variant). All client-side. |
| High | **Ranking metrics are undefined and contradict the protocol's own volume definition** — no tooltip/legend anywhere; in reality Trades = opens only, Volume = open-side notional only, PnL = realized price-PnL gross of funding/fees. The fee-tier system counts volume on open AND close, so a $20K round trip shows ~$40K toward tiers and ~$20K here — an unexplained 2× discrepancy in one product. Traders with big unrealized gains show a deceptive green +$0.00. | `sync-leaderboard/route.ts:227-235` vs `contracts/market/src/lib.rs:329,817,2048,2005,2052-2055`; `LeaderboardContent.tsx:132-137` | Align volume with the fee-tier definition (both sides); count closes in Trades; add column-header tooltips; rename "Realized PnL". |
| Medium | **No data-freshness indicator despite a pipeline that has silently gone stale before** — `traders.last_updated` exists but is never selected or shown; the cron runs only on the prod Vercel project; the silent 60s poll implies liveness the pipeline can't guarantee. | `db.ts:33`; `route.ts:12`; `LeaderboardContent.tsx:50-52` | Return MAX(last_updated); render "Updated Xm ago" (formatRelativeTime exists, `format.ts:89`), amber past ~10 minutes. |
| Medium | **All-time only, and the data model blocks timeframes retroactively** — `trades.timestamp` defaults to insert time (the cron omits it; tx created_at is parsed then discarded), so 24h/7d/30d boards can't be built from this data even after tabs ship; early farmers sit on top forever. | `db.ts:25`; `sync-leaderboard/route.ts:391,314`; no timeframe control in component | Persist tx created_at as the row timestamp NOW (already fetched); add 7d/30d/All tabs later with WHERE cutoffs. |
| Medium | **Aggregates mix trades from retired market deployments** — no market column; the contract filter applies only to newly scanned txs, so post-redeploy/reset the board ranks wallets on volume from a market that no longer exists, unreconcilable via the explorer links. | `db.ts:19-27`; `sync-leaderboard/route.ts:108,223-237` | Stamp a market_id at insert; scope aggregates to the current market; decide and label per-deployment vs lifetime. |
| Medium | **Rank renumbers on sort switch, and the chips promise a direction toggle that doesn't exist** — "#" is just idx+1 under the current sort; both chips render ArrowUpDown but clicking the active chip does nothing (desc only); default sort (PnL) isn't the first-listed chip. | `LeaderboardContent.tsx:55-58,110-124,150-156,35` | Stable rank against one declared primary metric ("Ranked by PnL"), or real column-header sorting with asc/desc; drop ArrowUpDown from single-direction chips. |
| Medium | **Synthetic backfill rows can permanently double-count volume/trades** — `open_position_<addr>` seed rows are never reconciled when the real event later inserts under `${hash}#${positionId}`; both count forever. | `sync-leaderboard/route.ts:368-382,337,391`; `db.ts:26` | Delete a trader's synthetic rows when a real position_opened inserts, or key synthetics by position id so the real event replaces them. |
| Medium | **Small-text contrast failures and missing table/sort semantics** — headers/labels at white/40 (~3.8:1) fail AA at 12px; empty state white/30 (~2.6:1); no `scope=col`, no accessible name on "#", no `aria-pressed` on sort chips, no `aria-busy` while loading. | `LeaderboardContent.tsx:132-137,73,84,95,145,111-124,141-148` | Lift labels to white/60+ and the empty state to white/50; add scope, sr-only "Rank", aria-pressed, aria-busy. |
| Low | **Zero PnL renders as a green "+$0.00"**, dressing open-only traders (and, given the pnl bug, all liquidation-only wallets) as winners; a young board becomes a wall of green zeros that reads fake. | `LeaderboardContent.tsx:11-17` | Render exactly-zero as neutral unsigned "$0.00"; reserve green/red for nonzero realized PnL. |
| Low | **API returns every trader ever with no LIMIT and runs CREATE TABLE DDL on every read**; the client re-sorts a fresh array copy per render. | `route.ts:8,12`; `LeaderboardContent.tsx:55-58` | Migrate schema at cron time only; cap at top ~100 + total count; memoize the sort. |
| Low | **No share hook on the one surface built for bragging** — PnlShareCard/Modal exist but the leaderboard offers no "share my rank"; a missed viral loop for a beta trying to grow. | `web/components/share/` exists; no affordance in LeaderboardContent | Once the self-row ships, add a share button reusing the PnL card with rank + volume, in brand gold. |

### Faucet (/faucet) + API Keys (/api-keys)

**Purpose & flow.** The faucet is onboarding step zero: activate a fresh testnet wallet (Friendbot), add the USDC trustline, claim up to 1,000 test USDC/day — a well-sequenced wizard needing exactly one wallet signature end-to-end. API Keys is the developer on-ramp: sign a zero-cost wallet challenge to mint gateway credentials (closed beta) and manage/revoke them; after any refresh, key management becomes write-only (see issues).

**Strengths**

- The faucet wizard is genuinely well built: account-exists → Friendbot → trustline → claim, each stage gated and reflected, with a `trustlineLocked` ref preventing refetch flicker while Horizon propagates (`useFaucet.ts:46-47,103-126,193-200`); the server re-validates everything client-side state claims (`api/faucet/claim/route.ts:43-82`).
- Complete daily-limit UX: progress bar, remaining counter, per-second UTC-reset countdown, auto-disabled over-limit amount cards (`ClaimSection.tsx:33-101`; `faucet.ts:184-250`); claim history is real Horizon payment data with stellar.expert links (`ClaimHistory.tsx:86-110`).
- The known not_in_beta 403 trap is handled proactively (dedicated amber card with wallet address, what still works, and a 24h-allowlist escalation path — `ApiKeyIssueCard.tsx:124-167`) and reactively (humanized 403/401 toasts, `:17-25,57-61`).
- Challenge signing is a SEP-10-style sequence-0 placeholder tx, never submitted, documented in code (`web/lib/api/sign.ts:1-16,36-62`); honest security microcopy ("only keeps a SHA-256 hash", `ApiKeyIssueCard.tsx:89-92`); disclosed rate-limit tiers match the real gateway config (`api-keys/page.tsx:103-119` vs `api/src/services/rateLimit.ts:11-13`).
- Every mutation has a disabled+spinner pending state — double-submit structurally impossible.

| Severity | Issue | Evidence | Fix direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| High | **Broke users are never routed to the faucet from the trading surface** — a 0-USDC user on /trade gets "Insufficient USDC balance" with no link/hint; repo-wide grep finds zero faucet references in trade, portfolio, vault, or wallet components; the only pointers are the nav's last item and non-linked landing prose. | `OrderPanel.tsx:226-227,772-774`; grep; `FinalSection.tsx:9`; `Header.tsx:17` | Link the error ("Get test USDC from the faucet →") and show an inline callout in OrderPanel when usdcBalance === 0 (and XLM guidance when xlmBalance < 1). Cheap, high-conversion. |
| High | **One-time API secret has no copy affordance and a destructive one-click Dismiss** — "copy it now… it cannot be retrieved again", yet the 64-char hex must be drag-selected (near-impossible on mobile) and Dismiss instantly unmounts the card with no confirmation. Highest-stakes moment, least protection. | `ApiKeyIssueCard.tsx:89-117,115-117,182-184`; `sessionAuthStore.ts:11-17` | Copy buttons for keyId, secret, and a ready `Authorization: Bearer keyId:secret` string; "I have stored the secret" confirm before Dismiss; optional .env download. |
| High | **Key management is write-only after a refresh** — the list authenticates only from the non-persisted session store; there is no way to paste an existing keyId:secret, so revoking a leaked/lost key requires minting yet another live credential, inverting the advertised security model. | `ApiKeysList.tsx:44-53`; `sessionAuthStore.ts:11-23`; `web/lib/api/keys.ts:100-110`; `api/src/routes/keys.ts:183-189` | "Sign in with an existing key" input, or better: allow list/revoke via a fresh wallet-challenge signature so lost-secret users can still revoke. |
| Medium | **Published replay-protection spec is wrong: 60s shown, 30s enforced** — the hero pill and step 4 present X-Timestamp as required on every call, while the gateway rejects >30s skew and actually treats the header as optional. Spec drift on a dev surface produces intermittent 401s the docs say should succeed. | `api-keys/page.tsx:43,82-93` vs `api/src/plugins/auth.ts:22,48-56` | Fix the pill (or raise server tolerance); reword step 4 as "recommended"; derive numbers from a shared constant so they cannot drift. |
| Medium | **The developer journey dead-ends after issuance** — the page's only links are Twitter and Discord; no `/docs` Swagger link, no curl example, no `pip install noether-sdk`. "What can I call now?" is unanswered. | grep of hrefs in api-keys surface; `api-keys/page.tsx:54-96`; gateway serves OpenAPI at /docs | "Start calling the API" block: {API_BASE}/docs, one copyable curl using the fresh key, SDK install commands. |
| Medium | **`localhost:4000` fallback can leak into production UI and requests** — every gateway wrapper defaults API_BASE to localhost when `NEXT_PUBLIC_NOETHER_API_URL` is unset; the beta check silently fails, issuance errors raw, and the success card literally instructs "Use against http://localhost:4000". Duplicated across five wrapper files. | `web/lib/api/keys.ts:6`; `ApiKeyIssueCard.tsx:15,110`; duplicated in `markets.ts:7`, `positions.ts:7`, `referral.ts:8`, `vaults.ts:8` | Centralize API_BASE; in production throw or render a "gateway not configured" banner; keep the fallback for NODE_ENV=development only. |
| Medium | **Faucet claims race on the shared admin account with no sequence retry** — concurrent claimers (demos, workshops) produce tx_bad_seq for one, surfaced as the generic "Failed to process claim." | `api/faucet/claim/route.ts:85-105,139-142`; CLAUDE.md SEC-3 | Retry once on tx_bad_seq server-side; message "The faucet is busy — try again in a few seconds"; longer term, a dedicated hot key. |
| Medium | **Beta gate can be raced**: while the allowlist check is in flight, `gated` defaults false and the Issue button is enabled — a non-allowlisted user who clicks during a cold-start window walks the full signature ceremony into the 403 the pre-check exists to prevent (the challenge endpoint doesn't enforce the allowlist). | `ApiKeyIssueCard.tsx:52-53,198`; `api/src/routes/keys.ts:100-127` vs `:168-175` | Treat beta === null as "Checking access…" (disabled); re-check just before requesting the challenge. |
| Medium | **Fixed 3-column grids break down on phones** — the faucet How-It-Works strip and the API Keys hero pills hardcode `grid-cols-3`; at 375px cards become ~105px slivers. The rest of the surface is responsive, so these read as oversights. | `faucet/HowItWorks.tsx:11`; `api-keys/page.tsx:40` vs `:103`; `ApiKeysList.tsx:98` | `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-3` (or a compact horizontal stepper for faucet steps). |
| Low | **Step numbering referenced but never established** — "Step 2: Claim USDC" / "Complete Step 1" with no card labeled Step 1; the strip uses First/Then/Done. | `ClaimSection.tsx:48,53-55`; `TrustlineSection.tsx:34`; `HowItWorks.tsx:5-7` | Title the trustline card "Step 1: USDC Trustline"; align the strip's labels. |
| Low | **A11y: selection is color-only, progress/statuses silent** — amount cards lack `aria-pressed` (border + 2px gold dot only); the limit bar is a bare div; labels lack htmlFor; revoke uses native `confirm()`; zero ARIA on the surface beyond the header hamburger. | `AmountCard.tsx:19-38`; `ClaimSection.tsx:67-77`; `ApiKeyIssueCard.tsx:94,186`; `ApiKeysList.tsx:75` | aria-pressed, role=progressbar + values, htmlFor/id, aria-live status region, dialog primitive instead of confirm(). |
| Low | **Microcopy drift**: "Connect your Freighter wallet" (LOBSTR/WalletConnect fully supported — a LOBSTR user may bounce); "tokens sent instantly" vs ~5s ledgers; disabled over-limit amounts give no reason. | `faucet/page.tsx:52-54`; `HowItWorks.tsx:7`; `faucet.ts:184-191` + `AmountCard.tsx:22-25` | "Connect your Stellar wallet (Freighter, LOBSTR, …)"; "in seconds"; tooltip on disabled amounts ("Exceeds today's remaining 500 USDC"). |

### Global chrome & information architecture

**Purpose & flow.** The persistent shell: header nav, wallet session lifecycle (connect/restore/sign/disconnect with live balances), global toasts, the feedback FAB, the referral banner, and the legal/geo-compliance wrapper (terms, /restricted, metadata). Connect is 2 clicks + extension approval; copy-address, switch, and disconnect all work from the connected chip; geo-restricted users are edge-rewritten to an honest /restricted page.

**Strengths**

- Wallet signing safety is pro-grade: before every sign the active wallet account is re-resolved and refused with an actionable message if it differs from the session address — preventing silent txBadAuth (`walletKit.ts:188-217`).
- Session restore never fires unsolicited popups: extension wallets restore instantly from localStorage, WalletConnect via persisted sessions with a readiness retry (`WalletProvider.tsx:94-117`; `walletKit.ts:109-164`); WC signing gets a guiding toast with a 2-minute timeout and the app modal hides under the WC QR modal (`WalletModal.tsx:52-55`; `useWallet.ts:91-115`).
- Ops guardrail in chrome: a fixed banner + console error when NOETHER_ROUTER is unset in a production build, preventing a silently degraded stale-price path (`providers.tsx:9-39`).
- Geo-gating is honest and precise: middleware guards only trading surfaces, /restricted names the exact jurisdictions and links the Terms, noindex set (`middleware.ts:30-59`; `restricted/page.tsx:4-27`). The 404 page is a model empty state that hides floating chrome and respects reduced motion (`not-found.tsx:17-35`).
- ~40 contract error codes translate to trader-relevant plain English (`contractErrors.ts:5-55`); a global prefers-reduced-motion kill switch exists (`globals.css:148-160`).

| Severity | Issue | Evidence | Fix direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| High | **Terms of Service is unreachable from the product** — the only working inbound link to the real /terms page is on /restricted; app pages have no footer at all, and the geo-block regime legally leans on a document users "agree to by accessing" but cannot find. (The landing's dead `#` links are itemized in the Landing section.) | grep: only `restricted/page.tsx:23` links /terms; `terms/page.tsx:10-107`; no app footer anywhere | Minimal one-line legal footer on app pages (Terms · Docs · Discord · © Noether); ship or drop Privacy. |
| High | **Protocol LP vault (/vault) is orphaned from primary navigation and confusable with /vaults** — the header has "Vaults" (marketplace) but no entry for the LP pool holding user funds; in-app it's reachable only via a promo card on /vaults; the singular/plural gives no scent. Reported independently by three auditors. | `Header.tsx:10-18`; `vaults/page.tsx:115-121`; `LandingFooter.tsx:86-94` | Add an "Earn" nav item for /vault, or make /vaults the umbrella with clearly-labeled "Protocol Vault (LP)" / "Leader Vaults" tabs; rename one of the two regardless. |
| Medium | **The feedback widget reports false success and destroys the message** — `window.open('mailto:…')` then an unconditional "Email client opened!" toast, field reset, and modal close; with no mail client or a popup block, the tester's bug report is silently gone. The highest-value signal a testnet collects. | `FeedbackButton.tsx:40-47` | POST to an API route or Discord webhook (the gateway exists); keep mailto as a secondary link; never clear state on an unverifiable action. |
| Medium | **No wrong-network or unfunded-account guidance — first-session dead end**: "Stellar Testnet" in the dropdown is a hardcoded string, not a check; a mainnet-configured wallet connects "successfully" into silent zero balances (Horizon 404 swallowed to 0) with a red "Low" tag and no CTA; nothing links the zero-balance state to /faucet or friendbot. | `ConnectButton.tsx:137,176-179`; `WalletProvider.tsx:36-40`; `walletKit.ts:188-217` (account check only); grep: zero network-mismatch handling | On connect, detect testnet-404 accounts ("Fund via Friendbot / check your wallet network"); make Low/zero rows link to /faucet; translate network-mismatch sign errors. |
| Medium | **Toasts never surface the tx hash or an explorer link** — the hash is returned by `submitTransaction` but every toast ends at text-only success/failure; on a chain-native DEX, "View tx" is the strongest trust primitive available and it is never offered. (Raw-JSON error toasts are covered in the Trade table.) | `client.ts:104-134`; `OrderPanel.tsx:259-276`; toast config `layout.tsx:56-78` | A standard tx-toast helper: pending ("Confirming on Stellar… ~5s") → success with stellar.expert/tx/<hash> link; route all errors through decodeContractError. |
| Medium | **Brand assets are 240KB raster-in-SVG fakes; Safari gets a blank favicon** — favicon.svg and the wordmark SVG are each a base64 1024px PNG wrapped in `<svg>`; the favicon is the only icon declared (no .ico/PNG/apple-touch-icon), and the stretched raster renders soft — undercutting the precision-mathematics identity. | `web/public/` (240,139B + 241,498B); `layout.tsx:22-24`; `NoetherLogo.tsx:12-18`; `LandingFooter.tsx:44-51` | True-vector wordmark (few KB), favicon.ico + 32/180/512 PNGs declared in metadata.icons. |
| Low | **Wallet dropdown labels free USDC as "Collateral" and silently zeroes NOE** — "Collateral" conventionally means posted margin; and WalletProvider's restore/refresh paths always write `setBalances(xlm, usdc, 0)`, stomping the NOE LP balance that useWallet fetches correctly — two refresh paths disagreeing about one field. | `ConnectButton.tsx:169-171`; `WalletProvider.tsx:85,107,142`; `useWallet.ts:59-64` | Rename to "Available"; fetch NOE in WalletProvider or preserve the existing store value. |
| Low | **Two different Discord invites shipped** — the 404 page and the landing footer use different URLs; at most one is canonical, and referral components hardcode a third duplicated copy. | `not-found.tsx:4` vs `LandingFooter.tsx:59`; `ReferralSignIn.tsx:187`, `CreateCodeCard.tsx:136` | One DISCORD_URL constant in packages/shared or lib/utils/constants, imported everywhere. |

### Cross-cutting: mobile & responsiveness

**Purpose & scope.** What every surface becomes at ~390px: breakpoint coverage, touch behavior, table strategies, fixed-element choreography, and whether any page is effectively desktop-only. The verdict: the mobile architecture has the right bones (trade bar, card layouts, bottom-sheet wallet modal) but one shared-Modal defect blocks the core money flow, and fixed-chrome collisions hit exactly the referred first-time visitor.

**Strengths**

- MobileTradeBar is the right pattern for a perp DEX: fixed Long/Short bar below lg with safe-area insets, direction preset into the shared store, and an h-20 spacer (`MobileTradeBar.tsx:36-54`).
- Card-based mobile variants replace tables where it matters most: positions (`PositionsList.tsx:248-277,908-1008`), orders (`OrdersList.tsx:357+`), leaderboard (`LeaderboardContent.tsx:185-227`).
- WalletModal is a true bottom sheet: `items-end`, `max-h-[85vh]`, internal scroll (`WalletModal.tsx:114-117`) — the pattern the shared Modal needs.
- App Header mobile menu done properly: backdrop, body scroll lock, auto-close on route change, 48px rows (`Header.tsx:34-49,96-141`); the chart keeps vertical page scroll on touch (`TradingChart.tsx:61-63`); toasts capped at 90vw, dropdown width clamped, timeframe strip overflow-x-auto; money inputs use `inputMode=decimal` (`OrderPanel.tsx:757-763`).

| Severity | Issue | Evidence | Fix direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| Critical | **Mobile order entry clips its own submit button**: the shared Modal is a centered `fixed inset-0` dialog with body scroll locked and NO max-height or internal scroll (grep confirms none). The only way to trade below lg puts the full ~1000–1100px OrderPanel inside it; on an 844px phone the dialog overflows both ends of a scroll-locked viewport, making the Buy/Long CTA (and on smaller phones the collateral input, and the modal's own close button) unreachable — the core money flow dead-ends after the form is filled. The same root cause clips the ~650–800px Set Stop-Loss/Take-Profit modals — risk-management actions — on small phones. | `web/components/ui/Modal.tsx:34,85,92-100,120`; `MobileTradeBar.tsx:17-23,56-68`; `OrderPanel.tsx:567,1099-1136,1138-1217,1231-1253`; `PositionsList.tsx:331-523,526-738` | Give ui/Modal the WalletModal treatment: `items-end sm:items-center`, `max-h-[85dvh]`, fixed header + `overflow-y-auto` content, rounded-t on mobile, sticky primary action. One change fixes trade sheet, SL/TP, deposit/withdraw, create-vault, and feedback modals at once. |
| High | **Landing hijacks all touch scrolling; overflowing content is permanently unreachable on phones** — every touchmove preventDefaulted, one section per completed swipe with 1–1.2s lockout, sections 100dvh + overflow:hidden; FlagshipSection's mobile stack extends past one viewport and is clipped forever; NOEIntro's 3 phases make early swipes appear to do nothing. First-touch impression: swiping feels broken, content missing. | `SlideContainer.tsx:128-142,156-159`; `landing.css:23-37`; `FlagshipSection.tsx:112-114,173-199`; `NOEIntroSection.tsx:22` | On `pointer: coarse`, skip interception entirely — native scroll + CSS snap proximity, sections allowed to grow; keep phase choreography desktop-only (mobile layouts already render phase content statically). |

---

### Cross-cutting: accessibility & microcopy

**Purpose & scope.** How usable the product is for keyboard, screen-reader, low-vision, and international users, and the quality of all user-facing copy, number/date formatting, and error surfaces. The core brand palette is actually accessibility-friendly — gold `#eab308` on `#0a0a0a` is 10.3:1, muted-foreground labels 7.8:1, nav inactive white/60 7.3:1 — the failures live in dimmed tiers, direction-colored CTAs, missing semantics, and locale drift. A keyboard-only user can technically complete a trade, but through unlabeled money inputs, an invisible focus ring, and modals that neither trap nor restore focus.

**Strengths**

- `prefers-reduced-motion` honored globally with a kill-all rule (`globals.css:148-160`) — rare in trading UIs.
- ~40 contract error codes map to actionable plain English, and `toast.promise` gives per-phase latency feedback tuned to 5s ledgers on every tx (`contractErrors.ts:5-55`; `OrderPanel.tsx:401-445`); consistent double-submit protection on every money action.
- PnL and direction are never color-only: signs, LONG/SHORT text badges, an AlertTriangle on liq risk (`PositionsList.tsx:790-800,829,845`).
- Native elements as the base (range slider, checkbox, select, real buttons — `OrderPanel.tsx:813-821,975-981,1046-1057`) keep keyboard operability mostly free; `lang="en"` set, a real `<main>` on /trade, Escape closes every modal (`layout.tsx:49`; `trade/page.tsx:704`; `Modal.tsx:53-67`).
- In-house precedents exist for the two worst problems: MobileTradeBar's black-on-emerald/red CTAs (9.2:1, `MobileTradeBar.tsx:36-49`) and ReferralBanner's `role="dialog"` + aria-label + honest microcopy (`ReferralBanner.tsx:97-146`).
- `format.ts` pins en-US for all money helpers with magnitude-based price decimals (`format.ts:6-46`); designed empty states everywhere; the honest APY-gating pattern ("Shown once fee revenue is tracked", `StatsBar.tsx:46-53`) is the model for every not-yet-real number.

| Severity | Issue | Evidence | Fix direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| High | **Primary Long/Short CTAs fail WCAG contrast** — "Buy / Long BTC" is 16px bold white on `#22c55e` = 2.28:1 (below even the 3:1 large-text floor); Short on `#ef4444` = 3.76:1; the toggle uses the same fills. The fix already ships in-house: MobileTradeBar renders the same actions black-on-fill at 9.2:1. Latent landmine: `--primary-foreground` is white on the gold `--primary` (1.98:1) — currently unpaired, but one `bg-primary text-primary-foreground` away from shipping illegible. | `OrderPanel.tsx:727,739,1238-1241`; `MobileTradeBar.tsx:40,46`; `globals.css:12-13` | Black text on the existing green/red fills (zero brand change); set `--primary-foreground` to near-black so the token can never pair white on gold. |
| High | **Pinch-zoom disabled globally while money data is set in 8–10px type** — `userScalable:false` + `maximumScale:1` on every page (WCAG 1.4.4 failure; Android enforces it), compounded by 85 uses of ≤10px text across trading surfaces (21 in OrdersList, 17 in PositionsList, 9 in OrderPanel, an 8px "CROSS" badge) — collateral, liq prices, and margin modes users are now forbidden from enlarging. Reported independently by four auditors. | `layout.tsx:10-15`; `PositionsList.tsx:798,807,819,847`; micro-font grep counts | Delete `userScalable`/`maximumScale` (inputs are already ≥14px, so the iOS auto-zoom motivation barely applies); raise the trading micro-text floor to 11–12px. |
| High | **No modal in the app has dialog semantics, focus trapping, or focus restore** — every money confirmation (close, SL/TP, vault deposit/withdraw, wallet connect, share) runs through Modal.tsx or WalletModal.tsx: no `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal`/`aria-labelledby`, Tab walks into the obscured page, no initial focus or restore, icon-only unlabeled X buttons; WalletModal nests an Install `<a>` inside a disabled `<button>` (invalid HTML). Escape also closes mid-signing (busy only disables buttons), so a keyboard user can lose a pending wallet flow invisibly. | `Modal.tsx:77-127,108-115,52-66`; `WalletModal.tsx:95-129,123-128,185-220`; consumers `PositionsList.tsx:280-328`, `MobileTradeBar.tsx:56`, DepositWithdrawModal | Fix once in the two shared components: dialog role + labelling, a small focus trap (or native `<dialog>`), focus restore, labeled close, ignore Escape while busy; move the Install link outside the button. |
| High | **Money form controls have no programmatic labels; errors are not wired to inputs** — the shared Input renders its label without htmlFor/id and errors without aria-describedby/aria-invalid; the collateral field announces only "0.00"; leverage range, trigger/stop/limit/TP/SL inputs, Slider, and the copy-address button all lack accessible names. Totals across ~120 component files: 15 aria-* attributes, zero `aria-expanded`, zero sr-only text. Confusing SL with TP in identical unlabeled textboxes places a wrong real-money order. | `ui/Input.tsx:17-21,52-54`; `OrderPanel.tsx:751-768,813-821,862-869,1006-1027,1111-1129`; `ui/Slider.tsx:75-105`; `ConnectButton.tsx:151-165`; grep totals | Add id/htmlFor + aria-describedby/aria-invalid inside ui/Input so every consumer inherits; explicit aria-labels on the OrderPanel's raw inputs ("Collateral in USDC", "Leverage: {n}x"); name all icon-only buttons. |
| High | **Locale leakage: Turkish month names on the chart, mixed number/date locales on the same row** — `createChart` gets no `localization` option so the axis renders in the browser locale ("05 Nis '26" — the screenshot bug); eight files use browser-locale `toLocaleString()`/`toLocaleDateString()` while format.ts pins en-US, so a TR/DE user sees "$12.500" (dot-grouped) in Size next to "$12,500.00" in PnL on one position row, plus "07.04.2026" next to "Jul 4" dates. Also hit on referrals, api-keys, faucet amounts, and vault detail (server-locale "Created" date inviting hydration mismatch). | `TradingChart.tsx:29-64`; same-row mix `PositionsList.tsx:805,814` vs `:845`; `[id]/page.tsx:84`; `ApiKeysList.tsx:12`; `ReferralActivity.tsx:16`; `VaultActivity.tsx:23`; `VaultTradeHistory.tsx:17`; `ReferralStats.tsx:45`; pinned helpers `format.ts:7,42,106`; also `OrdersList.tsx:225`, `OrderBook.tsx:233`, `RecentTrades.tsx:300`, `ClaimHistory.tsx:50,95,98`, `ClaimSection.tsx:64,80`, `PnlChart.tsx:22-34` | `localization: { locale: 'en-US' }` (+ dateFormat) in createChart; sweep every browser-locale call site onto format.ts helpers; lint-ban bare `toLocaleString()`. |
| High | **Raw machine errors can reach user toasts** — `client.ts` throws `JSON.stringify(response.errorResult, null, 2)` on submission errors and surfaces "Transaction failed: txFAILED" on-chain; decodeContractError passes anything non-`Error(Contract, #N)` through verbatim and untruncated, while DepositWithdrawModal's separate humanizer caps at 200 chars — error hygiene depends on which page you were on. A user whose trade fails stares at XDR JSON in a 90vw toast. | `client.ts:95,121,130`; `contractErrors.ts:63-78`; `DepositWithdrawModal.tsx:46-68`; consumed at `OrderPanel.tsx:412,443,498` | Map submission/on-chain failure classes to short human copy ("Transaction rejected by the network — your funds did not move. Try again."), log raw payloads to console, truncate unknown fallbacks (~140 chars) in one shared decoder. |
| High | **Keyboard navigation: sub-perceptible off-brand focus ring, no skip link, dropdowns without Escape/aria-expanded, tabs without tab semantics** — the global indicator is a 1px violet outline at 50% alpha (1.97:1 vs the 3:1 requirement; the only violet in a gold brand, and `::selection` is violet too), targeting only button/a; no skip link (9 header tab stops before content); asset selector, wallet menu, and hamburger expose no aria-expanded and ignore Escape; Tabs are plain buttons with no tablist/tab/aria-selected/arrow keys. | `globals.css:117-121,102-105`; grep 'skip' = none; `AssetSelectorDropdown.tsx:88-123`; `ConnectButton.tsx:100-116,118-125`; `Header.tsx:96-104`; `ui/Tabs.tsx:33-59`; per-component ring drift `ui/Button.tsx:14-21`, `not-found.tsx:60` | 2px solid `#eab308` outline + 2px offset on `:focus-visible` for all interactive elements (on-brand, 10.3:1); gold-tint selection; skip-to-content link in layout; aria-expanded + Escape on the three dropdowns; real tablist semantics (or the available shadcn/radix primitives). |
| Medium | **~110 instances of secondary text below 4.5:1, including money-input placeholders at 2.5–2.7:1** — text-neutral-500 (4.18:1) ×46 including the Market Info labels; white/40 (3.77:1) ×26; muted-foreground/50 (2.71:1) ×20 + 14 placeholders (the "0.00" hint in every money input); placeholder-neutral-600 (2.53:1) ×10 including the shared Input. | `trade/page.tsx:661-684`; `OrderPanel.tsx:763`; `ui/Input.tsx:32`; grep counts vs #0a0a0a | Secondary-text floor at neutral-400/muted-foreground (7.8:1); placeholders at white/55–60; reserve /40–/50 tints for decorative strokes, never words or numerals. |
| Medium | **Positive PnL renders with a double sign: "++2.34%"** — formatPercent already prepends '+' and the positions table + mobile card prepend their own, on the most-watched number in the product. | `format.ts:51-54`; `PositionsList.tsx:848,945` | Remove the manual '+' at the two call sites. |
| Medium | **Tooltips don't work for anyone who can't hover** — ui/Tooltip is mouse-only (no focus/blur, no role="tooltip") and has no live consumer; the six dead Info icons (see Trade) plus unexplained "Liq. Price", "Margin Ratio", "Healthy", "Post Only", and a funding rate shown with no interval or payer direction leave jargon unexplained by any path on keyboard/touch. | `ui/Tooltip.tsx:36-42`; `trade/page.tsx:677-682`; `CrossMarginBanner.tsx:66-97` | Make Tooltip focusable and touch-triggerable (button trigger, role=tooltip, aria-describedby); label funding "+0.0100% / 1h" with a "longs pay shorts when positive" tooltip. |
| Medium | **Trade, portfolio, and vault pages have no h1/h2** — heading outlines start at h3 card titles ("Place Order"), so SR users navigating by headings get no page context; compounded by the missing skip link. | grep '<h1\|<h2' empty for the three pages; `OrderPanel.tsx:570`; `trade/page.tsx:658` | Visually-hidden h1 per page ("Trade BTC-PERP — Noether"); demote card titles consistently. Per-route `<title>` metadata while there (the doc title is one generic string on every route, `layout.tsx:19`). |
| Low | **Error toasts use polite live regions** — react-hot-toast defaults mean SR users may miss failures entirely; combined with unassociated Input error text, failure states are structurally quiet. | `layout.tsx:56-78`; `ui/Input.tsx:52-54` | Assertive region (or role=alert) for error toasts; aria-describedby error wiring in Input. |
| Low | **Microcopy inconsistencies, one touching money semantics**: mixed "…" vs "..." in one file; "Trail Stop" vs "Trailing Stop" for the same feature; Title Case vs sentence case drift; and the Stop-Limit panel statically labels Limit Price "Max entry price" — wrong for Shorts (minimum acceptable), contradicting the correct direction-aware paragraph below it. | `OrderPanel.tsx:267,283` vs `:369,402,426`; `:715` vs `:1040`; `:1016-1019` vs `:1029-1033` | One microcopy pass: standardize ellipsis, one name per order type, sentence case for controls; make the Limit Price hint direction-aware. |

---

### Data-integrity ledger

Noether is a trading product: every fake, stale, or wrong-by-construction number is a direct trust liability. This table consolidates every such finding from all 13 auditors. **Class key** — *Hardcoded*: a literal in source presented as data. *Fabricated on failure*: an error path that silently invents a value. *Wrong by construction*: real pipeline, wrong semantics. *Dead control*: an affordance asserting function it doesn't have.

| # | Surface | Finding | Class | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Landing | Marketing screenshot bakes in broken data: "-9519.75%" 24h change, Turkish "Şub" axis, deprecated "Fee (0.1%)", "Gas (XLM) 9,996.60", pre-T2 nav | Stale asset | `web/public/images/trading-screenshot.png`; `Hero.tsx:65`; `FlagshipSection.tsx:55` |
| 2 | Landing / chrome | Terms + Privacy links are `href="#"`; real /terms exists unlinked; no privacy page | Dead control | `LandingFooter.tsx:75-80`; `web/app/terms/page.tsx` |
| 3 | Landing | "Technical Architecture" docs link is a Google Drive folder | Stale asset | `LandingFooter.tsx:81` |
| 4 | Landing | Unmounted FinalSection email form fakes success without sending anywhere | Dead control (dead code) | `FinalSection.tsx:20-24` |
| 5 | Trade | Order Summary fee tier / 14d volume: calls non-existent `get_trader_volume`, silently returns 0, accumulates client-side, resets on refresh — Tier-3 traders shown "Base" at 2.5× their real fee | Fabricated on failure | `market.ts:1272-1288`; `contracts/market/src/lib.rs:639`; `OrderPanel.tsx:113-114,148-163,404,428` |
| 6 | Trade | Cross-mode Liq. Price computed from the isolated formula though the contract stores 0 (account-level liquidation) — not a real trigger | Wrong by construction | `OrderPanel.tsx:171-177,1166-1183` vs `contracts/market/src/lib.rs:344-345` |

| 9 | Trade / Portfolio | Liquidations produce no history row, toast, or record of any kind (`position_liquidated`/`cross_liq` never parsed) | Wrong by construction | `market.ts:350-405,445,541-554`; `contracts/market/src/lib.rs:526-529` |

Referral earnings stats are honestly zero with "from v1.1" labels rather than faked. The null-gated-APY pattern — suppress and explain, never invent — is the house standard every finding in this ledger should converge on.

## Competitive Benchmarking

### The state of perp-DEX UX in 2026

Perp-DEX UX has converged hard. The trade-page geography is now a single canonical grid inherited from Binance — ticker strip with funding countdown on top, chart center, book/trades beside it, order ticket as the right rail, positions tabbed along the bottom — and Hyperliquid's decision to copy it verbatim is why pro traders onboard there in minutes. Around that fixed grid, three capabilities became table stakes between 2024 and 2026: **sign-once session trading** (one wallet signature authorizes a browser-held agent key; every subsequent order is popup-free — Hyperliquid approveAgent, Vertex 1CT, dYdX derived keys, Paradex/Extended key derivation, Lighter API keys, GMX Express), **gasless execution** (the user never sees a chain fee), and **push-fed order lifecycle state** (orders echo into the UI instantly and reconcile over websocket; nobody polls, nobody refreshes).

The second theme of the period is that trust failures, not feature gaps, killed or maimed venues: Hyperliquid's JELLY forced settlement (~$140M outflows), dYdX's 8-hour halt with frozen liquidations, Lighter's 4.5h sequencer outage during the $19B cascade, Paradex's $0 BTC print and chain rollback, and Drift's $295M social-engineering loss and rebrand to Velocity. The market now treats execution transparency, incident comms, and governance hygiene as UX surfaces. This is directly relevant to Noether: it is oracle-priced (its closest architectural relatives are GMX v2 and Jupiter Perps, both billion-dollar-volume venues with no order book and few pairs), it settles every action as one atomic wallet-signed Soroban transaction in ~5 seconds, and it currently carries a single-EOA admin (SEC-3). Jupiter proves that "3 majors, oracle price = execution price, zero slippage" is a winning product shape when stated confidently; GMX proves that a keeper-execution gap is the single largest complaint generator — a gap Noether's verify-then-trade router structurally eliminates and does not yet advertise.

The benchmarking below covers seven groups. For each: signature patterns, what Noether should adopt (adjusted for oracle pricing, ~5s deterministic ledgers, 3 pairs, gold-on-dark brand, pro-first audience), and anti-patterns to avoid.

---

### Hyperliquid — the gold standard and the expectation-setter

Hyperliquid is where Noether's target audience forms its expectations. Its "CEX feel" is three stacked mechanisms — sub-second HyperBFT finality, a one-time gasless "Establish Connection" signature creating an agent key (zero popups, zero gas afterward), and websocket-pushed UI state. Only the second and third are portable to Stellar; the 5s ledger cannot be hidden, so it must be made legible instead.

**Signature patterns**

- **Deliberately conventional trade grid** with a header stat strip showing Mark AND Oracle price side by side, 24h change/volume, OI, and funding rate with a countdown to the next hourly funding. Familiarity is the pro-onboarding strategy.
- **One TP/SL mental model across three surfaces**: order-form toggle (OCO children activate when the parent fills), a TP/SL button per position row (whole-position default), and draggable chart lines (drags that would instantly execute are rejected). Triggers always fire on mark price.
- **Per-asset leverage/margin-mode modal** that persists as that market's default; the ticket live-previews order value, margin required, and est. liquidation price.
- **Positions table as command center**: size, entry, mark, uPnL, liq price, margin, accrued funding, TP/SL column with edit modal, Market/Limit close, partial close by %.
- **Portfolio = Account Value + PnL graphs** (24h/7d/30d, 15-min sampling, vault balances included, Max Drawdown headline) with an explicit "not precise accounting" disclaimer.
- **Vault economics identical to Noether's `vault_factory`** — 10% leader profit share above high-water mark, hard ≥5% leader minimum stake — but presented with PnL curve, APR, TVL, max drawdown, open positions, depositor list, lockups, and a "Your Performance" tab. Leaders trade their vault through the *same* trade page via an account-context dropdown, not a separate manage UI.
- **Referral numbers matching Noether's contract** (4% referee / 10% referrer), volume-gated code creation, claim-to-balance above $1.
- **Points seasons** (1M/week, every Wednesday) that manufactured the HYPE airdrop loyalty flywheel; **builder codes** (user-signed max-fee approval for third-party frontends) turned competitors into distribution — ~40% of daily actives trade via third-party UIs, top three builders earned >$31M.
- **Notifications-first mobile**: after years of criticized PWA-only mobile, the official Android MVP (Apr 2026) shipped fill notifications *before* trading.

**Adopt for Noether**

1. Keep the exact grid arrangement so muscle memory transfers — but extend the now-real header strip (the old hardcoded OI/volume were replaced with indexer reads — verified in the same-day staging audit; the remaining sin is the failure fallback, which must render "—", never a fabricated value) and show Noeracle SSE mark beside the on-chain shim price as an "Oracle vs Mark" honesty feature, plus a funding countdown and per-position accrued-but-unpaid funding (funding is lazy).
2. Retitle the "Order Book" slot — it only ever shows the user's own pending limit orders. "Your Open Orders" or an "Oracle Feed" panel (recent signed attestations with timestamps) turns the no-CLOB architecture into a transparency showcase.
3. Package existing stop/take primitives as a TP/SL toggle at entry; since Soroban has no native OCO, either batch open + triggers in one tx or show an explicit "1/2 opening… 2/2 placing TP/SL" stepper — never leave a user unsure whether their stop exists when each step costs 5s.
4. Gold leverage badge → modal (1–10x integer slider, cross/isolated segmented control, persisted per pair) with live margin/liq/fee preview computed from `contracts/market/src/position.rs` math (1% maintenance margin — not copied CEX formulas).
5. Vault work is purely presentational: marketplace columns (PnL sparkline, TVL, leader, age), detail pages with "est. leader fee if you withdraw now" against the HWM, and an account-context switcher in the /trade header ("Trading as: Main / Vault X" gold-bordered chip) so `/vaults/[id]/manage` never grows into a second trading UI.
6. Portfolio graphs via a 15-min sampling cron into the existing Turso pipeline; copy the honesty disclaimer.
7. Session-key ladder: now — one review-rich signature per intent; next — pre-built tx + wallet auto-sign under a notional cap; mainnet — Stellar smart-account/passkey policy signers scoped to open/close but never withdrawals ("Sign once. Trade all session.").
8. Treat api-keys + SDKs as a builder-codes-style growth channel: add an optional fee-attribution/referrer field to `packages/tx-builders` now so it isn't a breaking change later.

**Anti-patterns to avoid**

- TP/SL market triggers defaulting to 10% slippage tolerance, discovered after losing money — Noether should state the exact execution rule (oracle price ± the P1-5 deviation band) and show "max execution price" in the TP/SL form; bounded oracle fills are a genuine advantage worth advertising.
- Stop-limits silently failing on gaps with no proactive warning — warn inline when an SL-limit is tighter than the deviation band, and recommend market-trigger "guaranteed close at oracle price."
- JELLY-style admin intervention without a pre-published rule: with a single admin EOA, Noether is one screenshot from an "FTX 2.0" accusation — publish the intervention/pause policy and multisig roadmap *before* any incident.
- Cockpit density with zero progressive disclosure ("a cockpit when all you needed was a steering wheel"); silent sub-minimum deposit loss (enforce the 10 USDC minimum client-side, never as a swallowed tx); points-criteria opacity; referral exclusions stated only in footnotes — Noether's gateway-applied discount is exactly the kind of quiet divergence to label in the UI until the market redeploy adds the on-chain hook.

### dYdX v4 — the mature pro interface, and a cautionary decline

dYdX publishes its design doctrine outright: reliable information architecture reinforces trust; critical data upfront with persistent **before → after summaries**; frequent actions in **2–3 steps max**. Its arc is equally instructive: market share fell 73% → ~7% (Jan 2023 → end 2024) while Hyperliquid took >75% — incentive-dependent volume, extra signing friction, and liquidity network effects beat technical decentralization.

**Signature patterns**

- **Zero-scroll 100vh tabbed layout** with minimizable positions strip, rearrangeable trade box, customizable header stats, and a **Cmd+K command palette**.
- **Progressive order-type disclosure**: Market/Limit top-level; Stop Market/Limit, TP Market/Limit under one conditional dropdown; TIF hidden in a per-type Advanced expander with strong defaults (Limit = GTD 28d, Market = IOC, conditional market = 10% max-slippage default) and a one-line intent description per type.
- **Margin UX (v5.0)**: margin mode dropdown atop the panel; isolated = protocol subaccounts; user sets *target leverage* and collateral moves only when an order is placed; dual-input leverage (slider + synced numeric field); per-market max leverage shrinks with size.
- **Popup-free trading after 2 signatures** that derive a client-held chain key; order place/cancel is gas-free. Reviewers still rank Hyperliquid's session model smoother — signature count is a competitive metric.
- **Engineered perceived speed**: submitted orders render immediately via the Indexer before block commit; loading states explicitly acknowledge decentralized latency; last-traded price is clamped inside an oracle band so triggers can't be tripped by rogue prints.
- **TP/SL from the positions row** (hover → triggers modal) with entry/liq/TP/SL as chart lines; mobile app defaults to a Simplified mode (risk chips Low/Med/High, buying power) with full-parity Pro mode.
- **MegaVault** APR shown with explicit methodology (trailing 30d × 365/30), enumerated yield sources, blunt loss copy; **Surge** points are auto-enrolled, no claim step, scored on taker volume/fees not PnL; affiliates get instant lifetime USDC commissions (up to $3,000/referral/month).

**Adopt for Noether**

1. The 2–3-step rule plus before → after blocks: render equity, free margin, est. liq price, margin used, and fee (tier projection already computed) inline in OrderPanel so the Freighter popup is the *only* extra step; reuse the same summary component in close, adjust-collateral, and vault flows. Pre-sign verification is worth more on a 5s chain with no fast undo.
2. Demote the own-orders "Order Book" to an Open Orders tab in the bottom strip; reclaim width for chart + ticket.
3. Copy the order-type packaging with honest oracle semantics: "triggers fire on the Noeracle oracle price, executed by keepers," and state what Post-Only/IOC mean when there is no matching book.
4. Per-pair "Contract details" popover (spec sheet): 10x max, 10 USDC min collateral, **1% maintenance margin**, 0.02%/0.05% fees + tier table, hourly lazy funding, Noeracle with 60s staleness bound and deviation band. Nearly free; exactly what Binance-native pros read before sizing.
5. Optimistic acknowledgment: ghost row on sign, staged status ("signed → submitted → in ledger (~5s) → confirmed"), failure path that restores state. Surface the deviation band as trust copy, mirroring dYdX's trigger clamping.
6. Cmd+K palette via the shadcn/ui `cmdk` already in the stack — cheap pro signal (3 pairs, Long/Short focus, Portfolio, Vault, Faucet, margin toggle).
7. /vault: kill the placeholder APR with a trailing 7d/30d method labeled next to the number, enumerated sources ("trader losses, trading fees"), plain risk copy. /leaderboard: auto-enrolled monthly seasons scored on fee-paying volume (14-day volume is already tracked), pinned "your rank" row.
8. Mobile: responsive two-density /trade (simple ticket by default at mobile widths, Pro toggle) — matching the founders' pro-first-with-disclosure decision. Swipe-to-trade does NOT map; a 5s signed tx must never fire from an accident-prone gesture.

**Anti-patterns to avoid**

- Per-action wallet friction compounding (Noether is structurally worse off; audit every flow for two signatures where one would do — trustline + approve + deposit, TP+SL as separate txs).
- Multi-hop deposit ceremony — dYdX's most-cited complaint; on mainnet, market Stellar's native Circle USDC as "one asset, one chain, ~5 seconds."
- Incentive-dependent volume mistaken for retention; an 8-hour halt with frozen liquidations and improvised comms (build the degraded-state banner, status page, and pre-written comms *now*); sunsets handled as announcements, not product flows — Noether's testnet resets and blue-green redeploys are mini-sunsets needing in-app migration notices; hidden dangerous defaults (the 10% conditional slippage buried in docs — Noether's analog: surface the deviation band and return human-readable abort copy, or protective reverts read as random 5s failures); docs lagging the UI.

### GMX v2 — the closest architectural analog

GMX is the reference for presenting an oracle-priced, no-order-book venue without faking anything — and the reference for how a two-step keeper execution gap generates every angry forum thread. Noether's router executes verify-then-trade in ONE atomic tx, eliminating GMX's entire created→executed failure class; that structural advantage should be marketed and visualized, not hidden.

**Signature patterns**

- **No fake order book.** A market-header stat strip (oracle price, OI per side, available liquidity per side, net funding/borrow rate per hour) plus a **"Depth" tab** rendering the *virtual* liquidity curve — execution price vs position size around a dotted oracle line.
- **CEX-vocabulary trade box** (Mar 2026 redesign): "Pay" renamed **"Margin"**, leverage selector in the trade-box header (slider + clickable stops, persists), size slider with USD/token toggle, and a collapsed **"Execution Details"** accordion (liq price, fees, network fee net of keeper refund with tooltip breakdown, spread, slippage, stored price impact).
- **TP/SL at entry as % of position value** with est. PnL, plus a ⊕ per position row opening a multi-level TP/SL modal; **auto-cancel of orphaned TP/SL** when the position fully closes (default-on, capped, warned).
- **Two-button close (Market + TWAP)** each opening with a size-% slider — partial close is the default interaction; TWAP is composed app-layer from standard limit orders with a "(3/5)" progress chip.
- **Three explicit trading modes** — Classic (popup per trade), Express (sign locally, Gelato relays, gas in USDC), Express + One-Click (local sub-account key auto-signs N actions) — same fees, one-line descriptions, recommended default.
- **Written non-guarantees**: "Limit/Stop/TP-SL orders are not guaranteed to execute"; "a Stop-Loss is a trigger order, not a guaranteed exit before liquidation"; failed-check orders enter a "frozen" state.
- **Pools page** with APY benchmarked against just holding the backing tokens, sellable liquidity as an explicit formula (pool × reserve factor − reserved by positions), and buy-box price impact.
- **Contract-native referral**: browser-sticky code written on-chain at the first order; discount applies automatically at contract level, no claiming for the trader; Traders/Affiliates tabs. **Leaderboard ranked by both PnL ($) and PnL (%)**, auto-qualified, net of fees.
- **GMX Account modal** on wallet-click: unified balance, PnL analysis, and a notification suite (price alerts, trade confirmations, liquidation-risk alerts).

**Adopt for Noether**

1. The stat strip + honest liquidity module verbatim (OI from market totals, available liquidity from vault free collateral, real funding). Noether has no price impact, so the Depth analog is simpler: max open size per side around the Noeracle mark, with the user's resting triggers plotted — panel named "Liquidity" or "Your Orders," never "Order Book."
2. The trade-box grammar: "Margin" label, header leverage popover with clickable stops, size slider, and an Execution Details accordion that is *simpler and honest* — entry (Noeracle mark), liq price, fee + tier note, referral discount line, Stellar network fee (a flex vs GMX's ETH execution fee), staleness bound.
3. Auto-cancel orphaned triggers via keeper on `position_closed`/`position_liquidated` events if WASM budget blocks on-chain OCO — and state the behavior in the UI. Surface a max-orders-per-position number instead of failing silently (Vec-based O(n) storage).
4. Close modal built for the contract's reality: today `close_position` takes no size argument (partial close was removed for WASM budget), so ship a zero-typing full-close modal now with a "positions close in full" line, and add the % slider when the C2 WASM refit reinstates partial close. Skip TWAP (nothing to smooth at $100K cap and zero impact), but steal app-layer composite orders and the "(tx 2 of 2)" progress idiom.
5. Order-cancellation reasons: Noether's `order_cancelled` event already carries a `reason` field — ALWAYS render it as plain language in the Orders tab and toasts. GMX's bare "order cancelled" + raw Arbiscan revert produced its angriest threads ("It didn't cost me 30,000 dollars but it did cost me to lose money instead of make it").
6. Copy the pending-status discipline for keeper-executed trigger orders (Open → Triggered → Executed/Cancelled+reason via indexer/WS) while advertising the inversion for market actions: "Executed atomically in one transaction — no keeper delay, no execution fee."
7. /vault: computed APR decomposed into fee income vs trader-PnL settlement, "withdrawable now" from vault free collateral minus reserved margin, formula in a tooltip. Referral: two-tab Traders/Affiliates page; fee line must show "referral −4%" explicitly. Leaderboard: dual $-PnL and %-PnL ranking so small testnet accounts get a ladder.
8. Wallet-chip account modal (USDC balance, free margin, cross health, claimable referral fees) and WS-driven liquidation-risk alerts.
9. Fee legibility as a game: "Tier 1 in $3.2K more volume" progress hint from the 14-day rolling volume the indexer already tracks. Do NOT fake OI-imbalance pricing Noether doesn't have.

**Anti-patterns to avoid**

- Silent order failure and jargon states ("frozen," "stored price impact") forcing docs round-trips; fee opacity through stacking, where costs computed at entry are charged at close — Noether's lazy funding is the identical trap: never let close-time PnL include a line the user never saw accruing.
- A panel that looks like an order book but is not (Noether's current version is a *stronger* form of this deception); unpredictable carrying costs (Noether has no borrow fee — keep it that way and preview funding plainly); a beginner cliff with no guided first trade (the /faucet is Noether's unfair advantage — wire the checklist); wallet-capability differences discovered at first failed signature (label Freighter/LOBSTR/LON differences in the picker); trust-eroding placeholder data — for a venue asking pros to sign real transactions, any number that can be caught being fake is worse than no number.

### Solana cohort — Drift (now Velocity) and Jupiter Perps

Critical context: **Drift no longer exists as a brand.** It lost ~$295M on April 1, 2026 to a North-Korea-linked social-engineering attack on its multisig signers and relaunched as Velocity DEX (July 1–2, 2026, USDT collateral, private beta). Study its UX legacy; treat its collapse as a governance lesson mapping directly to Noether's SEC-3 single-EOA admin. **Jupiter Perps is Noether's architectural twin** — oracle-priced, pool-as-counterparty, no order book, only 3 majors — and still leads Solana perp OI "with no token incentives." Three oracle-priced pairs is a proven product shape; stop apologizing for it.

**Signature patterns**

- **Worst-case-price framing**: Drift's 5s JIT Dutch auction showed the *maximum* price the taker could pay; makers could only improve it. A multi-second window framed as a bounded guarantee, not an unknown.
- **Typed order lifecycle**: Jupiter's two-tx keeper model walks request → executed/rejected as discrete states, with rejections (stale oracle, slippage) refunding visibly. Latency is measured and published (Drift v3: "85% of market orders filled within the same slot ~400ms"; Jupiter: ~0.4s).
- **Friction ladder**: Drift Swift gasless signed-message orders (keepers land the tx, MEV-protected) became the default; one-click auto-signing on the v3 roadmap; Jupiter accepts **any SPL token as collateral** with backend auto-swap.
- **Risk as one number**: Drift Health = 1 − maintenance margin/collateral, colored bar, Initial vs Maintenance toggle, partial-then-full liquidation. Jupiter shows liq price + hourly-compounding borrow fee per position; row-level Add TP/Add SL with **auto-cancel on manual close**.
- **FUEL points**: instant accrual (1/$1 volume), per-market boosters, published rates, linear no-tier redemption; taker/maker-split leaderboards with near-real-time reward estimates; referral 15% referrer / 5% referee.
- **Cost-of-carry transparency won by revolt**: Jupiter's Dec 2024 fee crisis (full JLP utilization spiked hourly borrow fees) forced a public fee cut (0.1%→0.07%) and co-founder threads; docs literally instruct users to "regularly check your borrow fees and liquidation price."
- **Mobile bar**: Jupiter's native app (V3, Jan 2026: fully native pro terminal; touch sliders for leverage/collateral/stops) vs Drift reaching beta only in Jan 2026.

**Adopt for Noether**

1. Worst-case-price framing for the sign→ledger gap: quote from the Noeracle SSE mark and render the button as a bound — "Long BTC — max entry $64,321" — enforceable on-chain via the P1-5 deviation band (error #81), with "entry will be ≤ $64,321 or the tx reverts" during the pending window.
2. Copy the state machine, not the mechanism: every action gets signed → submitted → confirmed-in-ledger-N (or typed failure), with contract errors mapped to human reasons and a next action ("#30 price too stale — refreshing quote"). Then market the difference: "no pending requests, no keeper queue — your trade lands at oracle price in one transaction or fully reverts."
3. Deterministic ledger-progress bar instead of a spinner (~5s close cadence is *easier* to communicate than an auction); publish measured p50/p95 confirm times.
4. Health as a numeric % + amber-gradient bar from the existing `position.rs` equity/maintenance logic (pending funding included), dual breakdown tooltip, honest note that liquidation is currently full (partial liq is the T3 target).
5. Live cost-of-carry per position: accrued funding to date, hourly rate and direction, and **funding-adjusted liquidation price** — converting the lazy-funding implementation detail from a trust risk into a transparency feature.
6. Row-level +TP/+SL chips pre-filled reduce-only, auto-cancel of linked triggers on close (batch in one tx or fire immediately after close confirmation, both shown in the lifecycle toast).
7. Deliberately do NOT adopt any-token collateral (Jupiter owns Solana's dominant swap aggregator; Noether has no in-house route, and USDC-only keeps vault accounting legible). Adopt the goal instead: a first-run checklist — fund XLM → USDC trustline → faucet → first trade — since trustlines are the #1 place Stellar newcomers stall.
8. A trust page before mainnet: admin-key model (SEC-3 → 2-of-3 multisig P3-8), oracle trust path including the O-1 disclosure (Noeracle persistent writes not yet publisher-authenticated), audit status, deviation-band protections. Post-Drift, this cohort's users diligence governance before depositing; a candid page is a conversion surface that costs a static page.

**Anti-patterns to avoid**

- Utilization-driven cost surprise and silently drifting liquidation prices ("if your docs tell users to poll, your UI failed"); liquidation seizing 100% of remaining collateral (keep Noether's 5% fee capped at 10% of collateral explicit; treat partial liquidation as a competitive feature); orphaned limit orders that outlive the position and re-open exposure; unexplained async failures and mystery refund txs; paywalled first sessions (Drift's ~0.035–0.225 SOL account rent — Noether: state XLM base reserve, trustline reserve, and 10 USDC minimum up front); clean UI masking margin complexity (simplify the surface, never the risk numbers); crisis handling that privileges insiders (Velocity's $5M-threshold recovery tokens: "my money still gone… rebrand and make some millions"); breaking doc URLs on rebrand.

### New-generation venues — Lighter, Paradex, Vertex, edgeX/Extended

This cohort defines the 2026 friction baseline: sign once, then trade with zero popups and zero visible gas. Lighter's official desktop/mobile UIs are literally API-key clients of its own venue (256 keys per account, indexes 0–3 reserved for the first-party apps); Paradex derives the trading key from one SIWE signature and keeps it only in RAM; Extended derives an L2 keypair from one L1 signature; Vertex 1CT scopes a localStorage key to trading-only with a 5-enables-per-week abuse guard. A venue where every trade pops a wallet modal is now categorically behind — Noether's single biggest gap.

**Signature patterns**

- **Sign-once session keys with hard scopes** (trade-yes, withdraw-never) and explicit lifecycle (expiry, "Remember Me," pre-expiry warnings).
- **Gasless via sponsor** — the only visible cost is the maker/taker bps line; zero-fee headlines (Lighter 0/0, Paradex 0% retail) monetized elsewhere; Lighter even *sells latency* (free accounts get deliberate ~200/300ms delays; staked accounts pay for fast lanes).
- **One signature = one intent bundle**: TP and SL attached at placement as "linked trigger orders" (Vertex), editable from the table or by dragging chart lines.
- **Dual-lane onboarding**: email/MPC lane (edgeX: 6-digit code → auto-generated address) beside the wallet lane; Extended marketed as "EVM in front, Starknet in the back."
- **Points seasons with published mechanics as best practice** (edgeX: 250K/week, Wednesdays, 60/20/10/10 split published; referral link = permanent 1.1x multiplier) vs opaque formulas as the loudest complaint (Lighter never disclosed accrual; Extended tweeted "exact criteria won't be made public"); Extended converted downtime into 300K itemized compensation points.
- **Vault trust kit**: operator profit share set at creation (Paradex 0–30%), LP tokens usable as collateral, disclosed withdrawal friction (edgeX 48h–7d locks), card metric row of TVL / age / PnL curve / max drawdown / operator stake.
- **Reliability as the real differentiator**: Lighter's single sequencer died ~4.5h during the Oct 10–11 2025 cascade ("manageable situations became total liquidations," LP pool −5.35%); Paradex's Jan 19 2026 migration bug printed BTC at $0, mass-liquidated, and forced a chain rollback.

**Adopt for Noether**

1. The realistic session path: a `authorize_session(session_pubkey, expiry_ledger, scope=open/close/cancel-only, max_notional)` delegation on the market/router, browser-held ed25519 key, relayer fee-bumps so the session account needs no XLM. Derive the key deterministically from one Freighter signature (RFC 8032 determinism) so the same wallet re-derives it on any device — and mint the gateway API key and the trading session key in ONE signature ceremony (the api/ wallet-challenge auth already exists). Scope constraint is non-negotiable: never vault or collateral withdrawals, enforced at contract level. Header pill: "1-Click: ON · expires 8h."
2. Fee sponsorship via Stellar-native fee-bump transactions, guarded by the gateway's tiered rate-limit; one microcopy line "network fees sponsored."
3. Bundle open + TP + SL into one router tx (one signature, one ledger wait) — batching is worth double on a 5s chain; label row-level edits with their honest cost ("≈5s").
4. Latency honesty inverted: publish the number ("≈5s on-chain settle"), progress bar tied to ledger close, and "price locked at $67,432.10, settling…" (the router relays the signed attestation in the same tx, so the price IS locked at submit — unlike a CLOB fill). Do not copy pay-for-latency tiering.
5. Optimistic acknowledgment decoupled from settlement: pending rows reconciled against indexer events over the existing WS channels; decoded errors ("#81 → price outside safety band — trade rejected to protect you") with one-click retry. This is worth refactoring /trade's useState+useEffect data flow into the Zustand tradeStore.
6. Season 0 with edgeX-style published split mapped to existing surfaces (taker volume, filled limit-order volume, vault TVL-days, referrals — all in Turso), fixed pot, fixed day, anti-sybil rules declared before the season; use the compensation-points lever for testnet resets.
7. Vault cards: TVL, age, leader stake badge — the contract-enforced 5% leader min-holding is a genuine differentiator and currently invisible ("leader holds ≥5%, enforced on-chain") — profit share, PnL sparkline, max drawdown; advertise "no lockup" vs edgeX's 48h–7d.
8. Incident UX: when the deviation band trips, show "price protection active — trading paused on BTC-PERP" as a state, not a raw failed tx; keep the direct-to-chain close path in `web/lib/stellar` working even when the gateway/indexer (Railway) is down, with a header status dot fed by `/v1/health` + attestation age.
9. With exactly 3 pairs, skip the market-search modal entirely: a persistent 3-tab market strip with live mark, 24h change, and funding countdown — something 200-market venues structurally cannot offer.

**Anti-patterns to avoid**

- Opaque points formulas and post-hoc sybil-slashing drama; points as the only product (Lighter's volume −83% after TGE; Vertex sunset despite praised UX, VRTX −98%); going down exactly when users need the close button; price-integrity failure on a non-CLOB venue (Paradex's $0 print is literally Noether's threat model — never disable the band/staleness guards for convenience, and pause liquidations on stale/deviant attestations); session keys without hard scopes; fake or hardcoded venue stats; undisclosed exit friction and minimums; wallet-popup-per-action as the default; token promises the product can't sustain; ops fragility unrehearsed — Noether's single-EOA admin and manual env topology across Vercel/Railway are the analogous risks (the D-4 stale-indexer incident was this class).

### CEX futures — Binance, Bybit, OKX: the muscle memory Noether inherits

CEXs define what "a futures UI" means to every trader Noether targets. The transferable material is convention, not architecture.

**Signature patterns**

- **The canonical 5-region grid** (Binance documents it as numbered regions) with the funding rate + countdown treated as mandatory contract-strip context.
- **[Cross][20x] pills** atop the ticket; leverage modal with tick-marked slider and a max-notional readout that shrinks as leverage rises; per-symbol persistence and account-age defaults.
- **Suppressible per-action confirmation dialogs** (default-on; Bybit pleads to keep them; pros disable for speed) — the *setting* is the pattern, not the dialog.
- **Live "Cost" and "Max" readouts** under the side buttons (Bybit documents Order Cost to the formula: initial margin + fee to open + fee to close); order value and margin cost always distinct, labeled numbers; OKX previews est. liquidation price in-panel.
- **TP/SL checkbox at placement + pencil-edit on the row**; Binance Split Target allows 4 TP + 4 SL rows validated by an allocation pie chart; Bybit distinguishes entire-position vs partial TP/SL.
- **Margin Ratio module** bottom-right (liquidation at 100%; OKX warns at 300%; Bybit sells a programmable auto-derisk "MMR Close Order") — one number that says "how close to death am I."
- **Trigger-price-type selector (Last/Mark/Index)** on every conditional — and the permanent "why was I liquidated when the candle never touched my liq price" FAQ class it generates.
- **Chart as management surface** (draggable order lines behind a confirmation toggle) and **keyboard-first order prep** (Binance: F1 buy input, Cmd+B/S place, Enter confirms).
- Progressive disclosure as whole-app modes (Binance Lite/Pro) — which reviewers say still doesn't fix trade-page density.

**Adopt for Noether**

1. Preserve the geography exactly; put a cross-margin Account Health card in the bottom-right slot where trained eyes look; contract strip = oracle price + real funding + staleness.
2. The pills + slider modal verbatim, persisted per pair client-side, defaulting to 2–3x (never 10x), with the $100K cap and 10 USDC minimum disclosed in the modal, and "applies to new positions" stated (no on-chain leverage adjust exists).
3. Cost/Max/Est-liq rows under the size field — the single highest-leverage muscle-memory fix; pros will not sign a tx without seeing cost and liq price first. Default size entry in USDC notional with a read-only asset conversion line; keep the 25/50/75/100% row.
4. Invert the confirmation pattern: the wallet prompt IS the confirm, so put the review payload inline pre-signature ("Sign & Submit"), offer one "review before signing" toggle, and reserve in-app modals for bulk-destructive actions only.
5. Positions-row schema and inline [Market] close with pending pulse; skip Quick Reverse (two sequential 5s txs); deliberately OMIT an Add-margin button rather than graying it out (`add_collateral` was removed for WASM size).
6. **Conspicuously delete the trigger-price-type selector**: one Noeracle price marks, triggers, and liquidates. Caption: "Triggers on oracle price — the same price that marks and liquidates." Market "no mark-vs-last ambush liquidations" as a trust differentiator — while labeling the chart's SSE stream "Oracle price" with a staleness indicator so displayed and settling prices never silently diverge.
7. Phase chart lines: read-only entry/liq/trigger lines first (gold/amber/red); drag-to-modify only when modify is one signed tx (today cancel+re-place = two signatures ~10s).
8. Keyboard shortcuts that compress everything *before* the popup ("one keystroke to signature," `?` overlay); a **single consolidated trade-settings sheet** (confirmations, per-pair defaults, read-only "Position mode: One-way," display units, shortcuts) — converting a documented CEX pain point into an onboarding advantage.
9. Post-liquidation forensics: every liquidation is a public tx with a `position_liquidated` event — render expandable rows (oracle execution price, threshold crossed, 5% fee/keeper reward breakdown, Stellar Expert link). "Every liquidation is verifiable on-chain" is the anti-Bybit-FAQ pitch.
10. Funding chip with CEX placement but Noether-truthful copy: "Funding 0.0100%/h (settles on close)" — omitting the qualifier generates "where's my funding payment" tickets.

**Anti-patterns to avoid**

- Clutter causing misclick losses (G2 reviews; ~20% of viewport on chrome) — with 3 pairs, disciplined empty space on near-black is on-brand and safer; promo/social sprawl inside the trading surface (the ?ref banner must never overlay the ticket or chart); dangerous leverage defaults (92.95% of simulated 25x trades liquidate, avg ~22.5 min — vendor simulation figure, directional); displayed liq price ≠ actual fill with no recourse (always show the on-chain execution price); per-symbol-looking controls that bind account-wide (Noether's per-position margin mode is genuinely per-position — label it and win a clarity point); confirmation stacking that trains users to disable all safety; settings scattered across four preference trees; buried load-bearing risk numbers (state "maintenance margin is always 1% of position value — no hidden tiers").
