### Retail-simple apps — Robinhood and Coinbase: risk communication and confirmation friction

Robinhood no longer picks simple OR pro — radically simple mobile defaults plus Robinhood Legend (free desktop, one-click auto-send) on one account: friction is opt-out per user, not a separate "simple mode." Its EU perps ticket (via Bitstamp) is the closest retail comp to Noether: Long/Short → Market/Limit → size in qty or USD → margin selector defaulting to **"Isolated 1x"** → leverage dropdown → collapsed "Take profit / stop loss: Add" → order summary with estimated costs and margin impact → swipe up to submit.

**Signature patterns**

- **Safe-default ticket with opt-in complexity**; risk communicated as a **named three-threshold margin ladder** in plain language (initial 20% / maintenance 10% / close-out 5%, cadence stated) plus **green/yellow/red dots** on position rows (yellow = margin ratio 80–100%); Coinbase compresses it to one 0–100% "liquidation buffer."
- **Exactly one confirmation point scaled to stakes** (swipe-up after a receipt showing total cost, fees, margin impact; Legend pros replace it with auto-send). 45% of Coinbase one-star reviews trace to fees discovered *after* confirming (single case-study figure — directional).
- **Education at the moment of intent**: Options Strategy Builder payoff diagrams (breakeven lines, max gain/loss) flowing into a pre-filled ticket; contract-spec cards on the instrument page, not in a gate; Learn-and-Earn (>$200M distributed) proved paying users token amounts to learn converts.
- **The Kearns rule**: catastrophic failures were display failures — a raw −$730K balance that wasn't a real obligation preceded a suicide and a record $70M FINRA fine for misleading displayed information. Every scary number needs one plain sentence of context; every cost visible before the final action.
- **Never block the next action**: Instant Deposits credit buying power before settlement; the perps **price ladder** prefills a limit order from any tapped level and — killer feature for a no-depth venue — can display **estimated position P&L at each price level** (pure client-side math).
- **Explore-first onboarding wins**: Coinbase loses 60–90% of users before first transaction; 25–40% abandon in KYC; verify-first walls score 6/10 vs explore-first 9.5/10 (Elsewhen).

**Adopt for Noether**

1. Default new wallets to Isolated + 1–2x; keep TIF/reduce-only/TP-SL as one-click collapsed disclosures that remember state.
2. A three-row "Margin to open / Margin to keep / Liquidation" block with live USDC values and a gold "?" popover stating cadence honestly ("evaluated against Noeracle mark; liquidation executed by keeper bots, typically within one 5s ledger").
3. Health dots per position + one buffer % for cross — and fire the warning tier EARLIER than Robinhood's 80% (e.g. 70%), because the remedy is a ~5s signed tx and there is no `add_collateral`; the alert CTA deep-links to the pre-built remedy tx.
4. The receipt lives ABOVE the wallet-sign step — the Freighter XDR popup is unreadable to most users, so the in-panel receipt is the de-facto confirmation. Include the honesty line: "Fills at Noeracle oracle price — no order book, no slippage."
5. Rebuild the fake "Order Book" as a **Price Ladder**: rows bracketing the live mark, click-to-prefill, own orders as gold markers, P&L-at-price toggle for open positions — the form works without pretending depth exists.
6. A thin payoff strip in the ticket: entry (gold dot), liquidation (red dot), TP/SL markers; leverage changes visibly drag the liq dot toward entry — "10x" becomes distance-to-death without a word of copy.
7. TP/SL inputs in three modes (price / % / **USDC amount** — "risk at most 25 USDC" is instantly parsed by pros and newcomers alike).
8. One-time three-card first-trade explainer per wallet (10x math, 1% maintenance + keeper fee, lazy funding), dismissible forever, skipped if the wallet has on-chain history — zero quizzes, zero lockouts. Upgrade /faucet into a guided first-session checklist (claim → open ≥10 USDC long at 2x → add TP → close, surfacing lazy-funding settlement experientially); completion unlocks a referral code or leaderboard badge, and the loop doubles as the SCF demo script.
9. Money-denominated risk sentences everywhere ("Liq. $58,340 — if BTC drops 4.2%, this position closes and you lose ~96 USDC"); "settling…" instead of unreconciled intermediate PnL. Empty states as one-sentence training manuals with exactly one gold CTA (and dash failed reads instead of rendering $0, so a broken balance can never masquerade as an empty state).
10. Explore-first: /trade fully alive with zero wallet — streaming marks, working chart, full receipt preview — wallet prompt only at submit. Never toast "connect wallet" on page load.

**Anti-patterns to avoid**

- Celebration gamification of risk-taking (confetti was cited in the $70M fine; never animate position OPENS — celebrate completed setup steps instead; with a gold-particle brand the temptation will exist); alarming numbers without context; hidden/late fees; verification walls before value; frozen (or worse, fake) data for logged-out visitors; quiz-lockout theater; "protective" defaults that silently cost users (a silent deviation-band revert is a collar-without-explanation — decode every guard rejection); a simplicity ceiling pros hit (collapse complexity, never delete it); notification spam that manufactures trading (alert only on margin health, fills, liquidation warnings, funding extremes — never "BTC is pumping!").

### Noether today — the honest baseline (live probe, 2026-07-04)

The Noether column below is grounded in a live-site probe plus repo context, not aspiration. Headlines: the documented testnet URL **testnet.noether.exchange is NXDOMAIN** (yet remains the primary CTA in the GitHub README); the whole app ships on the apex. **/trade serves the Next.js SSR error shell** (`__next_error__`: zero server-rendered text, no CSS in initial HTML, auto-injected `noindex`) because `web/app/trade/page.tsx` calls `useSearchParams()` at line 82 with no Suspense boundary — first paint is a blank near-black page until ~549KB of JS hydrates. All 11 routes share one identical title/description/OG image (a wordmark-less logo square); the favicon is a 240KB bitmap-in-SVG; the viewport blocks pinch-zoom app-wide (WCAG 1.4.4). The "Order Book" panel shows only the user's own pending limit orders; the leaderboard SSRs 182 chars of empty headers. (Two long-documented issues — hardcoded market stats and an always-$0 portfolio USDC balance — were re-verified against staging the same day and are **fixed**; they appear in this document as history, not as live findings.) On the positive side: geo-gating with a named policy shipped, /terms is current, the 12.5% APY hardcode is gone in favor of an honest "shown once fee revenue is tracked," /referrals honestly labels fee sharing "v1.1," fonts are self-hosted, and the contract layer (advanced orders, cross-margin, vault economics, on-chain referral) is *ahead* of the presentation layer almost everywhere.

### What Noether should steal first — ten highest-leverage adoptions

Ordered by leverage given oracle pricing, ~5s atomic transactions, 3 pairs, and a 2-person team. Items 1–5 are presentation-layer work over contract capabilities that already exist.

1. **Latency choreography + instant branded shell.** Fix the `/trade` SSR error shell (one Suspense boundary around the `useSearchParams()` consumer at `web/app/trade/page.tsx:82`) so first paint is a gold-on-dark terminal skeleton, then give every action an optimistic pending row with staged states ("signed → submitted → in ledger ~5s → confirmed"), a determinate ledger-progress bar, "price locked at $X, settling…" copy, and decoded contract errors (#30, #81, and the `order_cancelled` reason field) with retry. Fed by the existing api WS channels, state in the Zustand tradeStore. This is the single most important "feel" investment for an audience raised on instant acks. *(Every venue; esp. dYdX, Drift, Jupiter, GMX.)*
2. **Finish the real market-stats strip; never fabricate on failure.** The core strip is now wired to real reads — extend it with an OI long/short split, next-funding countdown, and oracle staleness age (3 pairs = 3 cheap queries), and dash out the failure paths that still render green +0.0000% funding or $0 prices. Show Mark (Noeracle SSE) beside the on-chain shim price as an honesty feature. One caught fake stat poisons every real one for exactly the audience being targeted. *(Hyperliquid, GMX, Drift.)*
3. **Retire the fake "Order Book."** Rename the own-orders panel "Your Open Orders" and spend the reclaimed column on an oracle/liquidity module: mark + attestation age, deviation-band status, max open size per side, funding countdown — with a click-through to the signed attestation. Turns the no-CLOB architecture into the brand story ("every price is a signed, verifiable attestation"). A Robinhood-style price ladder with P&L-at-price is the later upgrade. *(GMX Depth tab, Jupiter, Robinhood EU.)*
4. **Pre-sign receipt: Cost / Max / Est. liq price / fee-tier rows** live in the OrderPanel (from `contracts/market/src/position.rs` math, 1% maintenance margin), with before → after equity/free-margin deltas and inline validation of the 10 USDC minimum and $100K cap — every rejected tx costs the user 5 seconds, so client-side pre-validation is worth 10x a CEX's. The wallet popup becomes a rubber stamp; no in-app confirm modal stacked on top. *(Binance/Bybit Cost+Max, dYdX 2–3-step rule.)*
5. **Lazy funding made visible.** Per-position accrued funding (client-computable from the stored global rate), hourly rate + direction chip ("settles on close"), and funding-adjusted liquidation price. Jupiter's fee revolt is the proof of what happens otherwise: close-time PnL that includes a line the user never saw accruing reads as the venue skimming. *(Jupiter, GMX, Robinhood EU.)*
6. **TP/SL packaged, not primitive.** Entry-time TP/SL toggle plus row-level +TP/+SL chips (reduce-only pre-filled), batched into one router tx if possible, otherwise an explicit "tx 1/2 → 2/2" stepper; three input modes (price / % / USDC-at-risk); keeper auto-cancel of orphaned triggers on `position_closed`/`position_liquidated`, stated in the UI. One trigger-price everywhere — conspicuously delete the Last/Mark selector and caption why. *(Hyperliquid, GMX, Bybit, Vertex.)*
7. **[Isolated|Cross][2x] pills with per-pair persistence,** slider modal (default 2–3x, never 10x; amber-tinted 8–10x zone), 25/50/75/100% size row, a per-pair contract-spec popover (10x / 10 USDC / 1% MM / fee tiers / lazy funding / 60s staleness / deviation band), and one consolidated trade-settings sheet. Muscle-memory parity for the Binance/Hyperliquid crowd at trivial state cost with 3 pairs. *(CEX trio, dYdX, GMX.)*
8. **Positions table as command center.** Full column schema (Size | Entry | Oracle | Liq | Margin | uPnL+ROI% | Funding | TP/SL | Close), one-click Market close with a 5s pending pulse, partial close % (post-C2 refit — the contract closes in full today), health dots warning at ~70% (earlier than CEX 80%, because the remedy is a 5s signed tx), and on-chain liquidation forensics rows linking the `position_liquidated` tx. *(Hyperliquid, CEX trio, Drift health model.)*
9. **First-session faucet checklist + explore-first trade page.** /trade fully alive with zero wallet (streaming marks, working receipt); connect prompted only at submit. For empty wallets: connect → faucet → trustline (auto-fixed in-flow) → first guided trade that surfaces lazy funding experientially, checkmarks persisted, collapsed for pros. The faucet erases the #1 onboarding cliff every competitor still has — and the loop is the SCF demo script. *(Coinbase funnel lessons, Hyperliquid onboarding, edgeX dual-lane.)*
10. **Vault + referral trust kit now; session keys + fee-bump sponsorship as the mainnet track.** Vaults: marketplace metric row (TVL, age, PnL sparkline, max drawdown), "leader holds ≥5%, enforced on-chain" badge, HWM "your performance" with est. leader fee, and the account-context switcher so leaders trade via /trade instead of a second UI. Referrals: show "referral −4%" in the fee line only when it is true on-chain — until the market redeploy lands the referral hook, disclose "discount applied at settlement" inline. In parallel, design the session-delegation entry (scoped open/close/cancel-only, never withdrawals, expiry, notional cap) plus relayer fee-bumps, and put passkey policy signers on the mainnet roadmap — "Sign once. Trade all session." is the one structural gap between Noether and the entire 2026 cohort. *(Hyperliquid vaults/agent keys, Paradex/Vertex/Lighter session patterns, GMX referral placement.)*

## UX Principles & Pattern Library

This chapter consolidates eight thematic research passes into one evidence-graded pattern library. Each theme follows a fixed format: **key findings** (what the evidence says, sources inline), a **pattern library table** (Pattern | Who uses it | How Noether applies it, with repo file:line references), and **anti-patterns** — including defects verified in the current codebase during this research. Claims the researchers could not trace to primary evidence are explicitly marked **folklore**. Everything operates inside the two fixed founder decisions: the gold-on-dark brand stays (improve within it), and the product is pro-first (Hyperliquid/Binance/Bybit expectations) with progressive disclosure for Stellar-native newcomers.

Six principles recur independently across the eight datasets:

1. **The wallet signature is the confirmation.** Freighter/LOBSTR signing already forces deliberation; a mandatory in-app confirm on top is a double-confirm. Confirmation modals are a per-user preference, default on for newcomers.
2. **Narrate the ~5s ledger, never hide it.** Stellar's predictable close time is a feedback asset: staged, time-estimated progress beats spinners; optimistic UI must confess estimates ("≈") until on-chain events return real values.
3. **Previews must match the engine.** Liq price, fees, and entry previews compute from the Noeracle mark and actual contract parameters (1% maintenance margin, deci-bps fee tiers). A wrong preview at 5s settlement latency is worse than none.
4. **Never encode meaning in hue alone** (WCAG 1.4.1) — doubly binding for a brand whose amber is nearly the conventional warning color.
5. **Honest small numbers beat fake big ones.** Hardcoded stats are the fastest trust-destroyer for the pro audience — Noether’s own history proves the failure mode (market stats and a 12.5% APY shipped hardcoded for months; both verified fixed/null-gated in the July 2026 staging audit). The live remnant is the failure fallbacks that still fabricate (+0.0000% funding, $0 prices on failed reads) — keep every number real or dashed.
6. **Progressive disclosure means collapse, not a beginner mode.** Advanced controls fold into persistent expanders; help is pull-based; no forced tours.

---

### Order Entry & Leverage

**Key findings**

- **Notional sizing is the convention; collateral-only entry is the outlier.** Every major venue sizes orders in base-asset qty and/or USD value with a toggle — Bybit "Order by Qty" vs "Order by Value" ([Bybit API](https://bybit-exchange.github.io/docs/v5/order/create-order)), dYdX's size toggle ([dYdX Help](https://help.dydx.trade/en/articles/166979-interface-of-the-trade-tab)), Hyperliquid USD/coin. Noether's OrderPanel accepts only "Pay (Collateral)" in USDC and derives size. Finance-education literature identifies collateral-vs-notional confusion as the main reason retail traders "misjudge exposure and blow up."
- **Sliders are for approximate values only.** [NN/g slider research](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/gui-slider-controls/): "whenever the exact value matters, sliders are not okay" — pair with a numeric input/stepper ([NN/g input steppers](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/input-steppers/)). Noether's 1–10 step-1 slider + pills is acceptable at 10x; it fails at mainnet 25x without a paired numeric field.
- **Leverage access causally drives losses.** [Heimer & Simsek (NBER w24176 / JFE 2019)](https://www.nber.org/papers/w24176): the 2010 US retail-FX leverage cap reduced high-leverage traders' losses by 40% without harming liquidity. A conservative default (1–2x) with explicit opt-up is evidence-backed, not paternalism.
- **TP/SL attach at open is table stakes and takes three input modes** — trigger price, % change, target P&L/ROI% — always echoing estimated P&L ([Bybit TP/SL](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/How-to-Set-Up-and-Modify-TP-SL-Perpetual-Futures-Contracts)), with OCO parent-child semantics ([Hyperliquid TP/SL](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/take-profit-and-stop-loss-orders-tp-sl)). Noether's P4-17 attach (plain price inputs, isolated+market only, extra follow-up signatures) covers a fraction of this.
- **Routine confirmation dialogs breed habituation and increase errors** ([NN/g confirmation dialogs](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/confirmation-dialog/), [NN/g preventing user errors](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/user-mistakes/)). Pro platforms make order confirmation a per-user setting: Binance ships a toggle plus Enter/Esc confirm ([Binance futures interface](https://www.binance.com/en/support/faq/how-to-customize-the-binance-futures-trading-interface-a784518335b0492a9ebfa4a72e1ca092)); Hyperliquid and GMX offer skip-confirm/one-click ([GMX 1CT](https://gmxio.substack.com/p/one-click-trading-is-now-available)). On a wallet-signed DEX the wallet popup is already a confirmation — a mandatory UI modal on top is a double-confirm anti-pattern.
- **Validate on blur, clear on keystroke.** [Baymard usability tests](https://baymard.com/blog/inline-form-validation): 31% of sites lack inline validation; premature mid-typing errors feel like being "yelled at"; remove errors live once fixed; show positive confirmation for high-stakes fields. [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/errors-forms-design-guidelines/): errors belong next to the field, never only in a summary; icons + color, not color alone.
- **The web3 CTA is a state machine.** [ethereum.org DEX guidance](https://ethereum.org/developers/docs/design-and-ux/dex-design-best-practice/): the primary button text carries the blocking reason ("Connect wallet", "Enter amount", "Insufficient balance"); multi-step transactions show progress ("tx 1 of 2 — approving"). Maps directly onto Noether's multi-signature open→SL→TP pipeline and 5s ledger latency.
- **Pre-trade previews are the risk display**: estimated liq price on the order form ([Bitget](https://www.bitget.com/support/articles/12560603808759), KuCoin), full order cost = initial margin + open fee + close fee ([Bybit Order Cost](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/Order-Cost-USDT-Contract)), post-trade account impact including the pending order ([dYdX margining](https://docs.dydx.xyz/concepts/trading/margin)). Previews must be computed against the price the engine executes on — for Noether the Noeracle mark, which OrderPanel already threads through as `markPrice`.
- **Keyboard-first entry is fill-then-confirm**: hotkeys stage parameters ([Binance desktop shortcuts](https://www.binance.com/en/support/faq/a-complete-guide-to-desktop-app-keyboard-shortcuts-a54fe927dee14a30a0762c96517b0e8b), F1/F2), modifier+arrows nudge price/qty, a submit chord opens a lightweight Enter/Esc confirm (IBKR TWS, Trading Technologies, tastytrade all treat hotkeys as configurable, off by default). "Pros want zero confirmation" is **folklore** — they want zero mouse and configurable confirms.
- **Slippage needs oracle-window semantics.** Expose an editable default as an accelerator (ethereum.org) with auto mode ([Uniswap auto-slippage 0.1–5%](https://blog.uniswap.org/minimize-slippage-on-swaps)). On an oracle-priced venue, slippage means "max oracle move I accept between signing and the ~5s fill" — no CEX-trained user will guess this; Hyperliquid discloses a fixed 10% tolerance on TP/SL market orders rather than hiding it.

**Pattern library**

| Pattern | Who uses it | How Noether applies it |
|---|---|---|
| **Dual-denomination size input** — size in USD notional or asset qty via unit toggle; collateral becomes a derived line | Bybit, dYdX, Hyperliquid, Binance Futures | Rework "Pay (Collateral)" in web/components/trading/OrderPanel.tsx (~line 749): Size becomes the headline input with a USDC/asset chip (per-asset decimals trivial with 3 pairs: 5dp BTC, 4dp ETH, 0dp XLM); show "Collateral required: X USDC" + "Qty: 0.0091 BTC" as derived lines. Oracle-priced fills make USD notional exact (no depth uncertainty) — tooltip-worthy. Keep positionSize = collateral × leverage math; just invert which variable the user types. |
| **Slider + numeric entry + preset pills + per-market memory** | Hyperliquid (per-asset leverage dialog, persisted), Binance, Bybit; [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/gui-slider-controls/) | OrderPanel slider (lines 793–841, min 1 max 10 step 1) + [1,2,5,8,10] pills is fine at 10x; add an editable "x" numeric field now — mandatory at mainnet 25x (a 25-stop slider is un-draggable). Persist leverage per asset in tradeStore (currently one global unpersisted value), so 2x BTC and 5x XLM survive switches and reloads. |
| **Conservative default leverage with risk-graded feedback that avoids the brand hue** | Regulatory evidence ([NBER w24176](https://www.nber.org/papers/w24176)); dYdX/GMX default low; OKX/Bitcoin.com guides converge on 2–3x starting leverage | liquidationRisk thresholds (≥5 amber, ≥8 red; OrderPanel lines 215/804) are directionally right, but amber #f59e0b "medium risk" is nearly the brand primary on near-black — medium risk reads as "branded normal." Reserve gold for interactive/brand accents; encode risk as neutral → orange-red → red + AlertTriangle icon + text label. Default tradeStore leverage to 2x on first visit; at 25x mainnet, gate >10x behind a one-time acknowledgement, don't raise the default. |
| **TP/SL attach-on-open with ±% / ROI shortcuts and estimated-PnL echo** | [Bybit](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/How-to-Set-Up-and-Modify-TP-SL-Perpetual-Futures-Contracts), [Hyperliquid](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/take-profit-and-stop-loss-orders-tp-sl) (parent-order OCO), Binance; how-to: [OneKey](https://onekey.so/blog/ecosystem/stop-loss-take-profit-hyperliquid/) | Extend the P4-17 attach fields (attachSl/attachTp, OrderPanel lines 97–99) with ±% pills and a live est-PnL line from the same Noeracle markPrice prop as the liq preview. Critically: Noether pipelines follow-up signatures after the open confirms (isolated+market only; contract rejects SL/TP on cross, #80) — the UI must say "3 signatures: open, SL, TP" up front and show which step is in flight, or traders assume Hyperliquid-style atomic OCO, abandon mid-pipeline, and run naked positions. Long term, the Noether Router's verify-then-trade is the path to single-signature attach. |
| **Order-form liq preview with distance-to-liquidation %** — "Liq. $88,450 · −9.1% from mark", live with size/leverage/direction | [Bitget](https://www.bitget.com/support/articles/12560603808759), KuCoin, Binance/Bybit order forms, Gainium-class calculators | OrderPanel already computes liquidationPrice from Noeracle markPrice (lines 171–177; the code comment correctly insists on the execution source, not Binance). Add the distance-% line; verify calculateLiquidationPrice in web/lib/utils uses the contract's actual maintenance margin (100 bps per CLAUDE.md — "NOT 5%") and funding-inclusive equity for cross. Surface the same figure in MobileTradeBar. |
| **Order-cost breakdown before submit** — margin + open fee + reserved close fee + gas | [Bybit Order Cost](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/Order-Cost-USDT-Contract), dYdX, [ethereum.org](https://ethereum.org/developers/docs/design-and-ux/dex-design-best-practice/) | The fee-tier preview (projectedFee useMemo, lines 182–209: tier name, deci-bps, fee amount, next-tier volume) is already ahead of most DEXes — wrap it in an "Order cost" summary adding collateral + fee and a one-line XLM gas note (validation already requires xlmBalance ≥ 1). Tiers use deci-bps (FEE_PRECISION 100_000): always render both "%" and USDC — "0.050% · $0.50" — pros sanity-check bps, newcomers read dollars. |
| **CTA-as-state-machine** — the submit button names the blocker | Uniswap, [ethereum.org](https://ethereum.org/developers/docs/design-and-ux/dex-design-best-practice/), virtually all EVM DEX front-ends | OrderPanel builds an errors[] array (lines 218–243) gating canSubmit; render the FIRST blocker as the button label and the rest inline next to their fields, not a stacked list. A disabled gold button must keep ≥3:1 label contrast on the near-black panel (WCAG 1.4.3/1.4.11 borderline for dimmed #f59e0b) — prefer filled-but-desaturated with explicit reason over opacity-50. When valid: "Long $1,000 BTC-PERP". |
| **Validate on blur, clear on keystroke, confirm positively** | [Baymard](https://baymard.com/blog/inline-form-validation); [NN/g error guidelines](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/errors-forms-design-guidelines/) | Errors currently derive synchronously on every render — typing "1" toward "100" instantly trips "Minimum collateral is 10 USDC" (line 225 guards collateralNum > 0 && < 10, firing mid-entry). Track touched/blurred per field: suppress min-collateral and trigger-price errors until blur; keep hard ceilings (insufficient balance, $100k position cap) live since they inform sizing. Clear on keystroke. A subtle gold check on a valid TP/SL price fits brand + Baymard's positive-validation finding. |
| **Optional confirm modal, default-on, remembered skip — wallet signature is the final confirm** | Binance (setting + Enter/Esc), Hyperliquid (skip-confirm), [GMX 1CT](https://gmxio.substack.com/p/one-click-trading-is-now-available); [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/confirmation-dialog/) | Do NOT add a mandatory in-app modal on top of Freighter/LOBSTR. Make the in-panel Order Summary the review surface; offer Settings → "Order confirmation: on/off" in localStorage, default ON for Stellar-native newcomers. True GMX-style one-click needs delegated/session signing stellar-wallets-kit doesn't provide — mark "future", don't fake it. Honest framing: "one signature, ~5 seconds", stated in the confirmation copy. |
| **Percent pills sized against actually-spendable balance** (fees, gas, margin-mode aware) | dYdX (max size from remaining free collateral), Uniswap-style Max buttons, every major perp venue | Pills (lines 780–790, handlePercentage over effectiveUsdcBalance) should: (a) at 100%, reserve the projected open fee — fee is charged on notional, so Max solves c = balance − c·L·feeBps/100000 — so validation never instantly flags "Insufficient USDC"; (b) in Cross mode compute from free margin (Equity − Used, per CrossMarginBanner), not wallet USDC; (c) Leader mode from vault balance (already via effectiveUsdcBalance). Surface the $100k cap and 10-USDC min as pill-time constraints, not post-hoc errors. |
| **Post-trade account-impact preview for cross margin** — margin usage before → after | [dYdX](https://docs.dydx.xyz/concepts/trading/margin) (buying power incl. pending order), Bybit, Binance | When marginMode === 'Cross', add two summary rows: "Free margin: $412 → $312", "Margin ratio after: 14%", from the CrossMarginBanner formulas (Equity = pool + collateral + uPnL; Free = Equity − Used) plus this order's collateral. De-risks the cross-liquidation path (highest-supervision area per CLAUDE.md) by making over-commitment visible pre-signature; reuses existing reads (getCrossMarginBalance, positions prop). |
| **Keyboard-first grammar** — focus shortcuts, arrow steppers, Enter-to-submit, Esc-out | [Binance desktop](https://www.binance.com/en/support/faq/a-complete-guide-to-desktop-app-keyboard-shortcuts-a54fe927dee14a30a0762c96517b0e8b), IBKR TWS, Trading Technologies, tastytrade; [NN/g steppers](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/input-steppers/) | ArrowUp/Down steps on collateral (±10 USDC), leverage (±1x), trigger price (tick-aware: $10 BTC, $1 ETH, $0.001 XLM); "B"/"S" or 1/2 flips Long/Short when no input focused; Enter submits when canSubmit (wallet sheet is the physical confirm); Esc clears focus. Inert while any modal/wallet sheet is open; behind a Settings toggle (Binance-style opt-in). Inputs are already inputMode=decimal, right-aligned mono with a gold focus ring (focus:ring-primary) — the one WCAG 2.4.7 requirement the brand already nails; extend the ring to pills and slider thumb. |
| **Progressive disclosure of advanced controls, persistent expansion** | Robinhood Legend, dYdX, [Lollypop 2026 guide](https://lollypop.design/blog/2026/june/trading-app-design/); Hyperliquid keeps TIF in a compact dropdown ([order types](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/order-types)) | Fold TIF (GTC/IOC/PostOnly) + reduce-only into an "Advanced" collapsible (default closed, expansion persisted in localStorage — pro-first means respect the opened state forever). Demote "Trail Stop" from the 4-tab order-type row: it operates on EXISTING positions, so it belongs on the PositionsList row (Hyperliquid-style), freeing tab width on the 3-pair layout and in MobileTradeBar. |
| **Staged, latency-honest submit progress** | GMX, dYdX, Uniswap multi-step; [Lollypop](https://lollypop.design/blog/2026/june/trading-app-design/); [ethereum.org](https://ethereum.org/developers/docs/design-and-ux/dex-design-best-practice/) "tx 1 of 2" | Replace the single isSubmitting spinner with staged CTA/toast copy: "1/1 Sign in wallet" → "Submitting… ~5s" → "Position opened"; for TP/SL-attached opens "1/3 open · 2/3 stop-loss · 3/3 take-profit", matching the pipelined signatures in attachTpSlAfterOpen (lines 252–293). Optimistic "pending" rows in OrdersList/PositionsList until the ledger confirms. Never fake instant fills — pros watch the wallet and the chain; the credible story is honesty about the 5s window plus the router's verify-then-trade freshness guarantee. |
| **Slippage as an explained accelerator with a safe default** (oracle-window semantics) | [Uniswap auto-slippage](https://blog.uniswap.org/minimize-slippage-on-swaps), Hyperliquid (documented 10% TP/SL tolerance), ethereum.org | slippageTolerance (default 50 bps, preset+custom, surfaced as "Max entry price" at line 1018) is structurally right; add a tooltip defining it in ORACLE terms — "max Noeracle mark move between your signature and the on-chain fill (~5s)" — because CEX users think in book-depth slippage that doesn't exist here. Validate the custom field on blur (currently 0–10000 bps checked only at submit); consider per-pair auto defaults (XLM moves more per 5s window in % terms than BTC). |

**Anti-patterns**

- Collateral-only sizing ("Pay 100 USDC at 5x") — every reference venue sizes by notional/qty; the margin-vs-notional confusion literature says traders "blow up because they misjudge exposure." Noether's current OrderPanel is on the wrong side of this convention.
- A mandatory in-app confirm modal stacked on the wallet signature — [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/confirmation-dialog/): routine confirms get automated away ("the computer that cried confirm") and fail exactly when needed.
- Showing "Minimum collateral is 10 USDC" while the user is still typing "1…00" — the live-derived errors[] does this today; Baymard participants described it as being "yelled at."
- A naked leverage slider at 25x (mainnet plan) with no numeric input — precision-dragging a 25-stop slider is a motor-skill test, worse on mobile (MobileTradeBar).
- Defaulting to high leverage, or one global leverage value across assets — a 10x default carried from BTC into an XLM trade is silent risk escalation (Heimer & Simsek: caps cut losses 40%).
- Silently disabled submit with reasons in a separate stacked list — NN/g guideline 8 (never a summary alone); web3 convention is the CTA names the blocker.
- Max/100% pill computed from raw wallet balance — instantly trips "Insufficient USDC" once the fee is added; in Cross mode overstates capacity (use free margin).
- Amber as a bare "medium risk" hue inside a gold/amber brand — warning reads as brand accent. **Unified risk-color rule (used consistently across this document):** amber may mark the *Caution* status band only when paired with an icon + text label (never hue alone); continuous risk *ramps* adjacent to gold interactive controls — the leverage slider — skip amber entirely (neutral → orange-red → red); and risk is never color-only (~8% of men have CVD).
- Hiding the multi-signature reality of TP/SL attach — presented as one action (as on Hyperliquid, where it IS atomic OCO), users abandon after signature 1 and unknowingly run naked positions. State the signature count before submit.
- Promising "one-click trading" without delegated/session signing — GMX 1CT works via local sub-accounts; Stellar wallets in this stack cannot skip the signing sheet. **Folklore flag:** "pros demand zero confirmations" — Binance's own hotkey flow ends in an Enter/Esc popup; pros want zero mouse, not zero deliberation.
- Liq preview computed from a different price/margin model than the engine — e.g. off the Binance ticker or a 5% maintenance margin when the contract liquidates against the Noeracle mark at 100 bps (documented Noether pitfall). Worse than no preview.
- Tooltip-only errors and top-of-ticket validation summaries — in a 320px panel, errors must sit adjacent to their field (NN/g guidelines 8/9).
- **Folklore flag:** "Binance defaults new futures accounts to 20x" is widely repeated but was not confirmed in a primary Binance source — treat as anecdotal; the evidenced principle is simply "default low, let users opt up."

---

### Position & Liquidation-Risk Display

**Key findings**

- **Liquidation price is a first-class position-row column everywhere** — even under cross margin, where an ESTIMATED per-position figure is derived from account equity rather than hidden ([Hyperliquid liquidations](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/liquidations), [Bybit position tabs](https://help.bybit.com/hc/en-us/articles/4409329816473-How-to-navigate-Bybit-s-perpetual-futures-contract-trading-page), Binance, Crypto Facilities). Noether's PositionsList.tsx renders no per-position liq price for cross positions (verified: comment "Cross-margin positions don't have per-position liq price" at line 163; `return false` at 165) — off-convention.
- **Liq price on fee-accruing perps is dynamic.** [GMX](https://docs.gmx.io/docs/trading/v2/) warns borrowing/funding fees move the liq price and tells >10x users to monitor it ([GMX liquidations](https://docs.gmx.io/docs/trading/liquidations/)); [Jupiter](https://support.jup.ag/hc/en-us/articles/18735045234588-What-are-the-fees-associated-with-Jupiter-Perps) documents continuously accruing borrow fees raising liq price ([Jupiter liquidation](https://support.jup.ag/hc/en-us/articles/18431068922908-Liquidation)). Any static display on Noether (lazy hourly funding) understates risk over time.
- **Margin health = one normalized scalar with named bands.** [Drift](https://docs.drift.trade/trading/account-health): Health = 1 − maintenance-margin/collateral, liquidation at 0, yellow bar when initial margin exhausted. [Binance](https://www.binance.com/en/support/faq/how-to-reduce-your-chances-of-getting-liquidated-25edcd7fe0e544839d0847b8cbb2e400) liquidates at margin ratio 100%, advises staying under 80%; [OKX](https://www.okx.com/en-us/help/v-tiered-maintenance-margin-ratio-rules) alerts around 300% margin level. Numbers differ; the structure — safe band → warning band with proactive notification → liquidation band — is constant.
- **ROE%-on-margin is the convention but a documented confusion source.** [Bybit P&L calc](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/Profit-Loss-calculations-USDT-Contract) shows uPnL $ with ROI% in brackets (last-price basis by default, mark-price on hover); [Binance Square](https://www.binance.com/en/square/post/14186989026657) documents "+82.63% ROI on $1.65 of profit" at high leverage. Best practice: $ and % together, denominator explicit, price basis discoverable.
- **uPnL that silently excludes fees/funding is a known support-ticket generator** — Bybit maintains a dedicated article, ["Why Is My Closed P&L a Loss When My Unrealized Profit Was Positive?"](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/Why-Closed-PL-Loss-When-Unrealized-Profit-Positive). [Hyperliquid](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/funding) heads it off with a per-position cumulative Funding figure since open; HL PnL tooling standardizes gross PnL − funding = net PnL ([entry price & PnL](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/entry-price-and-pnl)).
- **Liquidation warnings follow an escalation ladder** mapping to [NN/g's indicators→notifications framework](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/indicators-validations-notifications/). [Binance](https://www.binance.com/en/support/faq/detail/360033525271) sends margin-call before liquidation-call notices and auto-cancels the former if the two would be too close — an explicit anti-spam rule; [Binance Portfolio Margin](https://www.binance.com/en/support/faq/liquidation-alert-and-auto-top-up-function-in-binance-portfolio-margin-d32047c9e6ec4d7daaac07620212f409) lets users set their own alert ratio; dYdX's mobile app shows risk levels throughout; the [dYdX-funded alert tool](https://www.dydxgrants.com/grants/liquidation-alert-tool) triggers on distance-to-liq % — the user-facing alert unit is % price move, not margin ratio.
- **Off-app channels cannot carry the safety load**: Binance explicitly disclaims guaranteed timely delivery of margin-call emails. On a 5s-ledger chain where a keeper liquidates in one transaction, only in-app real-time indicators are fast enough.
- **WCAG 1.4.1 (Level A) forbids color-only meaning** ([W3C](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG21/Understanding/use-of-color.html)); ~1 in 12 men have CVD, and red/green PnL plus green/yellow/red health is exactly the flagged scenario. [HRT's trading-UX practice](https://www.hudsonrivertrading.com/hrtbeat/optimizing-ux-ui-design-for-trading/) contrast-checks every status color against the actual background — their green success indicators failed on some backgrounds until checked.
- **PnL share cards have a standardized anatomy**: share icon beside the PnL(ROE) cell ([Dappgrid on Binance](https://dappgrid.com/share-binance-futures-profit-pnl/)), user selects included fields (ROI% default, $ opt-in), templates/stickers/export ([Tealstreet](https://docs.tealstreet.io/docs/trade/pnl-cards)), account-level summary cards ([Bybit](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/How-to-Generate-your-Trade-Summary-Card)), embedded referral QR — an acquisition channel, not just vanity.
- **Fast-updating numbers need tabular numerals**: `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/Properties/font-variant-numeric)) gives fixed-width digits so live prices don't jitter; preferred over monospace hacks ([DEV comparison](https://dev.to/alanwest/tabular-numbers-in-css-font-variant-numeric-vs-monospace-hacks-25cn)). [dYdX's design blog](https://www.dydx.xyz/blog/trading-interface-evolution) separately documents number-overflow shorthand ($5.2k) and before/after previews as trust-building details.
- **dYdX's published interface principles** — persistent account summaries, before/after comparisons, "design for power users without alienating first-timers", slider+numeric pairing — are the closest thing to primary design research from a perp DEX, aligned with [NN/g progressive disclosure](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/progressive-disclosure/) and [dYdX margining docs](https://docs.dydx.xyz/concepts/trading/margin).
- **Noether currently ships two conflicting health metrics**: CrossMarginBanner.tsx uses marginRatio = equity/maintenanceMargin (Infinity=healthy, >300% healthy, >100% warning) while portfolio AccountHealth.tsx uses marginUsage = collateral/netWorth with color breaks at 50/80 but text labels breaking at 30/60 — three inconsistencies (metric, direction, thresholds-vs-labels) a Drift/Binance-trained pro will read as the product not knowing its own risk math.

**Pattern library**

| Pattern | Who uses it | How Noether applies it |
|---|---|---|
| **Liq price as a first-class, dynamic column labeled with its price basis** ("Liq. (oracle est.)") | [GMX v2](https://docs.gmx.io/docs/trading/v2/), [Hyperliquid](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/liquidations), Binance, Bybit, Crypto Facilities | PositionsList.tsx already renders liquidationPrice per row — keep, but recompute client-side each 5s oracle tick folding in estimated accrued lazy funding (global rate × hours open), since on-chain liq uses the same Noeracle mark the keeper relays. Tooltip: "Liquidation executes at the on-chain oracle price, not the chart price." 3 pairs at 1% maintenance margin makes per-tick recompute cheap. Muted text normally; escalate only as distance shrinks. |
| **Distance-to-liquidation % as the human-readable risk unit** ("−8.3% to liq") | [dYdX grants alert-tool spec](https://www.dydxgrants.com/grants/liquidation-alert-tool) (trigger = % price move from liq); [MetaMask Learn](https://metamask.io/news/perpetual-futures-liquidation-mechanics) guidance | PositionsList.tsx already computes exactly this diff for its 10%-distance risk flag (lines 162–168, `diff < 0.1`) — promote the hidden boolean to a visible badge next to liq price. % distance is the only unit that reads consistently across BTC/ETH/XLM. Use it as the warning-ladder trigger (e.g. <15% caution, <7% critical) instead of margin ratio, which pros must mentally invert. |
| **One canonical account-health scalar, three named bands** (Drift-style: 100% safe → 0% liquidation) | [Drift](https://docs.drift.trade/trading/account-health), [Binance margin ratio](https://www.binance.com/en/support/faq/how-to-reduce-your-chances-of-getting-liquidated-25edcd7fe0e544839d0847b8cbb2e400), [OKX margin level](https://www.okx.com/en-us/help/v-tiered-maintenance-margin-ratio-rules) | Unify CrossMarginBanner.tsx (equity/maintenanceMargin, healthy >300%) and AccountHealth.tsx (collateral/netWorth, 50/80 colors vs 30/60 labels). Pick the 0–100% health form, compute in ONE shared helper in web/lib (same cross-equity calc as the contract, including pending lazy funding), consume in both components. Fix direction: today the portfolio bar fills toward danger while its label says "Safe" — one scalar means the bar always drains as risk rises. Bands: Safe / Caution / At Risk, each icon + label, never color alone. |
| **Three-stage warning ladder**: always-on indicator → passive toast on band entry → persistent blocking banner with inline "Add margin"/"Reduce position" | [Binance](https://www.binance.com/en/support/faq/detail/360033525271) (margin call → liquidation call + suppression rule), [Binance PM custom alert ratio](https://www.binance.com/en/support/faq/liquidation-alert-and-auto-top-up-function-in-binance-portfolio-margin-d32047c9e6ec4d7daaac07620212f409), dYdX mobile, [NN/g framework](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/indicators-validations-notifications/) | The crisis window is short (keeper liquidates via the router in one 5s tx once the mark crosses), so the ladder must be in-app and real-time: drive it from the Noeracle SSE stream (web/lib/stellar/noeracle.ts), not polling; skip email for v1 (Binance's own disclaimer shows off-app delivery can't be trusted). Stage-3 banner above PositionsList on /trade AND global on /portfolio and /vaults. Respect suppression: if distance collapses through both thresholds in one tick, show only the critical alert. Brand amber = Caution only when paired with an icon + text label (the unified risk-color rule in this chapter); red reserved solely for At Risk. |
| **Cross vs isolated: per-row mode+leverage badge + estimated cross liq price** ("est. (cross)") | Binance/Bybit (clickable mode badge per row), [Hyperliquid margining](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/margining); explainer: [MetaMask cross-vs-isolated](https://metamask.io/news/cross-vs-isolated-margin-perps) | Position.margin_mode is u32 0/1; PositionsList already shows an amber CROSS chip (lines 797–798) but no cross liq estimate (and SL/TP buttons are disabled for cross — M-3 interim guard, lines 769–865). Compute est. cross liq client-side from the aggregates CrossMarginBanner.tsx already builds (equity, total maintenance margin). Tooltip: "Cross positions share USDC collateral; liquidation triggers when account equity < maintenance margin (1%). This price assumes other positions' PnL is frozen." Badge click scrolls to the banner — progressive disclosure per [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/progressive-disclosure/) and dYdX's stated principle. |
| **uPnL as $ primary + ROE% secondary, denominator and price basis explicit** | [Bybit](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/Profit-Loss-calculations-USDT-Contract), Binance PNL(ROE%), [Hyperliquid](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/entry-price-and-pnl) | PositionsList already pairs formatUSD(pnl) with formatPercent(pnlPercent) — keep, add a tooltip stating the denominator ("ROE = uPnL / position margin"), the basis ("at oracle mark" — there is no last-traded price; no order book), and exclusions ("excludes fees and accrued funding — settled on close"). Always render an explicit "+" for gains. 10x makes ROI confusion milder than Binance's 75x case, but 25x mainnet is planned — bake labeling in now. Verify green/red pass contrast on near-black (HRT practice) and rely on the sign character for WCAG 1.4.1. |
| **Per-position "Funding accrued since open" + net-PnL preview at close** | [Hyperliquid funding](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/funding), [Jupiter](https://support.jup.ag/hc/en-us/articles/18431068922908-Liquidation) (fees raise liq price), [GMX](https://docs.gmx.io/docs/trading/liquidations/), [Bybit](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/Why-Closed-PL-Loss-When-Unrealized-Profit-Positive) | Noether's funding is lazy — nothing accrues on-chain until close/liquidate — which makes the display MORE necessary: users see zero deductions until the close suddenly includes hours of funding. Compute estimated accrual client-side from the stored global rate and position age (same math as apply_funding_to_position); show in an expandable row section on /trade and /portfolio and inject into the Close modal in PositionsList.tsx (today it shows only price PnL). Label "est. — settled on close." The trade-page funding-rate stat is now a real read (verified July 2026); the prerequisite that remains is its failure fallback — render "—", never +0.0000% — so the accrual display can never key off a fabricated rate. |
| **Partial close via 25/50/75/100% chips riding the reduce-only path** | [Bybit](https://help.bybit.com/hc/en-us/articles/4409329816473-How-to-navigate-Bybit-s-perpetual-futures-contract-trading-page) (Close-by-Limit/Market with qty, partial TP/SL), Hyperliquid (fractional close orders), Binance | Confirmed against the contract: `close_position` takes **no size argument** today (partial close was removed for WASM budget), so 25/50/75% chips require the C2 WASM-refit reinstatement. Until then ship a zero-typing 100% close with a "positions close in full on Noether testnet" line in the modal — never promise partial parity the chain can’t honor. Put chips in the existing Close modal (PositionsList.tsx handleClose); with 5s finality show staged feedback (signing → submitted → confirmed) and keep the button disabled through confirmation (the "Closing..." state must span the full ledger wait). After partial close, optimistically render reduced size with a "pending" shimmer until the event confirms. |
| **Before/after preview in every margin-affecting modal** (equity, health %, liq price, margin freed: Now → After) | [dYdX](https://www.dydx.xyz/blog/trading-interface-evolution) ("drastically decreasing time to confirm trade details"), [GMX v2](https://docs.gmx.io/docs/trading/v2/) edit-collateral, [Drift](https://docs.drift.trade/trading/account-health) | Perfect fit for 5s latency: the user waits anyway, so spend the pre-signature moment on comprehension. Add the delta table to the Close modal and cross-margin deposit/withdraw flows. Values come from the same shared health helper as the banner, so "After" is consistent everywhere. Pro-first: exact 7-decimal-derived numbers at the pair's display precision, raw value on hover. |
| **PnL share card: %-first privacy default, brand canvas, referral QR** | [Binance field selection](https://dappgrid.com/share-binance-futures-profit-pnl/), [Bybit Trade Summary Card](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/How-to-Generate-your-Trade-Summary-Card), [Tealstreet](https://docs.tealstreet.io/docs/trade/pnl-cards) | Audit the existing PnlShareCard.tsx / PnlShareModal.tsx against this anatomy: (1) ROE%-only by default, $ opt-in; (2) render the fixed brand — near-black canvas, gold accents, NOETHER slashed-O wordmark, math-glyph border strip (the card is the brand's most-traveled artifact); (3) embed the user's on-chain referral code as QR + ?ref=CODE link, closing the loop with /referrals and the sticky ref banner; (4) "testnet" watermark until mainnet. Long/short and leverage as text badges, not color-only (WCAG 1.4.1). |
| **Risk states never encoded by hue alone** (gold-on-dark discipline) | [W3C WCAG 1.4.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG21/Understanding/use-of-color.html) (normative), [HRT](https://www.hudsonrivertrading.com/hrtbeat/optimizing-ux-ui-design-for-trading/), Drift (bar + numeric % + label) | Brand amber ≈ conventional warning yellow, so hue alone cannot distinguish "brand accent" from "caution." Caution = amber + triangle icon + "Caution" label + filled banner; At Risk = red + octagon/alert icon + label + persistent border. CrossMarginBanner.tsx already pairs a Shield icon with a label — extend to the AccountHealth bar (fix the 30/60-vs-50/80 label/color mismatch to shared bands) and to green/red PnL cells (always print +/−). Run actual hexes against near-black; target AA on all status text. |
| **Tabular numerals + fixed per-pair precision for every live figure** | Financial-UI standard ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/Properties/font-variant-numeric), [DEV](https://dev.to/alanwest/tabular-numbers-in-css-font-variant-numeric-vs-monospace-hacks-25cn)); dYdX (overflow shorthand as designed edge case) | Numbers tick on every Noeracle SSE update and 5s ledger — apply Tailwind `tabular-nums` to PositionsList rows, CrossMarginBanner stats, the header mark price, and close-modal deltas. Fix display precision per pair (BTC-PERP 2dp, ETH-PERP 2dp, XLM-PERP 4–5dp) from the 7-decimal contract values in one formatter in web/lib/utils so liq/entry/mark align vertically. Cheapest pro-credibility win available before mainnet. |

**Anti-patterns**

- Two different "health" definitions in one product — Noether today (trade page: equity/maintenanceMargin, higher=better, 300%/100% bands; portfolio: collateral/netWorth, higher=worse, 50/80 colors vs 30/60 labels). Three simultaneous inconsistencies.
- Hiding liq price for cross positions instead of an account-level estimate — no major venue does this.
- Static liq price on a fee-accruing perp — with lazy funding the true liq price drifts while the shown number freezes ([GMX](https://docs.gmx.io/docs/trading/liquidations/), [Jupiter](https://support.jup.ag/hc/en-us/articles/18735045234588-What-are-the-fees-associated-with-Jupiter-Perps) both warn users).
- %-only PnL (or %-first without denominator) — produces the Binance-documented "+82.63% ROI = $1.65" confusion; always pair $ and %, name the denominator (margin, not notional).
- uPnL that silently excludes fees/funding, then a surprising realized number at close — Bybit needed a dedicated help article; lazy funding makes Noether's surprise bigger.
- Email/push as the primary liquidation-warning channel — Binance disclaims timely delivery and cancels margin-call notices when liquidation is imminent; on 5s ledgers only in-app indicators are fast enough.
- Warning spam / dueling alerts when price gaps through both thresholds — Binance's suppression rule (drop the milder notice) is correct.
- Color as the sole risk/PnL encoding — WCAG 1.4.1 Level A failure; especially dangerous where brand amber ≈ warning amber; require icon+label+sign redundancy.
- **Folklore flag:** the "color reduces cognitive load by 55%" figure attributed to NN/g in dashboard blogs has no NN/g source. Actual NN/g guidance is qualitative: few status colors, redundant encoding, preattentive contrast.
- **Folklore flag:** "keep ≥5% distance to liquidation" ([MetaMask Learn](https://metamask.io/news/perpetual-futures-liquidation-mechanics) and similar) is guidance folklore — fine as a default alert threshold, wrong as a hard-coded "you are safe" judgment in UI copy.
- Shipping risk UI next to fake numbers — the trade page currently hardcodes funding rate and market stats (known issue); a pro who catches one fabricated stat distrusts the liq price and health meter too.
- Fake urgency: continuously flashing/pulsing a row in the caution band trains users to ignore it — passive states stay calm; reserve motion for transitions into At Risk ([NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/indicators-validations-notifications/)).

---

### Onboarding & Activation

**Key findings**

- **The first on-chain transaction is the steepest funnel cliff**: benchmark drop-offs are 15–30% at wallet connect and 35–50% at first transaction/deposit ([Formo funnel benchmarks](https://formo.so/blog/web3-analytics-onchain-funnel-analysis)). Users who transact within 24h show ~3x higher retention; best-in-class targets: >70% onboarding completion, time-to-first-transaction under 10 minutes (vendor-reported — directional).
- **Front-loaded tutorials don't work.** [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/onboarding-tutorials/): onboarding tutorials do not improve task performance, are skipped and forgotten ("paradox of the active user"); contextual pull-based help at the moment of need is more effective. For pro-first Noether: no welcome tour, no carousel — invest in contextual affordances, teaching empty states, an optional checklist.
- **NN/g's three empty-state guidelines** — communicate true status, provide learning cues, provide a direct pathway ([NN/g empty states](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/empty-state-interface-design/)) — map onto verified Noether gaps: OrderPanel.tsx fires `toast.error('Insufficient USDC balance')` at line 522 with no faucet link (grep-verified: zero "faucet" references in OrderPanel.tsx or app/trade/page.tsx). (CLAUDE.md’s long-documented portfolio USDC-always-0 bug was re-verified as fixed in the same-day audit — the missing faucet link is the live violation.)
- **Checklists are the best-evidenced guided-first-action pattern**: 3–5 items ([Kompassify](https://kompassify.com/blog/how-to-create-a-user-onboarding-checklist)), quick win first (configuration-first sequences get abandoned), persistent placement beats modal interruption; completion rate is a primary onboarding metric ([Appcues](https://www.appcues.com/blog/user-onboarding-ui-ux-patterns); Chameleon benchmarks: 27% CTR on welcome-opened checklists, 67% completion of checklist-launched tours — vendor data).
- **Demo trading is table stakes** for this audience: Bybit auto-provisions a demo account with virtual 50K USDT + 50K USDC + 1 BTC + 1 ETH ([Bybit demo FAQ](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/FAQ-Demo-Trading)); Binance mock trading gives 3,000 virtual USDT; Hyperliquid runs a full parallel testnet app with a 1,000 mock-USDC faucet gated on prior mainnet deposit ([HL faucet](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/onboarding/testnet-faucet)). [Alpaca reports 75% of demo users convert to live within 60 days](https://tradeinformer.com/broker-news/75-of-this-brokers-demo-traders-convert-to-live-accounts-in-less-than-two-months) — broker-published, selection-biased; and "paper trading builds transferable skill" is partially **folklore** (demo omits slippage, fees, emotional stakes).
- **Signing friction is the defining perp-DEX UX gap**: Hyperliquid's session-key/agent-wallet model (one ApproveAgent signature, then zero per-order prompts) is repeatedly cited as its biggest UX edge over dYdX's sign-per-action ([Eco comparison](https://eco.com/support/en/articles/15039717-hyperliquid-vs-dydx-fees-liquidity-ux-compared)). Noether's every-action-is-a-tx at ~5s makes prompt-count-per-first-trade a critical number.
- **Activation = first trade executed**, not wallet connect or funding: 20–30% of trading-app users complete verification but never trade; winners convert 40–55% of signups to a first trade within 48h ([ProductGrowth playbook](https://productgrowth.in/insights/fintech/trading-app-onboarding/)). A no-KYC testnet with a faucet should target minutes.
- **Wallet-connect modals that educate convert better**: [RainbowKit](https://rainbowkit.com/en-US/docs/introduction) ships a "What is a wallet?" section, per-wallet install links, wallet detection, a separate mobile flow (see also [Pedro Duarte's connect-button teardown](https://ped.ro/writing/connect-wallet-button-but-better)). Tooltip research: ≤140 characters, never task-essential info in tooltips; pair jargon with plain-language definitions the way [Robinhood Learn](https://newsroom.aboutrobinhood.com/learn-and-grow-with-robinhood/) does.
- **First-transaction anxiety is measurable**: pre-signing previews (fees, liq price, margin), multi-step status trackers with time estimates, and explicit success confirmation reduce confirm-step abandonment — "transaction abandonment at confirmation" is a tracked web3 metric ([UXCam fintech KPIs](https://uxcam.com/blog/measure-fintech-app-onboarding-kpis/), [Formo dApp analytics](https://formo.so/blog/what-is-dapp-analytics-and-why-does-it-matter-in-web3)).

**Pattern library**

| Pattern | Who uses it | How Noether applies it |
|---|---|---|
| **Order-panel CTA ladder: Connect → Fund → Trade** — the ticket button morphs by user state; insufficient states are themselves CTAs | Hyperliquid ("Enable Trading"/"Deposit" ticket gating — [how to start](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/onboarding/how-to-start-trading)), dYdX, GMX; codified by [NN/g empty states](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/empty-state-interface-design/) | Verified gap: OrderPanel.tsx line 522 fires toast.error('Insufficient USDC balance'); no faucet reference anywhere on the trade surface. Replace the zero-balance state with an amber primary button "Get 1,000 testnet USDC" that routes to /faucet with return-to-trade — or better, drips inline. Reserve the gold fill for the single next-required step so the ladder reads as one guided path. |
| **In-flow faucet drip** — faucet as a step, not a destination | [Hyperliquid testnet /drip](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/onboarding/testnet-faucet), [Circle faucet](https://faucet.circle.com/), [Chainlink faucets](https://chain.link/faucets) | web/app/faucet/page.tsx (122 lines) exists but is orphaned from /trade. Embed a compact drip module in the OrderPanel zero-balance state and the /portfolio zero-balance state; default drip well above the 10 USDC min collateral; render the ~5s close as an explicit inline step ("Ledger closing… ~5s"), not a spinner. Do NOT copy Hyperliquid's mainnet-deposit gate — right for their anti-sybil goals, wrong while Noether's goal is testnet activation. |
| **Persistent 3–5 step first-session checklist, quick win first** — connect → claim USDC → first position → TP/SL or limit order → review on portfolio | SaaS standard ([Appcues](https://www.appcues.com/blog/user-onboarding-ui-ux-patterns), [Kompassify](https://kompassify.com/blog/how-to-create-a-user-onboarding-checklist)); Binance/Bybit first-trade reward hubs | Dock bottom-right on /trade above the positions tab — never a modal tour ([NN/g mobile onboarding](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/mobile-app-onboarding/) backs skipping front-loaded decks). Slim gold-bordered card, math-glyph strip as header rule. State in Zustand tradeStore + localStorage. Steps map 1:1 to the real funnel (stellar-wallets-kit connect → /faucet → OrderPanel market order → TP/SL in PositionsList → /portfolio). Pro-first: auto-collapse for wallets that already have on-chain positions; never block the ticket. |
| **Pull-based contextual help, no forced tour** — dotted-underline glossary terms, ≤140-char plain definitions, "Learn more" links | [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/onboarding-tutorials/); Appcues tooltip guidance; [Robinhood Learn](https://newsroom.aboutrobinhood.com/learn-and-grow-with-robinhood/); [IxDF progressive disclosure](https://ixdf.org/literature/topics/progressive-disclosure) | The correct disclosure layer for pro-first: Hyperliquid emigres ignore it, Stellar newcomers pull it. Apply to OrderPanel labels (margin mode, leverage, post-only/IOC), CrossMarginBanner, and the funding row — lazy hourly funding applied on close/liquidate genuinely differs from Binance's 8h cadence and deserves a one-line explainer. Muted gold dotted underline so glossary terms read as affordances without shouting. |
| **Teach the oracle model at the "Order Book" panel** — structural emptiness explained, not "no data" | [NN/g empty-state learning cues](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/empty-state-interface-design/); GMX (oracle-priced, no book) sets "no price impact / oracle price" expectations throughout | Noether's most dangerous misread: a Hyperliquid-habituated pro sees an empty OrderBook.tsx and concludes zero liquidity. Retitle "Your Orders"/"Open Orders"; empty state gets two lines — how Noeracle pricing works (fills at oracle price, 60s staleness guard, router verify-then-trade) + a "Place a limit order" pathway focusing the limit tab. Same treatment for RecentTrades if it only shows the user's own fills. |
| **Brand the testnet as the demo mode, with an unmissable environment badge** | [Bybit demo](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/FAQ-Demo-Trading), Binance mock trading, Hyperliquid separate testnet app | testnet.noether.exchange IS the demo tier — market it on the landing hero ("Trade the testnet — free USDC from the faucet") instead of hiding it. Slim amber-outline TESTNET chip beside the NOETHER wordmark on every page (not a full-width banner that eats chart space). Use [Alpaca's 75% demo→live stat](https://tradeinformer.com/broker-news/75-of-this-brokers-demo-traders-convert-to-live-accounts-in-less-than-two-months) as rationale, not forecast. |
| **Wallet modal that detects, educates, pre-empts known failures** | [RainbowKit](https://rainbowkit.com/en-US/docs/introduction), [Pedro Duarte](https://ped.ro/writing/connect-wallet-button-but-better), Reown/WalletConnect guidance | web/components/wallet/WalletModal.tsx (233 lines): detect Freighter and float it to the top; "Install Freighter →" when absent; collapsed "New to Stellar wallets?" education expander (invisible to pros); surface the documented LON-wallet incompatibility (fails Soroban auth signing) as an inline warning on that option, not a post-failure mystery. 15–30% connect drop-off justifies the design time. |
| **First-trade status ladder tuned to 5s finality** — Signed → Submitted → Ledger closing (~5s) → Position open, with plain-terms success moment | Blockchain-UX research on status trackers; Hyperliquid's sub-second feel is the expectation to manage against | Noether cannot hide the ~5s close, so narrate it: reuse OrderPanel's rich error mapping (InsufficientCollateral → "Minimum is 10 USDC", CrossMarginInsufficientFreeMargin) and add the positive-path stepper — the "Ledger closing" step can animate the math-glyph strip as a progress motif. The router bundles verify-then-trade in one tx, so one signature = one ladder; state "1 signature" up front to contrast with Ethereum multi-approve. Log a first_trade_confirmed event here. |
| **Plain-English trade receipt before signing** — margin, est. entry (oracle), liq price, fees in USDC and %, funding note; reactive | Robinhood (plain-English receipts), Hyperliquid ticket (liq/margin/fees inline) | OrderPanel already computes tier-aware fees reactively (0.02%/0.05% base, 4 tiers) — formalize into a bordered receipt block, amber accent on liquidation price (the number protecting a 10x-leveraged first-timer). Label the price row "Oracle price (Noeracle)" to reinforce the venue model at the moment of commitment. Maintenance margin (1%) and lazy funding as glossary-linked rows. Must live in the layout, never a tooltip. |
| **Instrumented activation funnel, time-to-first-trade as north star** — connect_modal_opened → wallet_connected → faucet_claimed → first_trade_submitted → first_trade_confirmed → checklist_step_completed | [Formo](https://formo.so/blog/web3-analytics-onchain-funnel-analysis) (6-stage model), [Formo dApp analytics](https://formo.so/blog/what-is-dapp-analytics-and-why-does-it-matter-in-web3), Mixpanel value-moment guidance, [UXCam KPIs](https://uxcam.com/blog/measure-fintech-app-onboarding-kpis/), [ProductGrowth](https://productgrowth.in/insights/fintech/trading-app-onboarding/) | The indexer→Turso pipeline gives first_trade_confirmed for free (position_opened by trader); only pre-chain steps need lightweight client events. Target time-to-first-trade in minutes (the <10 min best-in-class claim is a vendor figure — directional). Segment by referral code (?ref=CODE) since /referrals is live, and check whether /leaderboard visitors activate at higher rates. Review weekly; fix the largest cliff first. |
| **Browse-first: full read-only market before connect** | dYdX, Hyperliquid, GMX (all render live markets read-only); NN/g/Appcues deferred account creation | Noether mostly has this (Noeracle SSE feeds the chart without a wallet) — protect it as a hard rule: no connect-gating on /trade, /vaults marketplace, /leaderboard. The two historically-documented saboteurs — hardcoded market stats and a fixed 12.5% vault APY — were verified fixed/null-gated in the July 2026 audit; the live threats to the browse-first impression are the failure fallbacks that fabricate values ($0 prices on failed oracle reads, green +0.0000% funding). Dash them out before driving traffic — fake numbers are worse than an honest "testnet — stats coming" per NN/g accurate-status. |

**Anti-patterns**

- Forced welcome tours / tutorial carousels — NN/g found no task-performance improvement; "tutorials improve activation/retention" is **folklore**. Especially wrong for pro-first.
- Dead-end insufficient states — the current zero-balance path (toast, no faucet link; grep-verified) violates the direct-pathway guideline at the moment of highest intent.
- Inaccurate status as empty state — showing 0 where data is broken. Noether has shipped this class before (the since-fixed USDC-always-0 bug and hardcoded OI/volume/APY), and the $0-on-failed-oracle-read path keeps it alive today. For pros evaluating a venue, fake stats destroy trust faster than missing stats.
- Counting wallet-connect or faucet claim as activation — 20–30% fund and never trade; measure first trade executed and time-to-first-trade.
- Configuration-first checklists (margin mode, preferences before a quick win) get abandoned; >5 items adds measurable drop-off.
- Task-essential info hidden in tooltips — fees, liq price, margin are persistent layout elements.
- Testnet indistinguishable from real — no environment badge invites screenshots misrepresenting fund realness; CEXes strictly segregate demo accounts. Header-level TESTNET chip, not nothing (and not a space-eating banner).
- Copying Hyperliquid's faucet gating (prior mainnet deposit required) — wrong for Noether's current activation goal.
- Unnarrated 5s waits — a mute spinner reads as "broken" to traders habituated to ~0.2s finality; name, estimate, and step the latency or users double-submit and abandon.
- **Folklore flags on this theme's popular claims**: (a) 75% demo→live is one broker's self-reported figure; (b) "<10 min time-to-first-transaction" is a vendor benchmark without published methodology; (c) Chameleon/Appcues checklist stats come from vendor-customer datasets biased toward products that bought onboarding tooling.
- Spending design effort downstream while the top-of-funnel cliff remains — 35–50% of connected users never transact; fix connect→faucet→first-trade on /trade before polishing /vaults or /referrals onboarding.

---

### Vault & Copy-Trading

**Key findings**

- **Share-price (NAV-per-share) is the industry basis for pooled-vault performance**: [vaults.fyi](https://docs.vaults.fyi/general/methodology) computes every APY from on-chain share-price deltas over trailing 1d/7d/30d windows with min-TVL weighting and refuses projected APYs; [GMX](https://docs.gmx.io/docs/providing-liquidity/) documents GM "annualized performance" as a trailing share-price formula. Noether's vault_factory already stores nav_per_share and hwm_nav on-chain — the honest metric is computable today — but the UI shows "annualised closed-trade PnL / TVL," neither time-weighted nor inclusive of unrealized PnL.
- **[GIPS 2020](https://www.gipsstandards.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/03/2020_gips_standards_firms.pdf) prohibits annualizing sub-one-year returns** — directly indicting the DeFi habit (and Noether's current VaultMetrics hint) of turning a days-old record into an "APY." [DeFiLlama](https://defillama.com/yields) even ships an outlier-adjusted APY because inflated headline rates are endemic.
- **[ESMA's copy-trading briefing (ESMA35-42-1428)](https://www.esma.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2023-03/ESMA35-42-1428_Supervisory_Briefing_on_Copy_Trading.pdf) defines honest presentation**: past performance "should not be the most prominent element"; calculation method disclosed; benefits get the same prominence as risks; never forecast from simulated performance; all costs including profit share disclosed ex-ante — before the service starts, not at withdrawal. Copy trading with automatic execution qualifies as portfolio management under MiFID II.
- **Skin-in-the-game is displayed, not just enforced**: [Hyperliquid vault leaders](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/hypercore/vaults/for-vault-leaders-legacy) must hold ≥5% at all times and take 10% of profits above a high-water mark, with the leader's fraction shown on the vault page. Noether's vault_factory enforces the identical 5%/10%/HWM triple on-chain (leader withdrawals revert if the invariant breaks) but the UI does not show it — an unclaimed trust asset.
- **Max drawdown is table stakes** (Hyperliquid vault pages, [Bybit master-trader stats](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/How-to-Understand-Master-Trader-Performance), Bitget elite profiles). [Bitget publishes its exact formula](https://www.bitget.com/support/articles/12560603799585) — drawdown = (max ROI − current ROI) ÷ (max ROI − initial ROI + 1), full-period and rolling 21d. Publishing the method is itself the trust signal.
- **Copy trading causally increases risk-taking**: [Apesteguia, Oechssler & Weidenholzer (Management Science 2020)](https://pubsonline.informs.org/doi/10.1287/mnsc.2019.3508) — showing others' returns raises risk-taking, more with one-click copying, reducing ex-ante welfare (see also [CEPR VoxEU](https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/financial-social-trading-networks-case-copy-trading-platforms)). Raw-ROI marketplace ranking amplifies harm; Bitget mitigates with a composite score, [eToro with a daily 1–10 volatility-based risk score](https://help.etoro.com/s/article/How-does-eToro-calculate-and-present-risk?language=en_GB) shown next to returns with monthly history.
- **Lock-ups are communicated as depositor protection**: [Hyperliquid discloses 4-day HLP / 1-day user-vault locks](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/hypercore/vaults/for-vault-depositors-legacy) plus a withdrawal-slippage warning. Noether's vault_factory has NO lock-up and settles withdrawal in one ~5s transaction — a genuine differentiator, but only if withdrawal pricing while the leader has open positions is explained honestly (NAV is USDC-based; open-position PnL lands on close).
- **Realized vs unrealized are split, not blended**: [Bitget](https://www.bitget.com/support/articles/12560603811896) charts realized profits with unrealized behind an info symbol; Bybit updates cumulative ROI/profit hourly across 7/30/90d windows. Multi-window selectors prevent cherry-picking.
- **Radical position transparency is the on-chain vault's native advantage**: [Hyperliquid vault pages](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/hypercore/vaults) show open positions, full trade history, volume, and per-depositor "Your Performance" — the TWR-vs-MWR split GIPS formalizes ([LongsPeak: TWR judges the manager, MWR shows what you earned](https://www.longspeakadvisory.com/blog/when-to-use-time-weighted-return-twr-vs-money-weighted-return-mwr)).
- **[NN/g's four credibility factors](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/trustworthy-design/)** — design quality, up-front disclosure, comprehensive current content, connectedness — hold for fintech: users distrust products that make them dig for fees; disclosure belongs at the point of commitment (the deposit modal), not in docs.

**Pattern library**

| Pattern | Who uses it | How Noether applies it |
|---|---|---|
| **NAV-per-share chart as the hero performance display** — flow-immune, time-weighted; every displayed return derived from share-price deltas over trailing windows | [vaults.fyi](https://docs.vaults.fyi/general/methodology), [GMX GM pools](https://docs.gmx.io/docs/providing-liquidity/), [Hyperliquid vault pages](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/hypercore/vaults), GIPS-compliant managers | vault_factory already emits nav_per_share (7-decimal PRECISION) and the indexer projects vault events — add hourly NAV snapshots to the libsql projection; make /vaults/[id]'s chart a single gold NAV line on near-black (one gold series, muted grid, no rainbow). Replace VaultMetrics' "Annualised closed-trade PnL / TVL" APY with 7d/30d trailing share-price returns. Protocol /vault: NOE price = pool value / supply IS the share price — chart it instead of a static APY. |
| **The "young vault" guard: never annualize a sub-year record** — plain windowed return + age badge ("7d +4.2%", "New — 12d track record"), one decimal | [GIPS 2020](https://www.gipsstandards.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/03/2020_gips_standards_firms.pdf) (hard prohibition), [vaults.fyi](https://docs.vaults.fyi/general/methodology), [DeFiLlama](https://defillama.com/yields) | Directly fixes the known fake-data wound (the /vault APY was hardcoded 12.5% for months; VaultCard/VaultMetrics now annualize closed-trade PnL). Marketplace cards render "30d +4.2%" when eligible, else "— · 12d old"; the tile already has a null state ("Shown once fee revenue is tracked") — extend that discipline. Hyperliquid alumni recognize honest windowed returns and discount vanity APYs. |
| **Max drawdown with formula-in-tooltip + underwater strip** beneath the NAV chart | [Bitget published formula](https://www.bitget.com/support/articles/12560603799585), Hyperliquid vault stats, [Bybit MDD](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/How-to-Understand-Master-Trader-Performance) | VaultMetrics already shows drawdownBps with a danger tone above 15% — keep, and anchor it: tooltip "largest peak-to-trough fall in NAV per share, hourly snapshots, since inception" once the indexer NAV series exists. Underwater strip as a dim red/amber area under the gold NAV line (green/red reserved for signed values). With 3 pairs and 10x max, drawdowns are the main differentiator between vaults — make MDD sortable in the marketplace. |
| **Leader skin-in-the-game meter with "enforced on-chain" badge** — "Leader stake 8.3% — 5% minimum, enforced" + profit share + HWM status | [Hyperliquid](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/hypercore/vaults/for-vault-leaders-legacy) (≥5% floor shown; depositor guides like [ARX](https://arx.trade/blog/hyperliquid-vaults-explained/) treat it as a primary check), [eToro](https://help.etoro.com/s/article/How-does-eToro-calculate-and-present-risk?language=en_GB) Popular Investor profiles | vault_factory enforces the identical 5% floor (leader withdrawals revert) and 10%-above-hwm_nav profit share — no component surfaces it. Add a gold progress-bar chip to the /vaults/[id] header and a compact badge on VaultCard; copy: "Leader must keep ≥5% of this vault — enforced by the Soroban contract." A contract read, not a promise — a claim CEX copy trading cannot match; ideal for pro due diligence. |
| **Ex-ante profit-share disclosure with a worked example in the deposit modal** — "10% of profits above the high-water mark, charged on withdrawal. Nothing on losses." + (1,000 → +20% → 200 − 20 = 180) | [ESMA paras 42–43](https://www.esma.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2023-03/ESMA35-42-1428_Supervisory_Briefing_on_Copy_Trading.pdf), [Hyperliquid worked example](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/hypercore/vaults/for-vault-depositors-legacy), [Bybit profit-sharing page](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/Copy-Trading-Profit-Sharing-Explained), [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/trustworthy-design/) | Put it in DepositWithdrawModal on /vaults/[id]: persistent one-liner under the amount field + a details expander with the worked example and the vault's live HWM ("profit share only applies above 1.043") — hwm_nav is one Soroban read. Preview the estimated deduction on the withdraw side BEFORE signing: with 5s finality there is no abort window after signature, so all economics must be visible pre-sign. |
| **Withdrawal-expectations panel**: lock status, open-position caveat, settlement time | [Hyperliquid](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/hypercore/vaults/for-vault-depositors-legacy) (lock disclosure + "there may be some slippage as open positions are closed"), GMX price-impact warnings | Turn no-lock-up into a stated trust feature: "No lock-up. Withdrawal settles in one Stellar transaction (~5–10s)." Be honest about the caveat: NAV = total_usdc/circulating_shares, so the leader's unrealized PnL is NOT in your exit price until trades close — show a warning row when open positions exist ("Leader has 2 open positions; NAV reflects settled trades only"), marked against the live Noeracle price. If leader-configurable lock-ups are ever added, this panel is where they're disclosed. |
| **Multi-window return selector with realized/unrealized split** — 24h / 7d / 30d / all-time tabs; unrealized separately labeled | [Bitget](https://www.bitget.com/support/articles/12560603811896) (realized chart + unrealized in info symbol), [Bybit](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/How-to-Understand-Master-Trader-Performance), [vaults.fyi](https://docs.vaults.fyi/general/methodology) | shadcn Tabs pill row above the /vaults/[id] NAV chart, gold active state. The split is natural: realized = indexer NAV series; unrealized = the vault's open market-contract positions marked at the Noeracle price client-side (3 pairs → at most 3 marks to stream). Label "incl. unrealized, marked at oracle price" — pros will ask what the mark is. |
