| **Open-positions table + full trade log on the vault page** (radical transparency) | [Hyperliquid](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/hypercore/vaults), eToro (every position visible); depositor guides ([ARX](https://arx.trade/blog/hyperliquid-vaults-explained/)) instruct checking for cash-sitting vs concentrated leverage | VaultTradeHistory/VaultActivity already render from indexer events; add the live open-positions table by reading the market contract's positions for the vault address (same read path as /portfolio), marked at Noeracle price. With BTC/ETH/XLM only, add a one-glance net long/short exposure bar per pair — three thin gold/red segments, at home in the data-dense aesthetic. Positions are public on Stellar anyway; free-riding is not worth hiding for. |
| **Marketplace ranking that does not default to raw ROI** — default sort TVL/age/composite; sort controls exposed | [Bitget composite scoring](https://www.bitget.com/support/articles/12560603811896), [eToro risk score](https://help.etoro.com/s/article/How-does-eToro-calculate-and-present-risk?language=en_GB); causal rationale: [Management Science 2020](https://pubsonline.informs.org/doi/10.1287/mnsc.2019.3508), [CEPR](https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/financial-social-trading-networks-case-copy-trading-platforms) | /vaults page.tsx grid: default sort = TVL desc with an explicit dropdown (TVL, 30d return, max drawdown, age, depositors). Add a simple volatility chip — stdev of daily NAV returns over 30d bucketed Low/Med/High — rather than a proprietary score nobody can verify; on-chain data makes it reproducible, matching the every-action-on-chain positioning. Do NOT gold-highlight the top-ROI card; highlight is a brand accent, not an endorsement. |
| **Balanced prominence + "how is this computed?" tooltips on every metric** | [ESMA paras 39–41](https://www.esma.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2023-03/ESMA35-42-1428_Supervisory_Briefing_on_Copy_Trading.pdf), Bitget info symbols, vaults.fyi methodology links | The protocol /vault StatsBar renders APY as a green 3xl hero — demote to the same weight as TVL, keep the muted hint line, add tooltips ("APY: trailing 30d share-price return, annualized" / "Max drawdown: peak-to-trough NAV, hourly snapshots"). Gold-on-dark makes prominence discipline easy: metrics mono white, labels muted, gold for identity/CTAs, green/red only for signed deltas — VaultMetrics' tone system already half-implements this. |
| **"Your performance" (money-weighted, net of fees) separate from vault performance (time-weighted)** | [Hyperliquid "Your Performance"](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/hypercore/vaults), [GIPS](https://www.gipsstandards.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/03/2020_gips_standards_firms.pdf), [LongsPeak TWR/MWR](https://www.longspeakadvisory.com/blog/when-to-use-time-weighted-return-twr-vs-money-weighted-return-mwr) | Extend MyVaultPosition: "Your value: 1,043 USDC · Your net return +4.3% (after 10% profit share above HWM)" plus a withdraw preview ("If you exit now you receive ~X after fees") priced at current NAV — computable from on-chain hwm_nav + share balance in one read. Roll the same numbers up on /portfolio across all vaults. Without the split, a top-ticker entrant sees a green vault chart while personally down and concludes the UI lies. The 5s-finality advantage: preview and post-signature result should match almost exactly — say so. |
| **Counterparty-pool honesty for the protocol vault** (HLP/GLP pattern) — "Depositors take the opposite side of traders' PnL" in the money sentence; returns decomposed into fee income vs trader-PnL transfer | [GMX GM/GLP](https://docs.gmx.io/docs/providing-liquidity/) (63% fee split, APR formula), [Hyperliquid HLP](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/hypercore/vaults/for-vault-depositors-legacy) (open strategy PnL, 4-day lock) | The /vault page already has the right sentence in HowItWorks/RiskDisclosure ("When traders profit, the pool value decreases…") — keep it adjacent to the deposit button, not only in an accordion. Once the indexer NAV series exists, add a small two-series area chart: cumulative trading-fee income vs cumulative trader PnL settled against the vault (both flow through vault settle events). Frame NOE explicitly as the share: "NOE price = pool value / supply." |

**Anti-patterns**

- Annualizing days-old performance into an "APY" — VaultMetrics currently hints "Annualised closed-trade PnL / TVL"; GIPS prohibits it because a lucky week becomes "400% APY."
- Hardcoded or projected yields — the protocol vault shipped 12.5% APY hardcoded for months (now null-guarded; keep it that way). Never display a yield you didn't measure from share-price history.
- Cumulative-PnL charts as the vault hero — total PnL grows with TVL, flattering big old vaults while hiding per-share reality; depositor guides call stopping at total profit "a mistake."
- Counting only closed trades while open losers ride — realized-only windows dress the record (a leader can hold losers open to keep stats green); if the chart is realized-only, unrealized exposure must be disclosed beside it, and Noether's USDC-based NAV makes this mandatory to explain at withdrawal.
- Revealing the profit share only at withdrawal — ESMA requires ex-ante cost disclosure; hidden fees corrode trust (NN/g).
- Default-ranking the marketplace by raw ROI — experimentally shown to push users into excess risk; also gameable (a fresh vault with one lucky trade tops the list).
- Any stat without method and window — a bare "Drawdown 12%" is unfalsifiable; Bitget publishes its formula.
- Making the return figure the loudest element on the page — ESMA flags this explicitly; the /vault StatsBar green 3xl hero is this anti-pattern verbatim.
- False precision — two-decimal APYs and smooth interpolated charts on sparse young-vault data; round to one decimal, show data-age badges.
- Treating a "past performance is not a guarantee" footnote as sufficient — regulators require balanced prominence and method disclosure; disclaimer-alone is industry **folklore**.
- **Folklore / weak-evidence flags**: (1) the circulating "NN/g: 46% cite visual simplicity as the top trust factor" stat could not be verified in any primary NN/g report — do not cite; (2) "max drawdown is the single most important risk metric" ([ARX](https://arx.trade/blog/hyperliquid-vaults-explained/)) is practitioner opinion; (3) eToro's risk-score formula is proprietary — copy the display pattern, not a formula; (4) Hyperliquid vault-creation costs/lock details sit on docs pages marked "legacy" — re-verify before quoting in marketing comparisons.

---

### Referral, Points & Leaderboard Growth

**Key findings**

- **The 2024–2026 points meta converged on multi-month seasons with fixed, published weekly pools**: Hyperliquid seasons 1–2.5, Paradex 4M XP every Friday with a published end date, Extended 1.2M points/week ([OAK farming guide](https://oakresearch.io/en/analyses/fundamentals/ultimate-guide-farming-perp-dex-airdrop-2025), [Dropstab meta](https://news.dropstab.com/research/top-perpetual-dex-platforms-for-point-farming)), dYdX 4–5 governance-voted trading seasons over 6 months. Single-snapshot airdrops are dead; open-ended programs cause farming fatigue.
- **Hyperliquid is the only consensus success case** ([OAK](https://oakresearch.io/en/analyses/investigations/airdrops-2025-end-or-renewal), [PANews](https://www.panewslab.com/en/articles/zena4u1n)): transparent accrual + fixed weekly rate, explicit wash-trading penalties, seasons that re-rewarded users who stayed, 31–38.9% of supply for community — but analysts attribute durable retention to product quality, not the points.
- **Retention data is brutal and replicated**: 64% of recipients sell at TGE; 50–70% of airdropped tokens sold within 30 days; 88% of tokens depreciate within ~3 months; ~30% sybil prevalence; LayerZero filtered 59% of applicant wallets ([Coinlaw aggregate](https://coinlaw.io/token-airdrop-statistics/); [arXiv "Airdrops: Giving Money Away Is Harder Than It Seems"](https://arxiv.org/html/2312.02752)). Points buy activity spikes, not retention — plan seasons as marketing with a defined end.
- **The anti-wash standard is dYdX's formula**: accrue on TAKER FEES PAID × OPEN INTEREST HELD — both expensive to fake — with third-party wash detection and season-end disqualification ([dYdX Foundation](https://www.dydx.foundation/blog/trading-rewards), [Chaos Labs program](https://chaoslabs.xyz/posts/dydx-v4-launch-incentives), [Chaos Labs wash detection](https://chaoslabs.xyz/posts/dydx-chain-launch-incentives-program-wash-trading-detection)). Raw notional volume as the reward metric is now considered a design error.
- **Leaderboard research agrees** ([CHI 2017](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3025453.3025826), [ui-patterns](https://ui-patterns.com/patterns/leaderboard), [Yu-kai Chou](https://yukaichou.com/advanced-gamification/how-to-design-effective-leaderboards-boosting-motivation-and-engagement/)): the user's own position shapes perception of the whole app; global all-time boards demotivate the majority and stagnate; best practice is a short default timeframe, pinned self-rank with ~5 rows above/below, percentile framing ("top 24%"), rank-movement deltas, periodic resets. Duolingo's weekly 30-person leagues with promotion/demotion are the canonical reset design (claimed +25% lesson completion — [secondary source](https://duolingo.deconstructoroffun.com/mechanics/leagues)). Motivation theory backdrop: [NN/g on autonomy/relatedness/competence](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/autonomy-relatedness-competence/).
- **Two-sided referral rewards outperform one-sided in randomized field experiments** ([JMIS 2021](https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/abs/10.1080/07421222.2021.1870384)): both equal-split and generous schemes raise conversions mainly via INVITEE acceptance. [Hyperliquid's numbers](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/referrals) — 4% referee discount (first $25M volume), 10% referrer share (first $1B), $10k volume gate to create a code, $1 claim threshold — are the perp-DEX norm. Noether's referral contract already mirrors the 4%/10% split; remaining work is presentation, gating, claim UX.
- **The organic distribution surface is the PnL share card** (Hyperliquid, Binance, Bybit, plus a third-party generator ecosystem — [pnlgen.org](https://pnlgen.org/dex/hyperliquid), [Tealstreet](https://docs.tealstreet.io/docs/trade/pnl-cards)): market, side, leverage, ROE%, entry/mark, referral code + QR, percentage-only privacy option. Paired with dynamic OG images on the join link (1200x630, 1080x600 safe zone — [PageThen](https://pagethen.com/blog/og-image-best-practices), [OpenGraph.xyz](https://www.opengraph.xyz/blog/how-to-implement-dynamic-open-graph-images)).
- **"Scammy" is triggered by a broken social contract, not incentives per se** — the EigenLayer casebook: rules revealed/changed at claim time, geo-exclusion announced at distribution, top 2% of wallets holding ~90% of points, non-transferable tokens ([CoinDesk](https://www.coindesk.com/tech/2024/05/09/eigenlayers-eigen-airdrop-might-signal-demise-of-once-popular-points), [The Defiant](https://thedefiant.io/news/research-and-opinion/eigenlayer-airdrop-outcry-shows-how-points-are-raising-trader-expectations)). Mitigations: publish accrual formula, eligibility, geo restrictions, and end date BEFORE the season; never change rules mid-season; "points are not a promise of tokens" in plain sight.
- **Eligibility floors are competition hygiene**: [Gate's Long & Short King](https://www.gate.com/learn/articles/gate-perp-dex-long-short-king-competition/14240) requires >10,000 USDT volume AND ROI > 0 to rank; [Gemini's ROI Rumble](https://www.gemini.com/leaderboard) ranks 24h ROI periods; [SparkDEX](https://medium.com/@SparkDEX/sparkdex-eternal-perps-trading-competition-trade-compete-and-earn-rflr-rewards-dd29885d0c69) runs weekly calc with monthly epoch distribution.

**Pattern library**

| Pattern | Who uses it | How Noether applies it |
|---|---|---|
| **Fixed-pool, fixed-length points season with published rules** — pool/week, hard end date, exact accrual formula, anti-abuse rules, all immutable for the season | Hyperliquid ([PANews](https://www.panewslab.com/en/articles/zena4u1n)), Paradex, Extended ([OAK](https://oakresearch.io/en/analyses/fundamentals/ultimate-guide-farming-perp-dex-airdrop-2025)), dYdX ([Chaos Labs](https://chaoslabs.xyz/posts/dydx-v4-launch-incentives)) | Run testnet seasons as 2-week epochs aligning with the existing 14-day rolling fee-tier volume window (the indexer already computes it in Turso). Season header card on /leaderboard (web/app/leaderboard/page.tsx): pool, formula, countdown in gold-on-dark; rules link to /terms and /restricted. With 3 pairs and faucet USDC, keep pools small and symbolic — the point is rehearsing mechanics and the social contract before mainnet. |
| **Accrue on fees paid × time-weighted open interest, never raw volume** + season-end wash-detection pass | [dYdX Foundation](https://www.dydx.foundation/blog/trading-rewards) (activityScore 70–85% weight, ROI component 15–30%), [Chaos Labs wash detection](https://chaoslabs.xyz/posts/dydx-chain-launch-incentives-program-wash-trading-detection) | Critical because oracle-priced perps have no orderbook friction: a farmer trades against the Noeracle mark with zero spread, so volume is nearly free — only the 0.05% taker fee is a real cost. Fees paid are already tracked per-wallet for fee tiers; position events are in the indexer, so fees×OI scoring is computable off-chain today. Publish the formula on the season card; it also justifies keeping the volume column informational rather than prize-bearing. |
| **Self-rank pinning** — sticky "you" row with ~5 neighbors, gap-to-next, percentile framing | [ui-patterns](https://ui-patterns.com/patterns/leaderboard), [Yu-kai Chou](https://yukaichou.com/advanced-gamification/how-to-design-effective-leaderboards-boosting-motivation-and-engagement/), Duolingo, [Gemini](https://www.gemini.com/leaderboard); app examples: [Trophy.so](https://trophy.so/blog/leaderboards-feature-gamification-examples) | web/components/leaderboard/LeaderboardContent.tsx already knows the connected wallet via walletStore — add a sticky bottom row "You · #37 · top 18%" with a gold border-accent against the neutral white/[0.08] rows, plus "need +$412 volume to pass #36." No-trade wallets see the entry condition ("make 1 trade on /trade to rank") instead of a hidden row. Addresses the strongest research finding: own-position visibility drives perception of the whole product. |
| **Short default timeframe with tabs; all-time demoted to archive** + rank-movement deltas | [Gemini ROI Rumble](https://www.gemini.com/leaderboard) (24h), Gate (10-min refresh), Duolingo (7-day reset), [ui-patterns](https://ui-patterns.com/patterns/leaderboard) ("all-time boards grow too stable and elite") | The current /leaderboard is a single all-time table sorted by PnL or volume — on a 3-pair testnet it freezes into the same 10 wallets within weeks. Tabs: Epoch (default, matches the 2-week season), 7d, All-time. The 60s polling in LeaderboardContent.tsx is fine given 5s ledgers; add per-row movement arrows from a daily indexer snapshot, gold-up/gray-down to avoid colliding with green/red PnL cells. |
| **Dual ranking metrics with an eligibility floor** — PnL/volume (whale board) + ROI = PnL/collateral (skill board); floor = min volume, min trades, ROI > 0 | [Gate Long & Short King](https://www.gate.com/learn/articles/gate-perp-dex-long-short-king-competition/14240), [Gemini](https://www.gemini.com/leaderboard), dYdX ROI component | Add an ROI% column computed as pnl/collateral-committed from indexer position events, and a floor (e.g. ≥$1,000 faucet-USDC volume across ≥5 trades per epoch — cheap to meet legitimately at 10 USDC min collateral, but filters faucet-sybil noise). Label the floor explicitly under the Rankings header so Hyperliquid/Bybit-trained pros read it as competition hygiene, not opacity. |
| **Percentile-band prize framing, not winner-take-all** — name the top 10, then reward bands (top 5%, top 25%); celebrate movement | [Yu-kai Chou](https://yukaichou.com/advanced-gamification/how-to-design-effective-leaderboards-boosting-motivation-and-engagement/), Duolingo promotion zones, [SparkDEX weekly tranches](https://medium.com/@SparkDEX/sparkdex-eternal-perps-trading-competition-trade-compete-and-earn-rflr-rewards-dd29885d0c69), [CHI 2017](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3025453.3025826) | Publish the band table next to the season card (top 3 highlighted with the existing gold accent, ranks 4–10 listed, then "top 10% band"). The pinned self-row shows current band, not just rank. Keep top-3 styling restrained — gold is the brand's primary accent, so use weight and a thin gold rule rather than large gold fills, preserving hierarchy for numbers. |
| **Two-sided referral stated as one plain sentence with caps and gates** — "They get 4% off fees, you earn 10% of their fees" + volume gate before code creation + small claim threshold | [Hyperliquid referrals](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/referrals), Bybit; evidence: [JMIS 2021](https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/abs/10.1080/07421222.2021.1870384), [impact.com](https://impact.com/referral/better-referral-rewards-recipient-incentives/) | The contract already matches Hyperliquid's 4%/10%, and web/components/referral/ShareLinkCard.tsx states it — add the caps/gates story: enforce the existing min-volume config (web/scripts/referral-set-min-volume.ts) before CreateCodeCard allows creation, and say so ("create a code after $1k traded volume"). Pros recognize the Hyperliquid template instantly; deviating silently reads as amateur or bait. Also disclose that the discount is currently applied at the gateway, not in contract events (known off-chain-discount gap) — on-chain divergence a pro can see is a scam signal if unexplained. |
| **Referral join route with dynamic OG share card** — /join/CODE with a per-code 1200x630 OG image | Hyperliquid (app.hyperliquid.xyz/join/CODE); Next.js @vercel/og practice; [PageThen](https://pagethen.com/blog/og-image-best-practices), [OpenGraph.xyz](https://www.opengraph.xyz/blog/how-to-implement-dynamic-open-graph-images) | ShareLinkCard currently copies noether.exchange/?ref=CODE — a bare query param with the generic site preview. Add web/app/join/[code]/ that sets the sticky ref (ReferralBanner already persists ?ref=CODE) and an opengraph-image.tsx rendering the slashed-O wordmark, the code in large mono, "4% off trading fees" in gold on near-black; the math-glyph border strip makes the card recognizable in a feed. Headline + code inside the 1080x600 safe zone. A Next.js 14 App Router one-day task; no chain work. Vendor claim of ~3–5x CTR for tailored OG images is **directional, not peer-reviewed**. |
| **PnL share card at position close, referral code embedded, %-only default** | Hyperliquid, Binance (percentage-only option), Bybit, [Tealstreet](https://docs.tealstreet.io/docs/trade/pnl-cards), [pnlgen.org ecosystem](https://pnlgen.org/dex/hyperliquid) | Build client-side (canvas/ImageResponse) so nothing sensitive leaves the browser; trigger from the close-position success state on /trade and from web/app/portfolio. Entry/exit from Noeracle marks the UI already has; 3 pair-tinted variants of one gold-on-dark template with the glyph strip. Embed the trader's code + /join/CODE QR automatically — converting green trades into referral distribution is the loop Hyperliquid rode. The 5s tx latency helps: card generation fills the confirmation wait. |
| **Referral dashboard: accrued vs claimed, claim as a real transaction** | Hyperliquid dashboard (claim >$1, lands in spot balance), Bybit affiliate dashboards | ReferralStats/ClaimFeesCard/ReferralActivity (web/components/referral/) already sketch this; make accrual update from indexer events and give the claim button the same 5s-ledger pending treatment as trades (spinner + "confirming on Stellar ~5s" + tx hash link). Pros judge referral programs by whether earnings are claimable in-product without support tickets; a dead or vague claim button is a top scam signal. |
| **Season-end results ceremony and receipts** — frozen archived standings, per-band results, stated distribution date, permanent archive | Duolingo Monday ceremony ([Deconstructor of Fun](https://duolingo.deconstructoroffun.com/mechanics/leagues)), [SparkDEX](https://medium.com/@SparkDEX/sparkdex-eternal-perps-trading-competition-trade-compete-and-earn-rflr-rewards-dd29885d0c69), dYdX per-season reports | Freeze the epoch tab at season end into "Season N — final" (indexer snapshot); show the connected wallet a compact result card ("Season 2: #14, top 6%") reusing the PnL-card renderer for sharing. Distribution receipts (tx hashes) listed on the archive page are cheap, powerful anti-scam evidence — everything Noether does is already on-chain, so show the proofs. |
| **Pre-published anti-abuse and eligibility policy with disqualification receipts** — wash/self-referral definitions, sybil approach, geo restrictions up front; post-season disqualification counts | [Chaos Labs × dYdX](https://chaoslabs.xyz/posts/dydx-chain-launch-incentives-program-wash-trading-detection), LayerZero/Linea published purges ([Coinlaw](https://coinlaw.io/token-airdrop-statistics/)), Hyperliquid penalties; EigenLayer as the negative example | The testnet faucet makes sybil trivial (free USDC), so a season MUST publish expectations: fee-based accrual, self-referral exclusion (the referral contract binds referrer on-chain — checkable), and /restricted geo terms surfaced before accrual starts, not at reward time. Post-season, publish "N wallets disqualified for wash patterns" on the archive page. Filtering is normal; SURPRISE filtering causes outrage. |
| **Points honesty: transparent accrual, explicit non-promise** — visible UI copy, not buried ToS | Hyperliquid (transparent rate); EigenLayer/Blast as negative examples ([CoinDesk](https://www.coindesk.com/tech/2024/05/09/eigenlayers-eigen-airdrop-might-signal-demise-of-once-popular-points), [The Defiant](https://thedefiant.io/news/research-and-opinion/eigenlayer-airdrop-outcry-shows-how-points-are-raising-trader-expectations)) | One-line disclosure directly under the season card on /leaderboard and on /referrals: "Points measure testnet participation. They are not a token and carry no guarantee of future value — see /terms," muted-foreground style, always visible. For Hyperliquid-alumni traders who have been burned by ambiguity, this candor differentiates. Given SCF funding and a planned mainnet, never let marketing imply an airdrop the founders have not committed to. |
| **Pseudonymity by default, identity opt-in** — truncated addresses, opt-in display names, no absolute collateral on public boards | Hyperliquid leaderboard (optional names), Zerion/Nansen wallet-following culture, Binance %-only share; Arkham-style OSINT is the threat model | LeaderboardContent.tsx already truncates (truncateAddress) — keep as default; add opt-in display names via a signed off-chain profile using the existing wallet-challenge auth in the api/ gateway (no contract change). Show ROI% and volume publicly; keep absolute collateral off the public table even though derivable — pros with real size avoid boards that advertise account scale, which would empty the very leaderboard Noether wants them on. |

**Anti-patterns**

- Volume-ranked prizes on an oracle-priced venue — with no orderbook spread, self-churn against the Noeracle mark costs only the 0.05% taker fee; a volume leaderboard becomes a wash-trading treadmill paying farmers from the prize pool (dYdX/Chaos Labs found cyclical wash trading even with fee-based scoring; Gate requires ROI>0 + volume floors for this reason).
- A single all-time leaderboard with no self-rank row — the current /leaderboard state; it stagnates, motivates only incumbents, and fossilizes within weeks on a 3-pair testnet.
- Strategic ambiguity about points-to-token conversion plus airdrop innuendo — post-EigenLayer, the primary "scammy" trigger for exactly the audience Noether targets.
- Changing accrual rules, eligibility, or geo restrictions retroactively or at claim time — rule changes belong between seasons, announced in advance.
- Leaving the gateway-applied referral discount undisclosed — contract events show undiscounted fees while the gateway discounts; a pro reconciling on-chain data reads divergence as fee manipulation unless /referrals states it plainly (known repo issue).
- Faking scale — hardcoded/padded stats (the class Noether has since purged: the old OI/volume/APY placeholders); a leaderboard or referral page whose totals don't reconcile with the chain is discoverable in minutes. Honest small numbers with a season narrative beat fake big ones.
- Winner-take-all prize framing — motivates ~5–10% of participants and suppresses the rest; use bands and percentiles.
- Open-ended points programs with no published end date or pool — farming fatigue and "exit liquidity" suspicion; every credible 2025–26 program publishes both.
- Doxxing traders by default — full addresses tied to absolute PnL/collateral drives serious size off the board.
- **Folklore flags**: "OG images give 3–5x CTR" and "referred fintech users have 25–37% higher LTV" are vendor-marketing numbers (opengraph.xyz, growsurf/impact.com) — directionally plausible, not peer-reviewed. Duolingo's "+25% lesson completion from leagues" is a secondary-source case study without published methodology. Yu-kai Chou's "only the top 5–10% are motivated" is a practitioner heuristic — CHI 2017 supports position-dependent perception, not that exact figure.

---

### Visual Craft: Typography, Color & Contrast on Dark

**Key findings**

- **WCAG 2.x contrast ratios overstate legibility on dark backgrounds**; the [APCA documentation](https://git.apcacontrast.com/documentation/APCA_in_a_Nutshell.html) states WCAG 2 "cannot be used for guidance designing dark mode." Use APCA Lc targets alongside AA ([WebAIM](https://webaim.org/articles/contrast/)): Lc 90 preferred body, Lc 75 minimum body, Lc 60 minimum other content text, Lc 45 large/heavy only, Lc 30 spot-readable (placeholders/disabled), Lc 15 minimum for any discernible non-text.
- **Computed audit of Noether's actual palette (web/app/globals.css)**: gold #eab308 on card #0a0a0a = 10.32:1 WCAG / APCA Lc −66 (fine for accents and ≥14px semibold labels; below the Lc 75 body-text bar); amber-600 #d97706 = 6.21:1 / Lc −43 (large text only); amber-700 #b45309 = 3.94:1 (fails AA normal text — decorative only); loss red #ef4444 = 5.26:1 / Lc −37.7 (passes AA but APCA-adequate only for large/bold — too weak for 12px dense-table PnL; red-400 #f87171 reaches Lc −49); profit green #22c55e = 8.69:1 / Lc −57.7 (borderline small text; green-400 #4ade80 = Lc −71).
- **VERIFIED DEFECT**: `--primary-foreground` is white (`0 0% 100%`, globals.css line 13) on the gold primary (`45 93% 47%`, line 12 ≈ #eab308) — 1.92:1, a hard WCAG AA fail for every gold button/CTA. Black text on the same gold is 10.95:1 (APCA Lc 67.5). Binance, Bybit and most gold/amber trading UIs use dark-on-gold fills for exactly this reason.
- **Noether's red vs green (#ef4444 vs #22c55e) have only 1.65:1 mutual contrast** — effectively the same color under deuteranopia/protanopia. [WCAG 1.4.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/use-of-color.html) requires a second cue: signs (+/−), triangles, or a selectable palette. [Binance ships a CVD palette](https://www.binance.com/en/blog/ecosystem/color-your-trading-binance-adds-support-for-color-vision-deficiencies-421499824684903930) rendering up as blue, down as orange; [TradingView ships Color-blind and Monochrome schemes](https://www.tradingview.com/support/solutions/43000707158-how-to-change-the-color-of-heatmaps/).
- **Tabular lining figures are the settled standard for financial columns** ([Fonts.com](https://www.myfonts.com/pages/fontscom-learning-fontology-level-3-numbers-proportional-vs-tabular-figures), [Type Network](https://typenetwork.com/articles/opentype-at-work-figure-styles), [Datawrapper](https://www.datawrapper.de/blog/fonts-for-data-visualization)). One line on the web: `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/font-variant-numeric), [Tailwind](https://tailwindcss.com/docs/font-variant-numeric)) — eliminates digit jitter without monospace. Inter (already loaded) ships full tabular support; JetBrains Mono stays reserved for addresses/hashes/tx IDs.
- **Density is a first-class named mode**: [AWS Cloudscape](https://cloudscape.design/foundation/visual-foundation/content-density/) ships Comfortable + Compact; [Material documents applying density](https://m2.material.io/design/layout/applying-density.html) for scan/compare tables; [Pencil & Paper](https://www.pencilandpaper.io/articles/ux-pattern-analysis-enterprise-data-tables) gives 40px condensed / 48px regular / 56px relaxed rows, persisted per user. [Bloomberg-class users expect maximum data density](https://www.bloomberg.com/company/stories/how-bloomberg-terminal-ux-designers-conceal-complexity/).
- **Flash-on-update has a de-facto spec**: [AG Grid](https://www.ag-grid.com/javascript-data-grid/change-detection/) flashes a changed cell's background 500ms then fades 1000ms via CSS classes. [WCAG 2.3.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/three-flashes-or-below-threshold.html) caps flashing at 3/second; WCAG 2.2.2 requires pausable auto-updating content; [prefers-reduced-motion is an accepted mechanism](https://www.boia.org/blog/what-to-know-about-the-css-prefers-reduced-motion-feature).
- **Motion lives in the 100–500ms band** ([NN/g animation duration](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/animation-duration/): under ~100ms reads instantaneous, over 500ms drags); [NN/g complex-application research](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/complex-application-design/) says dense screens hinge on reducing visual search — strip non-data ink so preattentive cues do the work ([NN/g preattentive dashboards](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/dashboards-preattentive/)).
- **[Matt Ström's "UI Density"](https://mattstromawn.com/writing/ui-density/)** reframes Bloomberg's superpower as temporal density — near-zero latency — with a latency ladder: 1–10s operations (exactly Noether's ~5s close) call for indeterminate in-place progress while keeping surrounding data visible, never blanked panels.
- **Dark-surface craft consensus** ([Material dark theme](https://design.google/library/material-design-dark-theme)): avoid pure black — use near-black (#121212-class) surfaces and express elevation by lightening, because shadows are nearly invisible on #000; desaturate accents (saturated hues "visually vibrate"); prefer off-white (~#e5e5e5–#fafafa) over pure white body text. Noether's background is currently pure #000 with an almost-flat #0a0a0a card step.

**Pattern library**

| Pattern | Who uses it | How Noether applies it |
|---|---|---|
| **Tabular lining figures via font-variant-numeric, not monospace-everything** | Financial-table standard ([Fonts.com](https://www.myfonts.com/pages/fontscom-learning-fontology-level-3-numbers-proportional-vs-tabular-figures), [Type Network](https://typenetwork.com/articles/opentype-at-work-figure-styles), [Datawrapper](https://www.datawrapper.de/blog/fonts-for-data-visualization)); [MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/font-variant-numeric)/[DEV](https://dev.to/alanwest/tabular-numbers-in-css-font-variant-numeric-vs-monospace-hacks-25cn) document it as the correct alternative to monospace hacks | Noeracle SSE marks stream into /trade's ticker, OrderPanel estimates, and PnL cells — every tick risks digit jitter. Add Tailwind [`tabular-nums`](https://tailwindcss.com/docs/font-variant-numeric) to the mark price, PnL cells, margin/equity readouts on /portfolio, vault TVL/APY on /vault and /vaults, and /leaderboard volume columns. Zero new fonts, zero bundle cost — one utility on ~15 components. JetBrains Mono only for hashes/addresses/API keys. |
| **Right-aligned numeric columns with per-pair fixed decimals** | [Pencil & Paper](https://www.pencilandpaper.io/articles/ux-pattern-analysis-enterprise-data-tables); universal on Binance/Bybit/Hyperliquid tables; Bloomberg | With 3 pairs the precision map is trivial: constants in web/lib/utils (mirroring the 7-decimal contract PRECISION) consumed by positions/orders tables on /trade, /portfolio history, and the own-orders panel. This also converts CLAUDE.md's ad-hoc "format 7-decimal values to 2–4 decimals" rule into one formatNum(asset, kind) helper. |
| **Contrast-tiered gold usage map** — which golds may carry text on which surfaces | Method from [APCA-W3](https://git.apcacontrast.com/documentation/APCA_in_a_Nutshell.html) font-size/weight-to-Lc lookup + [WebAIM](https://webaim.org/articles/contrast/); values computed against Noether's own globals.css | Codify in globals.css: #eab308 = accents, labels ≥14px medium/semibold, links, active states (Lc ~66 — never long paragraphs); amber-400 #fbbf24 / yellow-300 #fde047 for small gold text (Lc −73/−88); amber-600 #d97706 only ≥24px bold; amber-700 and darker = decorative only (3.94:1 AA fail). Math-glyph strips live in the Lc 15–25 band — texture, never information-bearing — keeping ornament off the dense /trade panels' contrast budget. Brand stays exactly as-is; the tier list prevents new features (vault leader badges, referral copy) from putting body text in dark gold. |
| **Fix inverted button polarity: dark text on gold fills** | Binance (dark text on #FCD535 primaries), Bybit; required by WCAG 1.4.3 / [WebAIM](https://webaim.org/articles/contrast/) | `--primary-foreground: 0 0% 100%` in web/app/globals.css line 13 makes every shadcn primary button — Place Order, vault deposit/withdraw, referral claim, faucet — an AA failure today (1.92:1). One-token fix: set primary-foreground to ~`0 0% 4%` (near-black on gold = 10.3:1, APCA Lc 67). Visually matches what the pro audience sees daily on Binance. Highest impact-to-effort item in this entire report. White text stays for outlined/ghost gold buttons where the surface is the background. |
| **Three-tier AA-verified text ramp on dark surfaces** | [Material dark theme](https://design.google/library/material-design-dark-theme) (high/medium/disabled emphasis), WebAIM methodology, APCA polarity guidance | Noether has --foreground (98%) and --muted-foreground (65%); the missing piece is the enforcement rule for tier three: primary numerals ~#fafafa (18.9:1 on card), secondary labels ~#9fa0aa (7.6:1 on card, 6.7:1 on #1a1a1a — safely AA), and anything below 4.5:1 (zinc-500 #71717a: 4.1:1 on card, 3.6:1 on secondary surfaces) used ONLY for genuinely disabled states — never for "de-emphasized but needed" data like funding rates or timestamps. Consider #e5e5e5 for long-form body to soften halation. Verify tones against #1a1a1a inputs and #262626 borders, where chips/badges sit. |
| **Directional data = color + sign + shape, sized to pass on real surfaces** | [WCAG 1.4.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/use-of-color.html); Binance and TradingView ship signed percentages with arrows; APCA font-to-Lc method | Every gain/loss renders with (1) explicit +/− or ▲/▼, (2) direction color, (3) size/weight clearing contrast on its actual surface: red-500 #ef4444 (Lc −37.7) qualifies only for large/bold, so 12–13px dense-table PnL needs red-400 #f87171 / green-400 #4ade80 or semibold weight. Applies to PositionsList PnL/ROE, the 24h-change chip, vault PnL, leaderboard deltas. The own-orders "Order Book" panel gets NO fake red/green depth bars — style as "Your open orders" with a Long/Short text chip. |
| **Up/down color-scheme setting with a CVD-safe blue/orange option** | [Binance Style Settings](https://www.binance.com/en/blog/ecosystem/color-your-trading-binance-adds-support-for-color-vision-deficiencies-421499824684903930) + [candle customization](https://www.binance.com/en/support/faq/how-to-customize-the-candlestick-colours-and-technical-indicators-3929908c77d44df1b7542930db0efea3), [TradingView schemes](https://www.tradingview.com/support/solutions/43000707158-how-to-change-the-color-of-heatmaps/), [OKX candle settings](https://www.okx.com/en-ar/help/candlestick-faqs-and-settings) | Noether already centralizes --profit and --loss as CSS custom properties in globals.css — the entire feature is a Zustand-persisted preference in tradeStore swapping those two variables (plus chart series colors), flipping /trade, /portfolio, /vaults, /leaderboard at once. Ship: Green-up (default), Red-up (flip), CVD blue-up/orange-down. For an audience arriving from Binance/Bybit, absence reads as unfinished; roughly one day of work. **Folklore flag:** "red-up is the East Asian default" was not verified against a primary source — the evidenced pattern is simply that every major venue ships the setting. |
| **Named density modes: Compact default on /trade, persisted** — Compact ~32–40px rows / Comfortable ~48px; padding shrinks, type stays ≥12px | [Cloudscape](https://cloudscape.design/foundation/visual-foundation/content-density/), [Material density](https://m2.material.io/design/layout/applying-density.html), [Pencil & Paper 40/48/56px](https://www.pencilandpaper.io/articles/ux-pattern-analysis-enterprise-data-tables); Bloomberg as ceiling; CEX-parity expectations ([RankFi](https://rankfi.com/dex/hyperliquid-review/)) | Pro-first: /trade positions/orders/history and the own-orders panel default Compact so a Hyperliquid trader sees expected density; /portfolio and /vaults marketplace default Comfortable for newcomers (progressive disclosure), same toggle available. One `density` value in tradeStore mapped to Tailwind row-height/padding variants; persists like the color preference. |
| **Cell-flash convention: low-alpha luminance tint, 500ms flash + ~1s fade, ≤3/sec** | [AG Grid defaults](https://www.ag-grid.com/javascript-data-grid/change-detection/) (ag-cell-data-changed 500ms + 1000ms fade), Binance/Bybit tickers; [WCAG 2.3.1 cap](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/three-flashes-or-below-threshold.html) | Marks arrive via Noeracle SSE (shim fallback) — one price per pair, so flashes are naturally infrequent; still coalesce bursts to ≤2–3/sec per cell. Apply to the pair-header mark, mark column, uPnL cells. Use rgba of --profit/--loss at ~8–12% alpha over #0a0a0a so the flash respects the gold-on-dark mood, staying below the 10%-luminance general-flash definition. |
| **Digit-roll ticker for the hero price, reduced-motion fallback** | [Robinhood's signature digit animation](https://matt-croak.medium.com/animate-your-digits-like-robinhood-2fd3e24bdc16); Hyperliquid/Binance use instant-swap-plus-color (the roll is optional polish, the color cue is the convention) | One component: the /trade pair-header price fed by the Noeracle stream (~200–300ms roll on changed digits only, brief direction tint). Requires tabular-nums so the roll doesn't reflow neighbors. Under prefers-reduced-motion: instant swap, color-only. Pause the roll when the SSE feed goes stale and the UI falls back to the 60s-staleness on-chain shim — a frozen roll must never imply a live price; show an explicit "stale price" state. |
| **Motion-restraint envelope: 100–500ms, reduced-motion support, pausable auto-update** | [NN/g durations](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/animation-duration/); [WCAG 2.2.2 / prefers-reduced-motion](https://www.boia.org/blog/what-to-know-about-the-css-prefers-reduced-motion-feature) | One `motion-safe:`/`motion-reduce:` discipline in Tailwind across web/components; flash/roll utilities no-op under reduced motion; no continuous ambient animation on /trade at all (Bloomberg-calm: things move only when data changes). This addresses accessibility and the documented landing HeroBlobs rAF-loop perf issue together. |
| **Temporal-density feedback for 5-second transactions** — in-place indeterminate progress, surrounding data stays live, optimistic pending rows visually distinct | [Matt Ström's latency ladder](https://mattstromawn.com/writing/ui-density/); [NN/g complex apps](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/complex-application-design/) | Every action spans a ~5s close plus signing: the button enters a progress state, a ghosted pending row appears (reduced opacity, badge, motion-safe shimmer), and the mark ticker keeps streaming so the screen never feels frozen. The single biggest "feels like Hyperliquid despite 5s finality" lever available without protocol changes. |
| **De-ink dense panels; elevation by surface lightness** — max 1px #262626-class borders, no zebra+borders together, hierarchy via weight (600 numerals / 400 labels) and the text ramp | [NN/g preattentive dashboards](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/dashboards-preattentive/), [Material elevation-as-lightness](https://design.google/library/material-design-dark-theme), Tufte via [UI Density](https://mattstromawn.com/writing/ui-density/), [Pencil & Paper 1px rule](https://www.pencilandpaper.io/articles/ux-pattern-analysis-enterprise-data-tables) | The surface stack is currently #000 → #0a0a0a → #1a1a1a with #262626 borders — functional, but the pure-black base wastes an elevation step and kills shadow cues. Nudge base to ~#0a0a0c and cards to ~#101013: near-black brand preserved, /trade's chart/OrderPanel/positions get three readable planes (a token-level elevation tweak that stays inside the near-black identity — but it touches brand base tokens, so get founder sign-off first). Keep the math-glyph strips on landing, /leaderboard, page frames — not inside OrderPanel or tables — at decorative contrast (≈Lc 15–25) so gold ornament never competes with gold interaction accents. |

**Anti-patterns**

- **Found in codebase**: white text on gold primary buttons (`--primary-foreground: 0 0% 100%` over the gold primary, web/app/globals.css lines 12–13) = 1.92:1 — fails WCAG AA ~2.3x over the line on every primary CTA. One-token fix (near-black on gold, 10.9:1).
- Encoding long/short or profit/loss in hue alone — #22c55e vs #ef4444 are 1.65:1 against each other, indistinguishable under red-green CVD; violates WCAG 1.4.1 without a sign/arrow/label.
- red-500 #ef4444 for small (12px) dense-table numerals — passes WCAG AA (5.26:1) but APCA Lc −37.7 is large/bold-only territory: a case where trusting WCAG 2.x on dark surfaces misleads.
- Dark golds as text — amber-700 #b45309 is 3.94:1 on cards (AA fail); amber-600 only ≥24px bold. Deep golds belong to borders/ornament.
- Monospace-everything for numbers — degrades reading texture and density; `tabular-nums` on Inter achieves alignment with no tradeoff (the monospace hack is [explicitly obsolete](https://dev.to/alanwest/tabular-numbers-in-css-font-variant-numeric-vs-monospace-hacks-25cn)).
- Oldstyle/proportional figures or centered numeric columns — breaks column comparison; right-align with fixed per-pair decimals.
- Hue-strobing price flashes — full-saturation repaints per tick, or >3 flashes/sec, risk WCAG 2.3.1 and train users to ignore the signal; coalesce and tint at low alpha.
- Continuous ambient motion on trading surfaces (the landing HeroBlobs rAF loop is the in-repo example) — violates the "calm until data changes" terminal norm, burns battery, ignores prefers-reduced-motion.
- Blanking or overlay-blocking panels during the ~5s confirmation — turns Stellar's cadence into perceived breakage; keep data visible with in-place progress.
- Pure #000 background with shadow-based elevation — shadows are invisible on pure black; and pure-white-on-pure-black at 21:1 is widely advised against for halation/eye strain — though note the halation claim is design-system guidance (Material, APCA author), **not peer-reviewed research**: strong convention, not law.
- "Compact mode" via font-size shrink instead of padding/row height — density systems reduce spacing while keeping type ≥12px.
- Fake depth styling on the own-orders panel — red/green depth bars imply order-book liquidity an oracle-priced venue does not have; a trust risk with pros.
- **Folklore flags**: "striped progress bars feel 11% faster" (single-study claim repeated in essays); "Robinhood-style digit rolls increase engagement" (no public evidence — optional polish behind reduced-motion).

---

### Latency & Transaction Feedback

**Key findings**

- **[NN/g's three response-time limits](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/response-times-3-important-limits/) are load-bearing**: <0.1s feels instantaneous, <1s keeps flow, ~10s is the attention limit. For 2–10s waits NN/g prescribes a looped/animated indicator plus status; percent-done only for 10s+. Noether's ~5s inclusion sits in the "flow interrupted but attention keepable" band — staged active feedback, not a percent bar, escalating if it crosses ~10s.
- **Stellar's wait is predictable** (~5–6s close, definitive expiry via timebounds), converting NN/g's "high variability" problem into an opportunity: an honest time-estimated indicator ("confirming in ledger, ~5s") instead of the indeterminate spinner every EVM dApp is stuck with — and a provably dead, safe-to-retry state once the timebound passes, which most chains cannot offer ([Stellar submit-and-wait](https://developers.stellar.org/docs/build/guides/transactions/submit-transaction-wait-js), [getTransaction](https://developers.stellar.org/network/soroban-rpc/methods/getTransaction)).
- **The Doherty threshold (400ms) is largely folklore**: the 1982 IBM paper argued economic value of sub-second response, but the 400ms "threshold" popularized by [Laws of UX](https://lawsofux.com/doherty-threshold/) is disputed — critics trace the framing to the TV show Halt and Catch Fire ([Flashover critique](https://www.flashover.blog/posts/dohertys-threshold-is-a-lie/), [HN provenance thread](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=24031938)). Anchor to NN/g's 0.1s/1s/10s; the directional claim survives, the magic number does not.
- **Skeleton evidence is genuinely mixed**: [Bill Chung's 160-participant studies](https://uxdesign.cc/what-you-should-know-about-skeleton-screens-a820c45a571a) found skeletons only slightly better than spinners and only with a slow left-to-right wave (pulsing opacity and fast motion performed worse); [Viget's 136-participant study](https://www.viget.com/articles/a-bone-to-pick-with-skeleton-screens) found skeletons WORST on perceived wait, hypothesizing novelty draws attention — they work in familiar UIs with short waits ([NN/g Skeleton Screens 101](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/skeleton-screens/)). The circulating "30–50% faster perceived" stats are vendor **folklore** with no traceable primary study.
- **Optimistic UI has strict preconditions** ([Mishunov, Smashing Magazine](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2016/11/true-lies-of-optimistic-user-interfaces/)): success probability ~97–99%, failure communicated within ~2s, never when the outcome payload matters beyond binary success. Oracle-priced Soroban perps fit unusually well because simulateTransaction is a mandatory pre-submit step — insufficient margin, stale price (#30), and deviation-band (#81) failures are detectable BEFORE the wallet popup, making post-sign failures rare enough to justify optimistic pending rows.
- **Critical nuance**: the Noether Router relays a fresh price attestation in the same tx, so the actual entry price is the oracle price AT INCLUSION, not at click. Optimistic UI must show an estimated entry ("≈ $64,231") and reconcile with the true entry_price from the position_opened event — an exact optimistic price that later shifts destroys trust with pros.
- **Every leading perp DEX eliminated per-action wallet popups via delegated session keys**, with a consistent security contract: [Hyperliquid API/agent wallets](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/for-developers/api/nonces-and-api-wallets) (sign-only, cannot withdraw, up to 3 named + 1 unnamed, revocable, auto-pruned); [gTrade 1CT](https://docs.gains.trade/gtrade-leveraged-trading/one-click-trading-1ct) (key derived from one personalSign + PIN, encrypted in localStorage, approved as a trading-contract delegate, enable/disable/fund/export/reset in a dropdown); [dYdX trading keys](https://help.dydx.trade/en/articles/267486-api-trading-keys-creating-a-new-key-on-the-front-end) (STARK keys in localStorage after one onboarding signature); [Drift Swift](https://www.drift.trade/updates/introducing-swift-protocol-a-new-trading-standard-for-solana) (signed messages relayed gaslessly). Table stakes for the Hyperliquid-native audience.
- **dYdX v4 and Drift mask chain latency architecturally**: the UI's primary acknowledgment comes from an off-chain system ([dYdX indexer shows orders "immediately"](https://docs.dydx.exchange/concepts-limit-order-book-and-matching); Drift makers see orders 100–500ms pre-landing), with chain finality as background reconciliation. Transferable lesson: treat the fastest trustworthy signal as the UI event and the ledger as settlement of record.
- **Feedback-channel discipline** ([NN/g indicators/validations/notifications](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/indicators-validations-notifications/), [LogRocket toast guidelines](https://blog.logrocket.com/ux-design/toast-notifications/), [Carbon notification pattern](https://carbondesignsystem.com/patterns/notification-pattern/)): feedback must appear near the locus of attention; toasts are passive confirmations only — one at a time, 2–6s, explicitly a BAD channel for errors; errors need persistent inline placement with specific, constructive, non-blaming recovery copy ([NN/g error-message guidelines](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/error-message-guidelines/)). One toast per tx, updated through its stages.
- **[WCAG 2.2 SC 4.1.3 (Status Messages, AA)](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/status-messages.html)** requires tx status changes to be programmatically announced (role=status/aria-live=polite for progress, role=alert for failures), with the live region in the DOM from initial load; progress bars need role=progressbar.
- **CEX convention**: order-confirmation dialogs are a preference — [Bybit lets traders deactivate confirmation windows](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/How-to-activate-deactivate-trade-option-settings), Binance likewise; instant ack pairs with optional sounds, and the orders/positions table (not toasts) is the authoritative acknowledgment surface.
- **A University of Nebraska-Lincoln study cited by [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/progress-indicators/)**: users shown a progress indicator waited on average 3x longer and reported higher satisfaction — the strongest single evidence that Noether's 5s waits are a feedback-design problem, not a hard latency ceiling.

**Pattern library**

| Pattern | Who uses it | How Noether applies it |
|---|---|---|
| **Four-stage lifecycle on a morphing submit button** — Idle → "Confirm in Freighter…" → "Submitting…" → "Confirming ~5s" → success flash | Uniswap ("Confirm in wallet"), MetaMask-integrated dApps; stages map to [Stellar's sendTransaction→poll flow](https://developers.stellar.org/docs/build/guides/transactions/submit-transaction-wait-js); placement per [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/indicators-validations-notifications/) | Implement in web/components/trading OrderPanel: the gold CTA morphs through stages instead of spawning a modal — the button is the locus of attention, toast is secondary. Stage 2→3 is the long pole (~5s); label it honestly. Only the submit control locks (also prevents double-submit, which on-chain means a duplicate position). Reuse the same stateful-button component on /vault deposit/withdraw, /vaults deposit, /faucet claim, /referrals claim so the whole app speaks one tx language. |
| **Honest time-based progress for the inclusion window, escalating at 10s** | [NN/g progress research](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/progress-indicators/) (indicator → ~3x wait tolerance); [Stellar poll pattern](https://developers.stellar.org/network/soroban-rpc/methods/getTransaction) supplies timing | A thin 4px gold strip animating over expected inclusion (~6s p50, tuned from observed api-gateway latency) instead of an infinite spinner — a luminous gold sweep on near-black reads premium, and the math-glyph strip can double as the indeterminate fallback texture. If unresolved when the bar completes: indeterminate shimmer + "Taking longer than usual…"; at ~10s escalate to "Still confirming — view on explorer" with the tx hash. Never an unlabeled spinner past 10s. |
| **Optimistic pending row with estimate semantics, reconciled from chain events** — ghost row at ~60% opacity, pulsing gold dot, "Pending" badge, entry "≈ mark", PnL blank | [dYdX v4 indexer-first display](https://docs.dydx.exchange/concepts-limit-order-book-and-matching), Uniswap/MetaMask pending balances; rules from [Mishunov](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2016/11/true-lies-of-optimistic-user-interfaces/) | Noether qualifies for optimism precisely because it is oracle-priced: no depth, no partial fills — simulation-passing opens almost always succeed. But entry price is set at inclusion via the Router's fresh attestation, so the ghost row MUST show "≈" until position_opened returns the real entry_price (the event tuple carries it). On failure, mark the row "Failed" in place before removing — never silently vanish it. Wire reconciliation through tradeStore + the indexer/API WS channel, not per-row polling. Same pattern for the own-orders panel: a just-placed limit order appears instantly as pending — honest, since that panel only ever shows the user's own orders. |
| **Simulation-gated optimism: pre-flight errors before the wallet popup** | Soroban's required simulate-then-sign flow (Stellar docs); [NN/g error guidelines](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/error-message-guidelines/); Baymard inline-error placement | Extend packages/tx-builders + web/lib/stellar/market.ts so every builder returns typed simulation errors; OrderPanel renders them inline under the offending control in the shadcn form style. Map contract codes to trading language: insufficient collateral → "Add at least 12.4 USDC or reduce size"; #30 → "Price feed is refreshing — retry in a few seconds"; #81 → "Price moved outside the safety band — new mark $X". Never request a signature you know will revert — this is what makes the optimistic ghost row statistically safe. |
| **One-click trading via a delegated agent key with a visible kill switch** — in-browser keypair (derivable from one wallet signature + PIN), encrypted in localStorage, authorized on-chain as a trade-only delegate, funded with a little XLM | [Hyperliquid agent wallets](https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/for-developers/api/nonces-and-api-wallets), [gTrade 1CT](https://docs.gains.trade/gtrade-leveraged-trading/one-click-trading-1ct) ("nothing different other than not having to confirm transactions in a wallet"), [dYdX](https://help.dydx.trade/en/articles/267486-api-trading-keys-creating-a-new-key-on-the-front-end), [Drift Swift](https://www.drift.trade/updates/introducing-swift-protocol-a-new-trading-standard-for-solana) | Stellar lacks EVM-style session keys for classic G-accounts, so implement as (a) the market contract accepting an authorized delegate address per trader (open/close/cancel only — mirror vault_factory's leader-proxy pattern), or (b) longer-term a Soroban smart-wallet/passkey route. The Router already bundles oracle refresh + trade atomically, so an agent key makes the flow one click. Surface as a header toggle next to the wallet chip with a first-run explainer stating the Hyperliquid-standard guarantees (trade-only, this-device-only, revoke anytime). With the popup gone, the remaining ~5s is fully maskable by the optimistic patterns — the single highest-leverage latency fix available, and the honest answer to popup fatigue for an active trader signing dozens of txs per session. |
| **One stage-updating toast per tx, with explorer link — never stacks, never toast-only errors** | Uniswap/MetaMask pending-toast convention; sonner/shadcn toast.promise built for update-in-place; rules: [LogRocket](https://blog.logrocket.com/ux-design/toast-notifications/), [Carbon](https://carbondesignsystem.com/patterns/notification-pattern/), [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/indicators-validations-notifications/) | Adopt sonner's toast.promise keyed by tx hash: Submitted → Confirmed / Failed; success auto-dismisses ~4s, failure persists and is mirrored inline. Every state includes "View on stellar.expert." Brand styling: pending = gold pulsing glyph, success = green check (preserving long/short semantics), failure = red + Retry. Bottom-right on /trade so it never covers chart or positions. The toast is secondary; button and ghost row are primary — a pro watching the table should never NEED the toast. |
| **Wallet rejection is a neutral reset, not an error** | Uniswap-class dApps special-case the user-rejected code; rationale: [NN/g error guidelines](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/error-message-guidelines/), [Mishunov proportionality](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2016/11/true-lies-of-optimistic-user-interfaces/) | In the web/lib/stellar signing wrappers, branch on stellar-wallets-kit's user-rejection error before the generic path: quietly return the button to idle, keep ticket contents, at most a muted "Signature cancelled." No red, no "Transaction failed!", no error-log noise. Matters doubly for LOBSTR-via-WalletConnect (slower signing round-trips, more cancellations) and on /faucet and /referrals where newcomers cancel while learning what signing is. |
| **Failure taxonomy with distinct recovery paths and preserved ticket state** — (1) contract revert → specific inline reason + Retry re-simulating at the current mark; (2) timeout/not-found → "Checking status…" until the timebound passes, then a definitive "Order expired unfilled — nothing was executed. Retry?"; (3) RPC/network → "Connection issue — your funds are untouched" + auto-retry with backoff | Stellar timebounds make "definitively dead, safe to retry" provable — an advantage over Ethereum's stuck-tx limbo ([Uniswap pending-tx support](https://support.uniswap.org/hc/en-us/articles/7422909292813-My-transaction-has-been-pending-for-a-long-time-What-can-I-do)); [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/error-message-guidelines/); [Baymard](https://baymard.com/learn/checkout-flow-ux-optimization) (never make users re-enter data) | Set explicit timebounds (~30–60s) on every Noether tx so the UI can promise "nothing was executed" after expiry — copy that matters enormously when the retry re-prices at a moved oracle mark. Surface the re-priced mark in the retry affordance ("Retry at ≈ $64,310"): staleness (#30) and deviation (#81) failures resolve within seconds on the keeper's next attestation. Keep failed rows visible in the table for a beat. Never claim failure while the outcome is unknown. |
| **Skeletons only for familiar read surfaces; action feedback never uses skeletons** — slow left-to-right wave shimmer | [Chung](https://uxdesign.cc/what-you-should-know-about-skeleton-screens-a820c45a571a) (wave beat pulse; 65% perceived wave-as-faster), [Viget](https://www.viget.com/articles/a-bone-to-pick-with-skeleton-screens) (worst in novel UIs), [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/skeleton-screens/) | Skeleton the positions/orders tables on first mount, /portfolio balance cards, /vaults marketplace cards, /leaderboard rows (stable layouts, repeat visits, indexer-backed reads sometimes slow on public RPC). Shimmer in dim gold-on-charcoal (subtle luminance step, not white) to avoid the attention-grabbing flicker Viget found. Never represent a pending transaction as a skeleton — actions need status semantics ("Confirming"), not layout placeholders. Market-stat headers load fast with 3 pairs: no skeleton, just reserve space against layout shift. |
| **Sub-100ms local acknowledgment + double-submit lockout** | [NN/g response limits](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/response-times-3-important-limits/); [Baymard](https://baymard.com/learn/checkout-flow-ux-optimization) double-submission prevention; every CEX ticket disables on submit | Audit OrderPanel for synchronous re-render on click (Zustand update before await); keep fee-tier/margin recomputes under 100ms as sizes change. Lockout keyed by tx attempt, not global: after submit, the trader can immediately draft the next order for another of the 3 pairs while the first confirms — pros ladder orders; a globally frozen panel for 5s per trade caps throughput at ~12 trades/min and reads amateur. The 400ms Doherty figure is **folklore**; the direction (fast local ack) holds. |
| **Order-confirmation dialog as a preference: default on, one toggle from off** — review shows pair, side, size, leverage, est. entry, liq price, fees; "Don't ask again" | [Bybit trade settings](https://www.bybit.com/en/help-center/article/How-to-activate-deactivate-trade-option-settings), Binance desktop setting | Persist per-wallet in localStorage alongside walletStore. With 1CT on and confirmations off, the flow collapses to true one-click; with both on, newcomers get a safety net — the cleanest progressive-disclosure lever between the two audiences. The review modal doubles as the education surface (first place a newcomer sees liq price and the 1% maintenance margin). Wire the same preference into /vaults leader trade actions, which proxy the same market ops. |
| **Programmatic status announcements (WCAG 4.1.3) for the whole lifecycle** | [W3C](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/status-messages.html); Carbon-class design systems; sonner ships ARIA live wiring | Mount one persistent visually-hidden live region at app root (web/app/layout.tsx): role=status (polite, atomic) announcing "Order submitted", "Position opened, entry 64,231"; role=alert for failures; role=progressbar on the inclusion strip. Live regions must exist in the DOM from initial load — injecting on first tx silently fails in most screen readers. Cheap now, near-impossible to retrofit; the same state machine can feed optional CEX-style order sounds for traders watching the chart, not the button. |
| **Fastest-trustworthy-signal reconciliation: WS/event ack first, ledger as settlement** — optimistic local → RPC poll SUCCESS (~5s) → indexer WS confirms projection; disagreements resolved by the chain read | [dYdX v4 indexer architecture](https://docs.dydx.exchange/concepts-limit-order-book-and-matching), [Drift Swift](https://www.drift.trade/updates/introducing-swift-protocol-a-new-trading-standard-for-solana), [Stellar pollTransaction](https://developers.stellar.org/docs/build/guides/transactions/submit-transaction-wait-js) as authoritative | Noether already has all three layers (local store, direct RPC poll in web/lib/stellar, api/indexer WS channels) — the pattern is deciding precedence once and encoding it in tradeStore so /trade and /portfolio never flash contradictory position states. If the WS event lags the poll, show confirmed from the poll and let the indexer catch up silently. Also guards against the known prod failure mode where a stale indexer (D-4-class incidents) would otherwise blank the UI: direct RPC poll keeps single-tx feedback working when projections lag. |

**Anti-patterns**

- Global blocking overlay/modal while a tx confirms — freezes the terminal ~5s per action, caps throughput, violates the CEX mental model (the table just updates); lock only the submitting control.
- Three stacked toasts per transaction (signed/submitted/confirmed) — update one toast through its stages.
- Errors delivered only as a toast — NN/g explicitly calls toasts a bad error channel; failures persist inline near the ticket or row.
- An unlabeled infinite spinner for the confirmation wait — NN/g limits spinners to 2–10s with status text; on a chain with a known ~5s cadence it also wastes Stellar's predictability advantage.
- Optimistically displaying an exact fill/entry price before inclusion — the Router re-prices at inclusion with a fresh attestation; the number WILL drift. Show "≈" until position_opened returns entry_price, or pros read the shifting number as a rigged fill.
- Silently rolling back optimistic state on failure — Mishunov's core rule: the lie must be confessed; a vanished ghost row is worse than no optimism.
- Red "Transaction failed" when the user cancels the wallet prompt — rejection is intent, not error; blaming copy punishes newcomers learning what signing is.
- Declaring failure on timeout while the tx might still be included — use timebounds to reach a provable "expired, nothing executed" state before offering retry (blind retries risk double positions).
- Skeleton-screening everything — Viget found skeletons worst on perceived wait in unfamiliar interfaces; never skeleton an in-flight action or first-run/marketing surfaces.
- Citing the Doherty threshold (400ms) as established science — provenance disputed; anchor to NN/g's 0.1s/1s/10s. Likewise "skeletons feel 30% faster" has no traceable primary study. **Folklore.**
- Clearing/resetting the order ticket after a failed or rejected tx — input preservation is a top abandonment lever (Baymard); retry re-simulates the same values at the fresh mark.
- A wallet popup for every micro-action (cancel order, update SL/TP) with no session-key alternative — the entire comparable set (Hyperliquid, gTrade, dYdX, Drift) eliminated per-action popups; for a pro-first 3-pair terminal this is the difference between feeling like a CEX and feeling like a dApp demo.
- Toast/shimmer motion at full gold brightness during waits — high-luminance flashes on near-black grab attention exactly the way Viget found harmful; keep pending motion low-luminance, reserve bright gold for terminal success states.

---

### Mobile & Responsive

**Key findings**

- **Mobile perp usage skews toward monitoring, adjusting SL/TP, and emergency closing**; desktop is where multi-position risk is managed. Evidence is practitioner/broker-guide grade ([ForexMechanics](https://forexmechanics.com/platforms-tools/mobile-trading/), Axi, prop-firm guides) plus UNSW research showing mobile access increases trend-chasing — **directional, not RCT-grade**, but consistent. Design the mobile trade page around position health first, order entry second.
- **The converged small-screen layout** (Binance app, dYdX iOS, Hyperliquid mobile web): single-column chart-first page + persistent bottom Long/Short bar + order ticket in a bottom sheet + positions directly below the chart ([dYdX mobile guide](https://www.dydx.xyz/blog/new-dydx-mobile-trading-experience)). Noether has the skeleton (MobileTradeBar, W-7/P4-16) but renders the 1,257-line OrderPanel in a CENTERED modal (verified: web/components/ui/Modal.tsx line 85, `items-center justify-center`) — the single largest gap vs convention.
- **[NN/g bottom-sheet guidelines](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/bottom-sheet/)**: sheets are for quick contextual controls, need a visible X (never swipe-only dismissal), must never stack, can start half-height and expand. NN/g also cautions that bottom placement does NOT guarantee thumb reachability — the "bottom 60% thumb zone" heatmaps in fintech blogs are **folklore** extrapolated from Hoober's 2013 observational study.
- **[NN/g mobile-table guidance](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/mobile-tables/)**: transform rows to cards on small screens (Noether's PositionsList already does `hidden lg:block` table + `lg:hidden` card stack), or lock the leftmost identifying column, signal overflow with cut-off elements, sticky headers.
- **Touch targets**: [WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/target-size-minimum.html) sets a 24x24px legal floor, but Apple HIG says 44pt and Android 48dp ([LogRocket comparison](https://blog.logrocket.com/ux-design/all-accessible-touch-target-sizes/)); money-moving actions use platform numbers. [Baymard](https://baymard.com/blog/mobile-touch-keyboards): correct touch keyboards (inputmode/pattern) are among the highest-ROI mobile form fixes — 60% of sites still get it wrong; numeric keypads measurably cut typos ([cheat sheet](https://baymard.com/labs/touch-keyboard-types)).
- **For on-chain latency, the established dApp answer** ([Evil Martians](https://evilmartians.com/chronicles/decentralized-app-design-first-aid-common-dapp-ui-pains)) is a visible pending-tx lifecycle: instant tap acknowledgment (prevents double-submission), pending state, success with explorer link, optimistic UI reconciled against chain state. A 5s wait is short enough to animate honestly.
- **PWA/mobile-web is the pragmatically correct vehicle**: [Apple App Review Guideline 3.1.5](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/) requires crypto-exchange apps to have licensing in every region offered — blocking an unlicensed DEX from a first-party native iOS app (GMX ships mobile-web only — [Coin Bureau](https://coinbureau.com/analysis/dex-platforms-for-advanced-traders); Hyperliquid grew web-first with third-party wallet apps filling the gap — [Splash](https://splashwallet.xyz/learn/hyperliquid-mobile-app), [goodcryptoX](https://goodcrypto.app/hyperliquid-mobile-app/)). Cost: iOS PWA push requires Home-Screen install (iOS 16.4+), web-push opt-in runs ~16% vs 40–70% native, no background data-only push ([MagicBell](https://www.magicbell.com/blog/pwa-ios-limitations-safari-support-complete-guide), [Mobiloud](https://www.mobiloud.com/blog/progressive-web-apps-ios)) — mobile liquidation alerting is structurally weaker than a CEX app and needs email/Telegram supplements.
- **TradingView lightweight-charts hijacks vertical touch scroll by default**; `handleScroll.vertTouchDrag`/`horzTouchDrag` must be configured ([API](https://tradingview.github.io/lightweight-charts/docs/api/interfaces/HandleScrollOptions), [TradingView mobile docs](https://www.tradingview.com/charting-library-docs/latest/mobile_specifics/)) or the 400px chart becomes a scroll trap mid-page.
- **dYdX's two-mode iOS app (Simplified vs Pro, switchable)** is the reference progressive-disclosure implementation ([dYdX Help](https://help.dydx.trade/en/articles/273843-simplified-ui-on-ios)); for pro-first Noether the mapping inverts: one pro-complete ticket with advanced controls collapsed under a single "Advanced" accordion, not a separate beginner mode.
- **Deliberate-commitment gestures** ([Robinhood swipe-up-to-submit](https://robinhood.com/us/en/support/articles/futures-orders/), press-and-hold) add intentional friction before irreversible actions — but every Noether trade already ends in a wallet prompt, which IS the commitment step; adding swipe-to-confirm would double-confirm and slow pros. The correct adaptation: a complete pre-sign summary (est. oracle fill, fees, liq price) above the submit button ([Lollypop](https://lollypop.design/blog/2026/june/trading-app-design/)).

**Pattern library**

| Pattern | Who uses it | How Noether applies it |
|---|---|---|
| **Persistent bottom Long/Short bar on the trade page** — two full-width direction-colored buttons ≥44pt, one tap from anywhere | Binance app, dYdX iOS, Hyperliquid mobile web; codified in [Lollypop's 2026 guide](https://lollypop.design/blog/2026/june/trading-app-design/) | Already shipped (web/components/trading/MobileTradeBar.tsx, lg:hidden fixed bar with safe-area padding at line 36 — keep). Refine within brand: emerald/red for direction (PnL semantics), gold reserved for focus/selection; add a one-line strip above the buttons with the live oracle mark + funding countdown for the selected pair — oracle price IS the execution price (no book depth), and traders need it at the moment of commitment. |
| **Order ticket in a true bottom sheet, not a centered modal** — rounded top, drag handle + always-visible X, opens ~60–70% height, expands to ~85dvh, internal scroll, scrim | dYdX iOS, Binance app; rules from [NN/g bottom sheets](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/bottom-sheet/) | Highest-leverage mobile change: web/components/ui/Modal.tsx line 85 currently centers the 1,257-line OrderPanel — on a phone it floats mid-screen with cramped internal scroll. Add a sheet variant (mobile: slide-from-bottom, max-h-[85dvh], handle + X per NN/g; desktop unchanged); MobileTradeBar uses it. Handle/border in the brand's white/10 hairline on near-black. 85dvh, not 90vh, so iOS Safari's collapsing toolbar never clips the submit button. Dismissal via X, back gesture, and scrim tap — never swipe-only. |
| **Pro-complete ticket with one "Advanced" accordion** (inverted dYdX two-mode) — always-needed fields first; limit/stop-limit/trailing, TIF, post-only, reduce-only folded | [dYdX Simplified vs Pro](https://help.dydx.trade/en/articles/273843-simplified-ui-on-ios); [Lollypop](https://lollypop.design/blog/2026/june/trading-app-design/) expandable secondary controls | Pro-first means no dumbed-down mode — collapse instead. Keep OrderPanel's reactive fee-tier preview visible in the collapsed state (pros check fees). Persist accordion state in tradeStore so a pro who opens Advanced keeps it open. With 3 pairs, the asset selector is three large segment chips in the sheet header (gold-highlighted active pair), not a dropdown. |
| **Pre-sign summary instead of a second confirmation step** — full consequence summary (est. fill, size, fees, liq price, margin mode) directly above one submit button; the wallet prompt is the deliberate gesture | Adaptation of [Robinhood swipe-to-submit](https://robinhood.com/us/en/support/articles/futures-orders/) + Lollypop's "deliberate physical interaction" to wallet-signed dApps; Hyperliquid shows liq/fees inline then one click | OrderPanel's ORDER SUMMARY block already computes this — ensure it is the last thing visible above submit inside the sheet (never scrolled away), with est. entry from the live Noeracle mark and a staleness badge when the SSE stream lags (frontend falls back to the on-chain shim). Preserves speed for Hyperliquid migrants; the 5s ledger + wallet prompt already out-frictions any CEX. |
| **Explicit pending-tx lifecycle for 5-second ledgers** — (1) instant disable + "Confirm in wallet…", (2) "Submitting… ~5s" + indeterminate bar, (3) optimistic ghost row in Positions, (4) success toast + explorer link, (5) decoded contract error + re-enable on failure | [Evil Martians](https://evilmartians.com/chronicles/decentralized-app-design-first-aid-common-dapp-ui-pains); [Netguru](https://www.netguru.com/blog/financial-app-design) (immediate acknowledgment prevents double-taps) | Noether's core mobile-feel problem: every action is a Soroban tx. Wire the states into MobileTradeBar's sheet submit and PositionsList close/SL-TP modals. The ghost row matters most on mobile, where users close the sheet and expect the position to appear — reconcile against position_opened or the next getPositions poll. Show "Confirm in wallet…" distinctly from "Submitting…" because LOBSTR/WalletConnect signing can take 10s+ of app-switching; gold shimmer on the pending row fits the existing motion language. |
| **Positions as cards leading with PnL and liquidation distance** — two hero numbers (signed uPnL with ▲/▼ or Long/Short text; distance-to-liq); secondary metrics in a two-column grid; full-width ≥44pt Close and SL/TP buttons | [NN/g card transformation](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/mobile-tables/); dYdX iOS position panel with risk bands; Binance/Bybit app lists | PositionsList.tsx already splits table→card — keep, and audit hierarchy: since mobile is monitoring/closing, PnL + liq distance must be the largest text on the card (tabular figures read well on gold-on-dark). dYdX-style risk banding (low <20%, medium ≥20%, high >40% margin usage) can reuse CrossMarginBanner's margin-ratio math as a colored edge strip — amber fits the brand and is CVD-safe when paired with a % label. |
| **One-tap close with percentage chips, defaulting to 100%** — position summary sheet, 25/50/75/100% chips, single submit; emergency exit = two taps + one signature, no typing | dYdX iOS (close → percentage, default 100%), Hyperliquid Market-Close, Robinhood minimal close | Convert the existing Close modal to the sheet variant; keep 100%-only for now (the market contract closes in full — no size argument until the C2 WASM refit reinstates it) but make it zero-typing; add 25/50/75% chips when partial close returns. Do NOT stack it over the order sheet (NN/g: never stack sheets) — closing from the Positions tab already avoids this. Chips ≥44pt; any manual size field gets inputMode=decimal. |
| **Chart gesture containment** — one-finger vertical drags scroll the page; only horizontal drags/pinch touch the chart; long-press for crosshair | [lightweight-charts HandleScrollOptions](https://tradingview.github.io/lightweight-charts/docs/api/interfaces/HandleScrollOptions), [TradingView mobile specifics](https://www.tradingview.com/charting-library-docs/latest/mobile_specifics/); Binance/Bybit apps behave this way | TradingChart.tsx sits in a 400px block mid-page (app/trade/page.tsx line 614) — exact scroll-trap geometry. Set `vertTouchDrag: false` (keep horizontal pan + pinch); verify the price scale isn't pinch-hijacking. A ~5-line config change with outsized feel; with 3 oracle-priced pairs the chart is context, not execution — favor page scrollability. |
| **Replace the mobile "Order Book" with an Open Orders chip — no fake depth** | GMX and Jupiter Perps (oracle/pool-priced) ship no order book on any screen size ([Coin Bureau](https://coinbureau.com/analysis/dex-platforms-for-advanced-traders)) | The OrderBook panel only ever shows the user's own pending limit orders and is already hidden below xl (app/trade/page.tsx line 554) — make that the permanent mobile posture, not a responsive casualty: badge the Orders tab with the open-order count; each row cancellable with a ≥44pt target. Never reintroduce a depth-like visualization on mobile; Hyperliquid-trained pros will read it as fake. |
| **Sticky-header tables with locked first column for history** (tablet/desktop-narrow) — sticky thead + leftmost identifying column, horizontal scroll inside its own overflow-x-auto, right-edge fade signaling overflow | [NN/g mobile tables](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/mobile-tables/) (Samsung comparison tables cited) | PositionsList's desktop table already has a sticky thead (line 200) — extend to TradeHistory.tsx and OrdersList.tsx: sticky asset/side first column, tabular numerals, right-edge gradient fade in near-black. Every table inside its own overflow-x-auto so the page body never scrolls horizontally (matches the tab-strip treatment at line 596). |
| **Bottom tab bar for app-level navigation (4–5 destinations)** — Trade / Portfolio / Vaults / More (referrals, leaderboard, faucet, api-keys) | [NN/g mobile navigation](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/mobile-navigation-patterns/), Apple HIG/Material cap at 5, Binance app; [Smashing golden rules](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2016/11/the-golden-rules-of-mobile-navigation-design/) | Noether has 9+ surfaces but only header nav (components/layout/Header.tsx). Conflict to resolve: /trade's bottom is owned by MobileTradeBar — either hide the tab bar on /trade (Binance-style immersive trade) or stack the Long/Short bar directly above it; do NOT ship two competing fixed bars without a deliberate hierarchy. Active tab in brand gold on near-black. |
