| **Numeric touch keyboards and platform-size targets on all money inputs** — inputMode="decimal" (+pattern fallback), autocorrect/autocapitalize off; steppers, chips, sliders ≥44x44pt | [Baymard touch-keyboard research](https://baymard.com/blog/mobile-touch-keyboards) + [cheat sheet](https://baymard.com/labs/touch-keyboard-types); [WCAG 2.5.8](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/target-size-minimum.html) as floor only; [LogRocket sizes](https://blog.logrocket.com/ux-design/all-accessible-touch-target-sizes/) | Audit OrderPanel collateral/limit-price/SL-TP inputs and PositionsList's SL/TP modals for inputMode=decimal — a mistyped price on a 10x position is a real-money error class. Leverage selection on mobile = chips (2x/5x/10x) + fine slider rather than typing. One-day, zero-design-risk change fully inside the brand. |
| **Installable PWA with narrowly-scoped push (margin health), not a native app** — manifest + service worker; custom install prompt at engaged moments; push scoped to margin warnings, liquidation, fills; email/Telegram supplements | GMX (mobile-web only), Hyperliquid (web-first; third-party apps fill native — [Splash](https://splashwallet.xyz/learn/hyperliquid-mobile-app), [goodcryptoX](https://goodcrypto.app/hyperliquid-mobile-app/)); mechanics: [MagicBell](https://www.magicbell.com/blog/pwa-ios-limitations-safari-support-complete-guide), [Mobiloud](https://www.mobiloud.com/blog/progressive-web-apps-ios); constraint: [Apple 3.1.5](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/) | A first-party native app is a legal non-starter pre-licensing — PWA is not a fallback, it is the correct vehicle, and it matches the Next.js 14 stack (manifest + service worker in web/app). Prompt install from /portfolio or after a successful trade, not on landing. Plan honestly: ~16% web-push opt-in vs 40–70% native and no background data-only push means liquidation alerts also need a keeper-driven email/Telegram channel; the margin-health push is still worth it for the monitoring-first mobile audience. |
| **Mobile page order optimized for monitoring** — risk banner → chart → positions (default-open) → orders/history; ticket lives only in the bottom bar + sheet; equity and free margin within one viewport of top | dYdX iOS (position metrics below chart; portfolio-centric tabs); broker guidance on mobile jobs ([ForexMechanics](https://forexmechanics.com/platforms-tools/mobile-trading/)) | app/trade/page.tsx already stacks chart → tabs → Market Info below lg; move CrossMarginBanner above the chart on mobile when the user has positions, and default the tab strip to Positions when any exist. Given 5s tx latency, a trader reacting to a margin warning needs Close reachable in ≤2 taps from page load — this ordering plus the card Close button achieves it. Demote Market Info (OI/volume) to last; it serves curiosity, not the monitoring job. |
| **dvh + safe-area-inset for every fixed bottom element** — env(safe-area-inset-bottom) padding, dvh heights with @supports fallback, max-h-[85dvh] sheets over 100vh | Documented WebKit behavior ([bug 261185](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=261185), [Luke Channings' analysis](https://lukechannings.com/blog/2021-06-09-does-safari-15-fix-the-vh-bug/)); standard in Binance/Hyperliquid mobile web | MobileTradeBar already pads with env(safe-area-inset-bottom) (line 36) — carry the same treatment into the new Modal.tsx sheet variant (max-h-[85dvh], pb-safe) and any future tab bar. Test iOS Safari with the toolbar expanded AND collapsed: the submit button inside the order sheet is exactly the element the 100vh bug eats, and a hidden submit during a 5s-latency flow reads as a frozen app. |

**Anti-patterns**

- Centered modal for mobile order entry (current state: Modal.tsx line 85 centers the OrderPanel) — violates the bottom-sheet convention of every reference product; cramped internal scroll of a 1,257-line panel; submit floats mid-screen.
- Swipe-to-confirm or a second review screen on top of wallet signing — Robinhood's gesture exists because its taps are instantly binding; on Noether the wallet prompt already forces deliberation; double-confirming on a 5s chain churns the pro audience.
- Swipe-only dismissal or gesture-only affordances on sheets — accessibility failures and gesture ambiguity (NN/g); always render a visible X, honor back.
- Stacking bottom sheets (e.g., SL/TP editor as a second sheet above the order sheet) — NN/g flags this as a navigation-model breaker; route SL/TP edits from the position card.
- Rendering an "Order Book" panel on mobile at all — Noether's book only contains the user's own limit orders; the depth-panel idiom fakes liquidity and spends the scarcest real estate on the least-true panel.
- Leaving lightweight-charts default touch handling on — one-finger vertical drags trapped by the 400px chart mid-page (fix via handleScroll.vertTouchDrag=false).
- Designing to thumb-zone folklore — "60% of screen is thumb-reachable" heatmaps extrapolate Hoober's 2013 observational study; NN/g explicitly cautions bottom placement doesn't guarantee reachability. Soft prior, not law. **Folklore.**
- Unverifiable vendor stats as requirements — "72–78% of crypto trades happen on mobile" (crypto-dev-shop blogs) has no primary source; don't let it cut desktop-pro scope. Treat "mobile = monitoring only" as directional practitioner evidence, not proof. **Folklore.**
- Assuming PWA push ≈ native push — iOS requires Home-Screen install before the permission prompt exists; ~16% opt-in vs 40–70% native; no data-only background push. A liquidation-warning system relying solely on web push silently fails most users.
- Shipping a first-party native iOS app before licensing — [Apple 3.1.5](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/) requires exchange licensing per region; rejection or takedown is the expected outcome for an unlicensed DEX.
- 100vh-sized sheets and bottom bars on iOS Safari — the toolbar-overlap bug puts the submit button under browser chrome at the highest-stakes moment; use dvh + safe-area-inset (MobileTradeBar already does this right).
- Color-only direction/PnL encoding — keep Long/Short text labels and ▲/▼ glyphs alongside emerald/red (PositionCard mostly does this — audit chips and toasts).
- 24px-only touch targets on money actions — WCAG 2.2's 24px is a legal AA floor, not a usability target; Close/SL-TP/Long/Short need 44pt+ given the error cost at 10x leverage.
- Frozen or silent submit states during the ~5s close + wallet app-switch — no instant acknowledgment invites double-taps and duplicate positions; equally bad is faking instant fills with no pending state, which pros distrust the first time an optimistic fill diverges from the on-chain event.
- Letting the N+1 getPositions/getOrders pattern drive mobile refresh — on cellular, the per-position RPC fan-out makes the monitoring loop (the core mobile job) slow and rate-limit-prone; mobile reads should come from the indexer-backed API where possible.

---

## Recommendations Roadmap

This chapter converts the full audit (11 critical, ~50 high, and the data-integrity/medium/low sweeps) plus the external benchmark research into one phased plan. It honors the three founder decisions: the gold-on-dark brand is fixed (every visual fix below is opacity, weight, or token discipline — never a palette change); the audience is pro-first with progressive disclosure for Stellar-native newcomers; and the benchmark set is perp DEXs + CEX futures + retail-simple apps. It also respects the two physics constraints: pricing is oracle-based (Noeracle) with no order book, and every action is a wallet-signed Soroban transaction on a ~5-second ledger — so the roadmap invests in honesty, optimistic UI, and session-key delegation rather than impossible latency fixes.

**How to read each item:** What | Where (page/component) | Why (audit evidence with file:line citations, and/or the external pattern that motivates it) | Effort (S ≈ ≤2 days, M ≈ ≤2 weeks, L = multi-week or contract-coupled) | Impact (trust, activation, retention, pro-parity, a11y/reach).

**Coverage rule:** every critical and high audit finding appears in exactly one roadmap item below (criticals are cross-referenced in the coverage table after Phase C); anything we chose not to do is listed explicitly in "What we deliberately did NOT recommend."

---

### Phase A — Quick wins (each ≤1 week)

Phase A is a bucket of genuinely small fixes — copy, defaults, tokens, dead-panel removal, state-machine one-liners, single-component changes. The theme is **stop lying**: the pro audience Noether courts cross-checks every number against the chain (a consensus finding across the Hyperliquid, GMX, Drift/Jupiter, and CEX teardowns), and one caught fake number poisons trust in every honest one. Most of the product's worst trust failures are fixable at this size because the correct data or the correct in-repo pattern already exists a file away.

---

### Phase B — Near-term (2–6 weeks)

Phase B attacks the two systemic causes behind most findings: **client-side chain scans where the indexer should serve reads** (windows, N+1s, missing liquidations, fake tiers) and **the absence of a transaction-lifecycle layer** for a 5-second chain (silence, spinners, raw JSON). It also converts the three money surfaces (portfolio, /vault, /vaults) from dashboards into working tools, and repairs the landing's interaction model.

Phase C bundles everything that requires contract redeploys (grouped so testnet users see at most one migration), the session/delegation track that answers the product's #1 structural friction, and the trust apparatus real deposits require. Nothing here should slip past mainnet.

### Critical-finding coverage

| Critical finding | Roadmap item(s) |
|---|---|
| Liquidations invisible to the liquidated user | B1 (event parsing + toast); C2 (event enrichment) |
| Net Worth excludes cross-margin balance | A9 |
| Failed oracle read → $0 pricing / −100% PnL | A8 |
| /vault money actions silent | A6 |
| LP counterparty risk undisclosed | A7 |
| Vault NAV excludes deployed capital (V-1) | A20 (UI mitigation); C1 (contract fix) |
| APY never negative / inflatable | B17 |
| Leader mode shows/closes other vaults' positions | B18 (UI scoping); C1 (contract membership check) |
| Factory-wide total_usdc corrupts all manage-surface numbers | A20 (caveat/gate); C1 (root cause) |
| Leaderboard PnL excludes liquidation losses | B2; C2 (event pnl) |
| Mobile modal clips the submit button | A1 |

Every high finding is likewise mapped (A1–A33, B1–B30, C7–C8 absorb them all); the data-integrity sweep is absorbed by A13–A17, A24–A25, A27, A31, B2, B10–B11, B14, B17 and C2 — with one correction from the completeness review: ledger #49 (WalletProvider stomps the NOE balance to 0) had no home and is now assigned to A31 via the addenda below. Nothing critical or high is parked.

---

### Sequencing rationale

Phase A goes first because it is almost entirely the *honesty* class of fix — copy, tokens, defaults, and one-component state machines — and the pro-first audience punishes fake numbers harder than missing features: one caught fabrication (a green +0.0000% funding rate, a $1.000 NOE price, a −$0.00 fee) poisons every honest number on the page, which is why the fake-data findings outrank prettier work. Nothing in A depends on B or C, several A items are prerequisites for B (the FactoryError decoder before the leader-surface rebuild, honest dashes before real fee data), and together they stop the trust bleeding while the deeper work proceeds. Phase B then fixes the two systemic causes underneath most of the audit — client-side chain scans that should be indexer reads, and the missing transaction-lifecycle layer for a 5-second chain — because those two investments (B2/B5/B11 and B21) each retire a dozen findings at once and create the data plumbing C6 and C11 stand on. Phase C bundles all contract-coupled work into as few redeploys as possible (C1 and C2 ride together with the already-planned referral-hook refit, respecting the WASM size budget and giving testnet users one migration, not five), and adds what real money non-negotiably requires: per-vault accounting, session-key delegation as the honest answer to signing friction, pre-liquidation alerting, and the trust/status/legal surfaces that post-Drift diligence culture now treats as conversion pages. Throughout, the 5s ledger is treated as a fixed constant to choreograph — optimistic rows, deterministic progress, locked-oracle-price copy, session keys — never as a number to hide.

### What we deliberately did NOT recommend

- **A fake order book or depth visualization.** The venue is oracle-priced; the honest moves are renaming, an oracle-transparency module, or a price ladder (B10). Faking depth would be caught instantly by the target audience.
- **TWAP / iceberg close.** With zero price impact, a $100K position cap, and 3 pairs, there is nothing to smooth (GMX teardown conclusion).
- **Any-token collateral with backend auto-swap.** Jupiter's version works because they own the dominant aggregator; Noether has no in-house swap route, and USDC-only keeps vault accounting and liquidations legible.
- **A native mobile app.** Apple's guideline 3.1.5 blocks unlicensed exchange apps, and a two-person team should not split focus; the PWA + push path (C5) plus excellent mobile web (A1, B25) is the correct vehicle.
- **A separate "Lite" mode.** The founders chose pro-first with progressive disclosure; collapsed Advanced sections, tooltips, and the one-time risk interstitial serve newcomers inside one layout.
- **A Last/Mark/Index trigger-price selector.** One oracle price marks, triggers, and liquidates — the ambiguity class doesn't exist. Ship the static caption ("Triggers on the oracle price — the same price that marks and liquidates") and market the absence as a feature.
- **In-app confirmation modals stacked on wallet prompts.** The wallet signature is the confirmation; the pre-sign receipt in the panel is the review surface (B21, C10). Extra modals double-confirm a 5s action.
- **Faking sub-5s finality or millisecond latency claims.** Ledger close is physics. Optimistic UI with staged, honest progress (B21) and eventually session keys (C3) are the legitimate levers; Lighter-style pay-for-latency tiers would read as throttling at this scale.
- **Session keys via custodial workarounds.** Until real on-chain delegation or passkey smart accounts ship (C3), no browser-held key should be dressed up as "one-click" — key custody theater is worse than popups.
- **Hedge-mode UI.** Not worth the surface area at 3 pairs; a read-only "Position mode: One-way" settings row (C10) answers the muscle-memory search definitively.
- **Chart drag-to-modify orders in v1.** Modify = cancel + re-place = two signed txs (~10s) today; read-only chart lines ship first (B9), and drag returns only after C4 makes edits atomic, gated behind a confirm chip.
- **Four-way split TP/SL targets.** O(n) Vec order storage and the WASM budget make it expensive; one TP + one SL covers the 90% case (B8), with the cap stated rather than silently failing.
- **Swipe-to-trade gestures.** A 5-second, irreversible signed transaction should never fire from an accident-prone gesture.
- **Token or airdrop implications in points.** SCF-funded, no token announced; Season 0 (C11) carries an explicit non-promise, because ambiguity is the documented way points programs curdle.
- **Any brand change.** Every visual fix above is opacity, weight, spacing, or token discipline inside the existing gold-on-dark identity; the wordmark, slashed O, and math-glyph strips stay — demoted only to decorative-contrast roles where they currently compete with information.

## How to Validate — Metrics & User Testing

Research identifies problems; only measurement confirms fixes. Two lightweight instruments, sized for a two-person team:

### 1. Instrument the activation funnel

The one funnel that matters on testnet, each step an event (client-side analytics or a small table in the existing Turso DB — the indexer already sees the on-chain half):

```
land → connect wallet → faucet claim → trustline set → first order signed
     → first order confirmed → TP/SL attached → position closed → returns within 7 days
```

Track per step: conversion %, median time-to-next-step, and drop-off point. Three numbers to watch weekly:

- **Time-to-first-trade** (land → first confirmed order). The onboarding kit (roadmap items around the faucet checklist) should move this under ~5 minutes for a new wallet.
- **Signed-but-failed rate** (orders signed that end in error). Error-copy and pre-validation work (Phase A) should push this toward zero; every failure costs a 5-second round-trip plus trust.
- **Second-session rate within 7 days.** The retention proxy that referral/leaderboard/points work must move; on-chain reads make this computable from the indexer alone (distinct wallets with trades on 2+ days).

Also log every toast-level error with its decoded reason — the frequency ranking of decoded contract errors is a free, permanent UX backlog prioritizer.

### 2. Five-user guerrilla tests, twice

Run once before Phase B ships and once after (the before/after is the point). Five participants is enough to surface most severe issues; recruit 3 perp-experienced traders (Hyperliquid/Binance users — the pro bar) and 2 Stellar-native users who have never traded perps (the newcomer bar). 30 minutes each, screen-shared, thinking aloud, no help given. Tasks:

1. "Get yourself test funds and open a 3x long on BTC." (activation + faucet discoverability)
2. "Protect that position so you can't lose more than half your margin." (TP/SL discoverability + risk comprehension)
3. "How much would you pay in fees on a $1,000 trade, and when could you be liquidated?" (fee/liq transparency — they must find real numbers, not guess)
4. "Put 50 USDC to work earning yield, then tell me what could go wrong with it." (vault flow + whether counterparty risk actually landed)
5. "Close everything and show me your history." (close feedback + whether liquidations/closures are legible after the fact)

Score each task: completed unaided / completed with struggle / failed — plus one quote per task. A task where 2+ of 5 fail is automatically a high-severity finding.

### Phase gates

- **Phase A ships** → signed-but-failed rate drops; zero raw XDR/JSON in user-facing toasts; contrast spot-checks pass AA on the money path.
- **Phase B ships** → time-to-first-trade under ~5 min; task 2 and 5 above pass for 4/5 users; liquidated users can point to the event in their history.
- **Phase C (pre-mainnet)** → full funnel re-run on mainnet candidates, plus the roadmap's structural items verified against real-money stakes.

## Appendix — Sources

Every URL cited by the research agents, deduplicated and grouped by research topic (~400 sources). Link rot is expected over time; titles are kept verbatim so a dead link can be re-found.

### Data-dense dark trading UI craft: numeric typography, density modes, gold-on-dark contrast, red/green semantics, and motion/flash conventions — evidence-based patterns for Noether
- [AWS Cloudscape Design System — Content density (Comfortable/Compact)](https://cloudscape.design/foundation/visual-foundation/content-density/)
